“You can teach me in secret. In any case, the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art activates without leaving any outward sign, so no one else will ever know.”

It was a martial art that would never become known to outsiders.

I could sense Father’s hesitation. If he became serious, grew angry, and pressured me forcefully, in the end I would have to tell him. But Father did not do that. Father was Father.

In any case, from my position, it was a martial art I absolutely had to obtain. If I learned the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art, it would be like gaining an extra life.

“Please think it over and send me word.”

I bowed respectfully and was about to turn away when Father’s voice came to me.

“The Wind God Four Steps comes first.”

Amazingly, Father had decided to transmit the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art to me.

He was really going to teach me the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art?

Perhaps Father wanted me to become his successor. Or perhaps he thought I was bound to die while facing the Eight Demon Venerables anyway, so he did not care.

“Very well.”

“Why accept so readily? I could take only the Wind God Four Steps and not teach you.”

“At the very least, you are not someone who would do something shameful in front of his son.”

A sneer brushed Father’s lips. Using even the word shameful was the strongest move I could make. How could Father not know what I was thinking?

‘I’m sorry, Father. But I can’t just hand over the Wind God Four Steps for nothing, can I?’

Fortunately, Father’s reaction was not that bad. He was the sort who would value me securing a price like this more highly than foolishly offering up a martial formula for free.

“Good. Tell me the formula.”

“Yes.”

I taught Father the formula of the Wind God Four Steps.

Father quietly closed his eyes and pondered the formula.

When three sijin had passed, Father began to display the Wind God Four Steps.

I watched without blinking even once.

The Wind God Four Steps that Father displayed was the same as what I had shown, and yet different.

Father seemed to be saying this:

This is how I interpret this martial art.

I imprinted every single movement of the Wind God Four Steps Father displayed into my mind. It was not because I intended to imitate it exactly. It was because I knew that the level of one’s martial learning was determined by how one interpreted those minute differences—what parts were different from mine, and why.

After completing all the forms, Father quietly fell into thought.

I, too, sank into deep meditation as I compared the Wind God Four Steps Father had just displayed with the Wind God Four Steps I had displayed.

I had to learn.

Father’s attitude toward martial arts. The way the greatest thought, and the depth of his interpretation.

At last, Father awakened from his meditation.

“It truly is an excellent martial art.”

If the words “excellent martial art” came from Father, that was the highest praise.

“You can’t swallow it down because your conscience would bother you, can you?”

Father snorted, but this time there was satisfaction mingled in it.

Now I had created the greatest variable since my regression.

Could Hwa Mugi truly defeat Father, who had even learned the Wind God Four Steps?

Of course, I intended to beat Hwa Mugi to death myself, but giving Father the Wind God Four Steps like this was a kind of contingency plan.

Father kept his promise.

“From now on, I will transmit the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art. I will say it only once, so memorize it well.”

Before hearing the formula, I sat down cross-legged on the spot.

Because Father would truly say it only once, I did not intend to memorize it with my head, but by circulating the formula myself and engraving it into my body. This was not something anyone but an expert of my level should attempt.

Father began transmitting the formula of the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art.

“From the Mingmen point, move your inner qi gently through Shenshu, Zhishi, and Weishu. At this moment, the true qi must be as light as snow settling on blades of grass. Speed is important through Pishu, Ganshu, and Geshu. Walk, run, then raise the speed as if lightly soaring, and when you reach Shenzhu, Fengmen, and Quyuan, pour it out powerfully like a crashing waterfall……”

The formula was openly difficult from the very beginning. Its difficulty was truly at the extreme. Even as I moved my true qi according to the formula, my heart kept dropping in terror. I resented Father to the point that I could hardly believe he had not stopped me from circulating the formula directly, even though he knew it was this difficult.

Naturally, several crises came. I managed to get through the minor ones, but near the end, a major crisis struck.

In an instant, my true qi flowed backward, and my meridians rampaged as if they would burst. The taut meridians felt as though they might explode at any moment. If I had not strengthened my meridians with the Heavenly Meridian Strengthening Art before, I would certainly have suffered severe internal injuries.

‘So learning the Heavenly Meridian Strengthening Art was preparation for this very day. Then I will not die today!’

At the very edge of a cliff where qi deviation, internal injuries, and death awaited if I fell, I became the embodiment of positivity and fought back.

I also believed.

I was not fated to die while learning martial arts from Father.

Did my faith reach through?

The true qi that had been bucking like a wild horse settled down. My inner power flowed along the meridians it was meant to follow, and having overcome the crisis, I was able to complete the final formula of the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art.

“Hooooo.”

I brought the circulation to a close with a long breath and slowly opened my eyes.

Father was standing by the large window of the Heavenly Demon Hall, looking outside.

Had he known that such a great crisis had passed through me?

If I had fallen into qi deviation, would he truly have helped me?

Until I heard the words “from now on, you are the next Heavenly Demon” from Father’s mouth, I could be certain of nothing.

Whether he knew my feelings or not, Father looked out the window and spoke of the greatness of the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art.

“When your life hangs by a thread, the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art activates on its own. If, in the future, you achieve great completion in the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art, you will never die.”

The instant I heard those words, one question arose in my heart.

‘Then why did you die, Father?’

Chapter 23: They Were Living Just Fine.
---------------------------------------
“Clearly Father had attained the Great Completion, so why did the Heavenly Demon Guardian Spirit Art not activate? Could it have activated and he was still defeated? Or did he fight such a fierce battle that not even enough internal energy remained to activate the Heavenly Demon Guardian Spirit Art?”

The more I came to know of Father’s overwhelming martial prowess, the more questions I had about his death. Did Hwamugi truly defeat a father like this?

I wanted to ask Father.

What on earth happened that day?

But I could not ask. For it was something that had not yet happened.

And I would never know, going forward.

Because that event would never come to pass.

I smiled brightly and spoke to Father.

“A secret has formed between Father and me. That makes me happier than having learned the Heavenly Demon Guardian Spirit Art.”

Perhaps the word “secret” grated on him, for Father furrowed his brow. I quickly added and turned away.

“Even if you are annoyed, you cannot do anything to me. For now, I am still more familiar with the Wind God Steps.”

Using the Swift Steps, I dashed out of there in an instant.

It was precisely at the moment my figure reached the end of the red carpet.

Swish!

Along with a refreshing sound of wind, I felt something brush past my side.

In the next instant, I halted my Swift Steps.

Before I knew it, Father was blocking my way.

Thwack!

And he ruthlessly flicked my forehead with his finger. It was not a situation where I could dodge, and had I tried, Father’s movement was too fast to avoid.

“Ow!”

I clutched my forehead. It was no exaggeration. It hurt so much that stars flashed before my eyes.

“You were acting so cocky, I actually thought you were faster than me.”

In the next instant, Father vanished.

He disappeared from my sight using the Shadow Stealth Step, and immediately returned to Taesa’s side using the Swift Steps. Watching that movement, I felt once more that his interpretation of martial arts was certainly different from mine.

I could tell. That he had not come merely because he found me annoying and wanted to strike my forehead.

Father was showing me intentionally. That the Wind God Steps as I had interpreted them were something like this.

“The footwork you used to return was the Wind God Steps, but what on earth was that movement technique you flew in with at first?”

“It is the Heavenly Demon Flying Art (天魔飛行術).”

The Heavenly Demon Flying Art was Father’s exclusive movement technique.

“Is it faster than the Swift Steps?”

“Of course it is, for now. I cannot say what will happen once the Wind God Steps attain the Great Completion.”

“The day I attain the Great Completion, let us settle this.”

“I will reach the Great Completion first, so surely you already know the result.”

“That remains to be seen. I might reach the Great Completion faster. After all, a young mind is sharper, is it not?”

“You will bleed from that clever head of yours.”

Blood was trickling from my forehead where it had been flicked.

“Aaaah! I should have just asked you to teach me the Diamond Indestructible Divine Art (金剛不壞神功) instead.”

Bearing this glorious wound upon my forehead, I departed the Heavenly Demon Hall.

A wound that could not be bought even with money.

I felt that I had grown one step closer to Father once more.

Of course, when such thoughts arise, one must be careful.

Because in human relationships, mistakes are always committed the moment you let your guard down, believing you are close.

In any case, through this martial exchange, both Father and I had taken a step forward. The future in which Hwamugi arrives was predetermined, but the present was ever-changing.

* * *

“Who on earth is he? Which arrogant bastard laid his hands on the young master?”

Ian grew agitated upon seeing my bruised forehead. In that moment, she was a fiercely protective older sister whose little brother had come home beaten.

“If you knew? Would you avenge me?”

“Of course I must. Just say the word! Who is it? I shall go at once and—”

“Father hit me.”

“…….”

“Why are you not going?”

Suddenly composed, Ian took a step back.

“I am one who protects, not one who attacks.”

Watching her speak so decisively, I laughed. Joking and teasing like this—something I could not do in the past—was enjoyable. It was also enjoyable to see her eye-smile vanish as if her eyes were sinking into her flesh.

“Oh, and I became the Hwangcheongakju. Once the official appointment comes down, I shall be entering the Hwangcheongak.”

Ian’s expression became absurd, beyond mere surprise.

“You speak of the joyous occasion of becoming the Hwangcheongakju so casually?”

“What is so special about that?”

“Good heavens! Do you speak without knowing how authoritative the Hwangcheongak is? You are speaking of none other than the Hwangcheongakju! It is a place where even the Demon Lords are dragged off to be punished should they commit crimes!”

As if I did not know? It was precisely why Father was sending me there.

The news of me becoming the Hwangcheongakju would probably cause another great stir within the sect.

“Congratulations, truly, congratulations.”

“Thank you.”

“But why did the Sect Leader suddenly appoint the young master as the Hwangcheongakju?”

“Perhaps he felt sorry after hitting me.”

“Please, no jokes!”

“I told him. That the main sect must change. Into a place where one need not learn martial arts that ruin one’s body merely to protect someone.”

“!”

Ian flinched and asked with a trembling voice.

“W-what do you mean by that?”

“I will make it a sect where one does not have to live with an obese body for life due to the side effects of the Full Body Petrification Art.”

Ian’s face went blank. How long did she stand there in a daze?

“Surely… you knew?”

“Yes.”

Soon, she smirked and spoke nonchalantly.

“This is the path I chose.”

“You were merely forced to choose it when you were young.”

“No.”

Ian was resolute.

“I remember clearly. That day, you told me. That learning the Full Body Petrification Art carried such side effects. Therefore, you said the choice was mine. That is why I chose it.”

That choice could not have been just. Because one would have asked a young girl thus:

You must learn this martial art to protect the young master, but can you endure the side effects?

Therefore, the choice was coerced.

But I did not say that to her. Because I did not wish to diminish her pride that it had been her own choice.

“I shall fix that side effect. Not now, but later.”

Ian answered with a smile.

“You cannot fix it.”

“I can fix it. Believe me.”

“Yes, I shall believe you.”

She took my words as a joke. The one who had passed down the martial art had likely said so. That the side effects could never be removed.

Perhaps no longer wishing to discuss it, Ian changed the subject.

“But if the young master becomes the Hwangcheongakju, will the current Hwangcheongakju not object?”

“What can be done? It is an order from the Sect Leader.”

In truth, the current Hwangcheongakju was not much of a concern. Judging from the process of handling the previous Demon King incident, he had clearly either been recruited by the Demon King or was under duress. Since he would have had to step down anyway, that must be why Father appointed me. Sima Myeong would handle his resignation properly.

“Rather, I am worried about the martial artists handling the actual work. If the son of the Heavenly Demon drops in out of nowhere as the Pavilion Lord, would they not resent it?”

“The one who dropped in so suddenly is such a magnificent and wonderful person, so no one shall be displeased.”

“Thank you for saying so, at least.”

“After all, the evaluation of the one who pays the monthly salary is always predetermined.”

Smiling at her recurring joke, I brought up the matter I had been putting off.

“Since talk of wages has come up—Ian, there is something I need to settle this time. Listen carefully to what I am about to say.”

“Yes, please speak.”

“Bodyguard Ian, from this moment, I dismiss you from your position as my bodyguard.”

Ian’s large frame jumped with a start and landed with a thud. She was so surprised that I felt almost sorry.

“Wh-what do you mean, all of a sudden?”

“I am dismissing you from your position as bodyguard.”

“Are you serious?”

“Yes.”

She looked at me with an expression as if she had lost the entire world.

“Let me ask once more. Are you serious?”

Her voice shook pathetically, but I answered resolutely.

“I am.”

“May I ask the reason?”

“Because you refused to avenge me against Father.”

She did not laugh even at the joke.

Srrrng.

She drew her sword and pointed it at her own throat.

“I would rather die. I shall die as the young master’s bodyguard.”

Ian, once is enough to die.

I quickly spoke to her.

“I do not mean to part with you. You must remain as a martial artist under my command.”

“Oh my! You should have said so sooner. I nearly died! Please look at my neck; is there no wound?”

Only then did she let out a sigh of relief. But that did not mean all her worries had vanished.

“But being a bodyguard is my calling. I have never once considered doing anything else. What in the world are you planning to make me do?”

“Martial artist, Ian! I hereby bestow upon you a new position.”

“Yes!”

“I appoint you as the Commander of the Ghost Shadow Squad (鬼影隊).”

She was just as surprised as when she had heard she was dismissed as my bodyguard.

“The Ghost Shadow Squad? Was there such an organization in the main sect?”

“There is not.”

“You would have me command an organization that does not exist?”

“I shall create it henceforth. As my direct organization, you will receive your salary not from the sect, but from me.”

“Ghost Shadow Squad… does it mean the shadow of a ghost?”

“Yes. If I must ascribe a meaning, I shall become the ghost, and you all shall become my shadow.”

I had deliberately included the character for “shadow” (影) when naming it. Since she had lived her entire life as my shadow, it would be a name that felt that much closer to her.

“I like the name! But have you received the Sect Leader’s permission?”

“Not yet.”

Forming a private organization within the main sect was strictly forbidden.

“I shall certainly obtain permission.”

“The young master will accomplish it. The problem is me.”

“Why you?”

“What do you mean, why? First of all, I do not possess the skill to take charge of such a grand organization, do I?”

“Do not worry about that. I shall teach you martial arts befitting that position.”

“Even if I grow stronger… would I suit such a position? No one will follow me.”

They will follow.

They will all follow, having fallen for your character.

And someday, they shall learn that the commander they followed is the greatest beauty under heaven. Yes, this martial world shall witness the strongest organization in history led by the world’s greatest beauty.

“Ian, can you become the Commander of the Ghost Shadow Squad for me?”

Our gazes met and intertwined in the air.

Her confidence was at rock bottom, but because the words “for me” were included, her answer was predetermined.

“Yes, I shall accept the command.”

“Good. For now, I am the sole commander, but it shall become the strongest organization in the martial world. And from now on, discard your existing martial arts and unconditionally train in cultivating internal energy and building stamina. When the time comes, I shall teach you new martial arts.”

“Understood.”

She answered spiritedly, but Ian wore a dazed expression, not knowing whether to rejoice or worry.

“Then who shall protect the young master from now on?”

“Am I a child? I shall protect myself. A martial artist must protect their own body.”

In her two eyes, small and buried in flesh, there was nothing but worry.

“I do not know what I should say.”

“Simply say ‘Thank you.’ I shall pay you the highest salary among all the commanders of the main sect.”

“But do you not lack funds?”

“I shall earn them.”

“By doing what?”

“There are ways to earn money. If it truly comes to it, I shall sweep the courtyard of the Heavenly Demon Hall if necessary.”

“Young master, I do not need money. I have saved all that I have earned until now.”

“Money is always necessary. It is your worth, your very value. You receive it because you are worth it. So do not feel burdened.”

“……Yes.”

“And once you begin spending, it goes quickly. Save even more fiercely. Life is long. If you are unlucky, you will live to a hundred. Nay, you may achieve the Returning to Youth (返老還童) and have to live another hundred years.”

Ian laughed at my jesting words.

The me of the past would have worried about her.

Could she accept this change? Might a problem not arise?

But having lived a lifetime, I realized that all those worries had been in vain.

It turned out I did not need to worry. Everyone was smarter than I had thought, better at looking out for themselves, and lived just fine.

Chapter 24: What Will Happen Will Happen.
-----------------------------------------
There was still some time before I would officially take up the post of Pavilion Master of Huangcheon Pavilion. In the meantime, I decided to take care of one thing.

I intended to give the Ghost Shadow Unit warriors who would be joining me the very best treatment. Loyalty would not mean squeezing the youth out of them under some empty pretext; I would repay them with a monthly salary commensurate with their abilities.

Whether they squandered their money in the belief that life could end at any moment, or bought land and houses to prepare for old age, I did not care. As long as they did not gamble.

To do that, I needed an enormous amount of money. Fortunately, at this point in time, I knew of a way to make a fortune.

After leaving a letter for Ian saying that I was going out for some fresh air, I slipped out of the Cult in secret.

My life was one lived with regression in mind.

I had spent most of my time busy and exhausted, searching for the materials needed for the Grand Art. Even so, I had made sure to remember the things I needed to remember. I knew exactly when and where the various incidents that had occurred throughout decades in the martial world had taken place—especially those I could make use of.

This time, the incident would occur in a village near Wuyi Mountain in Fujian Province. An event I absolutely could not miss would take place there.

I ran all the way there using the Wind God’s Four Steps, both to train and to make the journey. I had only just reached the level of a toddler taking its first steps, but the more I ran, the more deeply I fell into its profound mysteries.

Whenever I rested, I gazed across the vast plains and sank into old memories. Whenever wind blowing in from the horizon sent clouds of sand and dust rolling toward me, I would leap to my feet and start running again.

To arrive on the day the incident occurred, I ran joyfully on some days and slowly on others.

Thus, after running and running, I arrived at the village beneath Wuyi Mountain.

There was a story I had once heard from an old wandering warrior during my own days as a vagabond.

He said that he had once run a freelance warrior office, earning commissions by arranging work. The incident that had occurred during that time—the one he always began with a sigh and the words, “It was a horrifying affair,” whenever he got drunk—would take place here today.

I asked a merchant and learned where the local freelance warrior office was.

At the end of the marketplace stood an old building where wandering warriors gathered.

When I stepped inside, more than a dozen wandering warriors were seated in the suffocatingly cramped space. One was dozing while leaning against the wall, while others tended their weapons or chatted in groups of two or three. Several gazes turned toward me, then drifted away when they found nothing particularly interesting.

I walked over to a middle-aged man seated at a desk in the corner, writing something.

The sight of him stirred my emotions. He was none other than Imchu, the man who had told me about today’s affair during my days as a wandering warrior.

“Is there any work?”

At my question, Imchu raised his head.

“I’ve never seen you before. Did someone recommend you?”

“Brother Seogwang told me to come here.”

“What is your relationship with Warrior Seo?”

“We worked together once.”

“Doing what?”

“I helped him with some business on Hengshan.”

“The Su Dongpo matter?”

“No. It was the Namak Sect.”

I could tell that he had deliberately asked the wrong question despite already knowing the answer. Since all sorts of people gathered here, Imchu was being cautious about verifying my identity. But I had already come prepared.

“If Warrior Seo recommended you, I can trust you. Can you work today?”

“Of course.”

“Fortuitous timing. There’s a major job today, and you came at just the right time. Go sit over there and wait. We need more people to gather.”

I went and sat where he had indicated. A few wandering warriors glanced at me, but I did not spare them so much as a look.

After about an hour, everyone needed for the mission had gathered. There were fifteen of us in total.

In my previous life, Imchu had said that fifty wandering warriors had been mobilized for this job. So much for the exaggerations of wandering warriors over drinks.

In any case, even fifteen men meant this was a major mission. Usually, jobs involving wandering warriors required as few as two or three people, and at most six or seven. Unless it was a truly urgent matter, no one employed so many people living such rough, rootless lives.

“Warrior Yang will be leading you today.”

The man I was introduced to, Liang Dang, was a veteran who had spent many years as a wandering warrior.

“The pay is high because this is a dangerous mission. Anyone who does anything stupid will die by my hand first, so keep that in mind.”

After warning everyone, Liang Dang approached me.

“I hear Warrior Seo recommended you?”

“That’s right.”

“Can I trust your skills?”

“Do I have to cut down someone to prove myself? You?”

I had to come on strong enough to look like a wandering warrior. Being too meek would only make me look suspicious.

“Hey, don’t get cocky. In this line of work, that head of yours can drop like morning dew before you know it.”

I sensed that he was trying to make an example of me and establish discipline among the wandering warriors, so I did not pick a fight.

“I’ll follow orders, so don’t worry. I’m only here for the money.”

“We’ll see.”

And so, the fifteen wandering warriors boarded the prepared horses and carriage and headed somewhere.

Only after several meetings to determine the final destination did we meet the woman and the boy.

Their faces were so alike that anyone could tell they were mother and son.

Only after meeting them did Liang Dang tell us about the mission.

“We have to escort these two safely to Nanping. We need to move as quickly as possible.”

The fact that fifteen wandering warriors had been mobilized for their escort meant that the woman and boy were people of considerable status. At the same time, it meant they were in serious danger.

The boy inside the carriage looked at me through the window. He was a cute, handsome child. I smiled and waved at him, and the boy gave me a little bow.

He seemed good-natured and well educated. The woman riding with him gave me a slight nod before closing the carriage curtain. Her face was filled with unease and worry.

The carriage carrying the two began to move. The wandering warriors rode alongside it, guarding it from the front and rear.

The carriage did not stop until the horses were exhausted and could run no farther.

While the horses rested, the people also took a break and ate a little. Liang Dang handed out the dried meat he had prepared to the wandering warriors. The woman and boy were given food prepared separately.

The wandering warriors had been paid twice their usual fee for this mission. Liang Dang, who led them, had probably received several times more. Perhaps for that reason, he was extremely tense about completing the mission safely.

“We don’t know when we’ll get to eat again, so fill your bellies while you can!”

After resting for about half an hour, the carriage set off again.

How long had we been traveling?

One of the wandering warriors tumbled from his galloping horse.

“Stop!”

The warriors halted and examined the fallen man. His face had turned black, and he was dead, foam spilling from his mouth.

“He’s dead.”

The warrior making the report looked at his companion and was startled. More precisely, they were both startled. The veins in their eyes had begun to bulge, and their faces had also turned black.

“Poison?”

The moment he uttered the word, they clutched their throats and fell backward.

“Ugh.”

The wandering warriors’ faces and bodies turned black as they collapsed one after another.

Only three people remained standing to the end: Liang Dang, who had been leading us, another wandering warrior, and me.

I could tell from the way those two looked at me.

*They poisoned us!*

Even I had not known this would happen. When Imchu told me about it in the past, he had said that the wandering warriors had been attacked from outside, and that not even their bodies had been found. Who would have guessed that the leader had poisoned the food?

The two of them had apparently disposed of all the bodies to conceal the fact that they had used poison.

“Why aren’t you dead?”

Liang Dang glared at me in astonishment.

“Because I didn’t eat the dried meat you gave me. How could I eat food given to me by someone I met for the first time today?”

Though many things had changed after my regression, some things remained unchanged. Just as the cook who had mixed Dispersing Qi Poison into the food was destined to die that day, the deaths of these wandering warriors were destined to happen as well.

Even if I tried to prevent unnecessary killing, some things could not be helped. What was meant to change would change, and what was meant to happen would happen.

The two men drew their swords at the same time.

“You would have been better off eating it and dying.”

At that exact moment, something unexpected happened.

*Shnk!*

Liang Dang’s sword pierced through the other wandering warrior’s back and came out through his chest.

“Urgh!”

The man collapsed, coughing blood.

I shook my head and said to Liang Dang,

“You idiot. You should have made him deal with me first, then killed him afterward. Do you even know how skilled I am?”

“That bastard was calculating and selfish. He would never have fought first. If I had to step forward and fight anyway… it was better to kill him while he was distracted by you.”

The two of them must have been evenly matched.

“What a pathetic life. You live by standing in line and stabbing the other man in the back. Did you think your turn wouldn’t come?”

“I won’t let anyone stand behind me, so stop worrying about it.”

“What are you planning to do with the money you earned by killing even your comrades?”

“There’s plenty I’ll do. Drink liquor, buy women.”

“You killed all these people for that? With all those ghosts clinging to you, will your thing even be able to stand under the weight?”

“You stupid bastard. Worry about your own life!”

Liang Dang had killed his companion because he had another source of confidence.

Four men appeared from that direction. Three of them wore masks, while only the one who seemed to be their leader had his face uncovered. From their eyes and the way they walked, I could tell they were all highly skilled.

“Have you arrived?”

Liang Dang greeted them respectfully. Judging by his tone and behavior, it seemed that these newcomers had hired him.

The unmasked man paid no attention to either Liang Dang or me. He looked toward the carriage and shouted,

“Come out already. Before I burn the whole carriage to ashes.”

At that, the woman and boy climbed down from the carriage, their faces pale with fear.

“You hid like rats so well that it was quite a chore finding you.”

The woman pleaded with the man.

“Warrior Gwon, please spare Yangi, at least.”

The woman seemed to know the man well. The man called Gwon appeared to ponder for a moment before saying,

“Very well. For the sake of our former ties, I’ll spare you.”

A moment of joy and hope passed over the woman’s face. Then Gwon revealed his vile nature.

“Did you really think that would happen? You damned bitch. You should be on your knees, begging and groveling, yet you still act like a mistress?”

Mocking her desperate maternal love, Gwon threw back his head and laughed.

The frightened boy clung to the hem of his mother’s skirt. The woman’s expression hardened as she was humiliated by a former subordinate. But, desperate to save her child somehow, she pleaded again.

“I’ll give you ten times what you’re being paid if you let Yangi live.”

“Money may be the greatest thing in the world, but it can’t be worth more than a life. Your father has already been subdued and captured as well. It’s all over.”

At the mention that her father had been captured, the woman let out a sorrowful sigh.

Then the man called Gwon turned his gaze toward me.

I was smiling and waving at the boy, who looked as though he might burst into tears at any moment, just as I had when we first met.

Thinking that the sight did not fit the situation, Gwon asked Liang Dang,

“What’s with that bastard? Wasn’t he one of your men?”

Everyone’s gaze focused on me. In particular, the eyes of the woman sheltering the boy were filled with desperate hope that this unexpected variable might change everything.

I slowly walked over and stood in front of the woman and boy.

“I’m the man who was paid to escort these two to their destination. And anyone I’m supposed to protect belongs behind my back.”

At that moment, the woman, who had been grasping at even the thinnest straw, found a rope to cling to—one twisted from Heavenly Silkworm Thread.

Chapter 25: You’ve Come to the Right Place.
-------------------------------------------
“What is he, I’m asking?”

Quan asked, glaring at Yang Dang.

“A wandering swordsman I haven’t disposed of yet.”

“You should have disposed of him! The original agreement was that you would only hand over the woman and the child, wasn’t it?”

“I’ll handle him.”

“Handle him now!”

Yang Dang drew his sword and approached me. He was confident he could beat me. I wondered just how much arrogance and delusion my youthful face would stir up from now on, how many more Yang Dangs it would summon.

“At a glance, you lot look like scoundrels who betrayed your master. Do you really think you’ll get paid properly? They’ll probably kill you to silence you and swallow the money they owe you too.”

At that, Quan’s eyes flared.

“What? Scoundrels who betrayed our master?”

“I only stopped there because there’s a child present.”

Quan laughed as if dumbfounded.

“This one’s a strange kind of madman.”

“You’re flustered because I hit the mark, aren’t you? You really were planning to kill him and pocket the money, weren’t you? Don’t. He killed all of that man’s companions to get this far.”

Unable to hide his thoughts, Quan’s face flushed red.

“You vermin of a wandering swordsman!”

Quan shouted to his men.

“Kill that bastard first!”

Three masked men stepped forward.

“See? He said that bastard first. Doesn’t that mean you’re next after me?”

At those words, Yang Dang could not hide his dismay.

“Even if you’re going to run, you should at least get paid before you go.”

Yang Dang, who had taken one step back, flinched in surprise.

“What nonsense is that? When did I run?”

The very moment he burst out in anger—

Swish!

A hidden weapon Quan had thrown in a surprise attack cut through the air. Unable to dodge the sudden projectile, Yang Dang toppled over where he stood.

“Idiot, handling things this badly.”

I looked at the dead Yang Dang and said,

“If you live like that, this is how it comes for you. Your turn too.”

This time, I spoke to Quan.

“You’re no different from him. You should have kept even one of them alive to face me. How could you kill him just because you lost your temper? If none of you can control your anger, how do you expect to live long?”

“How long are you going to listen to that bastard run his mouth?”

At Quan’s shout, the three masked men drew their swords and advanced.

Indeed, they were skilled enough that there had been no need to keep Yang Dang alive as a shield.

I looked at the woman and gestured for her to cover the child’s eyes. She held the child close and covered both his eyes and ears.

I did not drag out this fight.

Before they could attack, I launched myself forward first.

I unleashed the King of Hell Step from the Four Steps of the Wind God.

I had only just reached the stage of taking my first steps in it, but it turned my opponents into newborn infants.

They were given no chance to defend themselves or do anything at all. In a flash, I had closed in right before my opponent’s nose, and the moment he perceived me, his throat was cut.

I saw the eyes beneath the second man’s mask widen. If I could have read his thoughts, I imagine they would have been something like, “Huh! What—” before his throat was also slit.

The third masked man failed to respond properly even though two of his comrades had died.

Because, in truth, the time that had passed was still less than the blink of an eye.

Now that the Four Steps of the Wind God had been added to my original martial arts, this was an attack that men of their ability could not have blocked even if they had prepared for a hundred years. As before, I dug in with the King of Hell Step and cut down the third masked man with the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

Quan stared at me in horror. To his eyes, three lines of sword light must have appeared at once, and his subordinates must have seemed to collapse simultaneously.

“A swift sword art?”

“I didn’t even use a swift sword art, but I suppose that’s how it looked to your eyes just now.”

“Who—who are you?”

Quan’s voice trembled with terror.

Instead of answering, I asked the woman,

“Who is that man?”

“He is Quan Yuan, one of the retainers who guarded our house. I am Jin Silian, daughter of the Master of Golden Manor.”

“Golden Manor!”

I reacted as though startled, but in truth, I already knew who she was.

This woman’s father was Jin Ashu, a great merchant said to rank among the top ten in the Central Plains, the richest man in Fujian and the master of Golden Manor.

He was the reason I had come all this way.

The rebellion at Golden Manor.

That was the incident Im Chu had told me about. The incident in which the Master of Golden Manor was betrayed and killed by the retainer he trusted most.

The daughter of the Master of Golden Manor, who had escaped the villains’ clutches because her schedule had happened to change, had hired wandering swordsmen and tried to flee, but in the end, all of them lost their lives.

“I want to hire you, young hero. Please help us.”

“The situation has changed, so my price has gone up.”

“That’s fortunate. We can afford any price, no matter how high. Please kill that man and save my father.”

At that, Quan Yuan pleaded with me.

“Spare me and join our side. I’ll speak to the elder and have him pay you more than that woman will!”

“You’ll pay more than the daughter of the Master of Golden Manor? How?”

For a moment, Quan Yuan was rendered speechless.

I walked toward him and rebuked him coldly.

“You said you were a retainer, didn’t you? Then your place should have been in front of that woman and child.”

Quan Yuan desperately tried to avoid my sword, but the gap in ability between us was not something that could be filled by a frantic struggle to survive.

My sword split through Quan Yuan’s flesh and bone, then pierced his heart as well.

He fell with a single scream. Only after seeing him die did Jin Silian finally look relieved. Of course, she still had the child’s eyes and ears tightly covered.

I searched Quan Yuan and the masked men and took the daggers they carried to use as hidden weapons. The place we were headed from here would be swarming with enemies.

“Let’s go.”

“We have to take our Yang somewhere safe first.”

Climbing onto the driver’s seat, I said,

“Get on. Right now, the safest place is beside me.”

Jin Silian did not hesitate for long. She had seen my skill with her own eyes, and in any case, if not for me, she would already be dead.

Once she climbed aboard with the child, the carriage turned back the way it had come and began racing swiftly.

* * *

Lü Xiang.

The master of the Lü Family Manor, the guardian clan of Golden Manor, and the ringleader of today’s betrayal.

He had prepared for this undertaking for a full ten years. During that time, he had won over the key martial artists of Golden Manor, eliminated some by disguising their deaths as accidents, and sent far away those martial artists who could neither be bought nor removed.

He thought he had succeeded perfectly. Until I appeared again with Jin Silian, that is.

When Jin Silian and the child got down from the carriage, he smiled brightly. He had mistaken it for his men having captured the two alive.

“Wahahaha!”

From his perspective, if he had let this mother and child escape, he would never have slept soundly for the rest of his life.

“Where has Quan Yuan gone?”

He thought I was one of Quan Yuan’s subordinates.

“He’s dead. Everyone but me is dead.”

He must have felt something off in my tone, but he was more curious about his next question.

“By whom?”

“A young, handsome, mysterious expert.”

“What?”

Lü Xiang shouted, his expression full of incomprehension.

“You pathetic fool! What on earth are you thinking, praising an enemy like that?”

“Because it’s true.”

“Fine. Let’s say it is. Then how did you survive—”

Lü Xiang flinched, then grasped the situation.

“It was you!”

I answered with a faint smile.

Lü Xiang shouted and gathered his men. They came pouring in and surrounded me and the carriage.

“If you saved the woman and child, you should have fled far away. Why return to the tiger’s den?”

I looked around.

“Where is there a tiger here? All I see are packs of dogs that bit the master who fed them.”

Not only Lü Xiang, but also the men surrounding us began to emit killing intent. They were Lü Xiang’s arms and legs, and the martial artists of Golden Manor who had betrayed their master. Because they had guilty consciences, they hid them behind even colder anger.

Standing beside the carriage, Jin Silian held her son tightly. It was a situation that should have terrified her, but she showed no sign of fear. She knew well that composure belonged only to the strong, and as befitted the blood of a merchant, she was quick-witted. So that I could fight freely, she took her son and went into the carriage.

Lü Xiang, too, sensed that I was no ordinary man and became extremely cautious.

“Who are you, young hero? First, let us go inside and speak over a drink. I will serve you good wine.”

“No need for manners. I won’t go easy on you even if you pretend to be decent. I won’t go easy on you even if you beg. So curse and rant as much as you like.”

Realizing that persuasion would not work, Lü Xiang gave a cold order.

“Attack together and kill him!”

He emphasized the words “attack together.”

As soon as the order fell, they charged from every direction.

Swish! Swish! Swish! Swish! Swish!

With the refreshing sound of wind, daggers flew from my hand. By the time they thought to dodge or deflect them, the daggers had already severed even those thoughts and lodged in their vital points. The martial artists rushing in fell one after another and rolled across the ground.

The throwing knife art I had learned in my life before regression was the Life-Seizing Secret Art of Ximen Zhe, the Grandmaster of Flying Knives.

Before or since, I had never found a throwing knife art superior to it. It was a martial art worth carrying with me for a lifetime.

When I ran out of the daggers I had brought, I saw the survivors rejoice. It was a very brief joy born from the misconception that I was an expert in throwing knives.

I drew my sword and charged at them. Their abilities varied across the many stages between experts and middling warriors, but before the fully mastered Flying Heaven Sword Art, they could show no meaningful distinction.

After I swept through the courtyard once, all the enemies had fallen, and Lü Xiang’s face had gone pale.

“Who are you, great hero?”

The sword dripping with blood had turned a young hero into a great one.

“Great hero, my ass. I’m just a passing blade who smelled money.”

“How much will it take for you to simply withdraw?”

“Do you have a lot of money?”

“Enough to change one man’s life.”

“If you had that much money, why did you do something like this? If you wanted to change your life, you should have used your own money. Why touch someone else’s? And your own master’s money, at that.”

Unable to give a decent answer, Lü Xiang merely chewed at his lip.

Just as he was racking his brains, hesitating over what to do—

“Throw down your sword!”

From the building behind him, one of his men came out with a sword held to Jin Ashu’s throat.

It seemed that the subordinate intended to stage a hostage situation and force me to submit, but at the sight, Lü Xiang sighed and looked utterly dismayed.

He had been trying to hide Jin Ashu somehow and use him to negotiate with me, but a foolish subordinate had brought him outside. In any case, the matter had already happened, so Lü Xiang shouted fiercely and pressured me.

“Hurry and throw down your sword! Otherwise, the Master of Golden Manor dies!”

In that instant, a streak of sword qi tore through the air.

The fourth form of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, the Blazing Heaven Form.

It had already been a wild and tyrannical sword qi projection, but when it was unleashed through the Black Demon Sword and loaded with powerful internal energy, its momentum was enough to startle the heavens and overturn the earth.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwak!

The sword qi flew like a living whirlwind and swept through the martial artist.

He had been using Jin Ashu as a shield, hiding two-thirds of his body behind him, but the exposed one-third had vanished. Leaving only Jin Ashu standing, the dead body collapsed with a thud.

Perhaps he thought now was his chance, since I had poured out internal energy to launch the sword qi; Lü Xiang thrust his sword like a lightning bolt in a surprise attack.

Because I had been prepared, that attack in which he had put all his hopes naturally missed. With each successive attack, Lü Xiang’s openings continued to show.

Even if he had spent his entire life honing his craft, he would not have been a match for me. But when greed had filled the place where training should have been, there was no way he could be my opponent.

I could have subdued him at once, but I gave him a few more exchanges. If I suppressed the master of the Lü Family Manor too easily, their curiosity and fantasies about me would grow too large.

Lü Xiang’s sword flew in repeatedly, aiming for my vital points. Because his life was on the line, his attacks were desperate.

Those watching must have held their breath at the seemingly neck-and-neck duel, but my heart was as clear and still as a polished mirror.

After about thirty exchanges—

Thuk.

With the sound of flesh tearing, the fight ended.

Lü Xiang’s thrusting sword had stopped in midair. Reflected on its blade was the sight of Jin Ashu rejoicing.

My sword, which had crossed obliquely through Lü Xiang’s attack, was embedded in his heart.

Lü Xiang’s eyes as he looked at me were filled with resentment, but I spoke to him calmly.

“You were the head of the guardian clan, weren’t you? You, of all people, should not have done this. In your next life, if you want to earn money, become a merchant. If you want to kill people, become an assassin.”

Lü Xiang, already dead, could give no answer. When I pulled out my sword, he crumpled.

Jin Silian, who had been watching outside through the carriage window while holding her breath, let out a cheer and got down with her son.

“Father.”

“Grandfather!”

Jin Silian and the child ran to Jin Ashu and embraced him.

“Silian! Yang!”

Holding her father tightly, Jin Silian shed tears, and when his mother cried, the child cried after her.

A short while later, the imprisoned martial artists of Golden Manor were released.

Once the courtyard was put in order, Jin Ashu once again expressed his gratitude to me. Having heard the earlier story from Jin Silian, he was far more grateful that I had saved his daughter and grandson than that I had saved him.

“I will never forget this grace. Young hero, you are the benefactor who saved our clan from annihilation. Might you please tell me your honored name?”

Having dealt with Lü Xiang and his men single-handedly, I could imagine just how great his curiosity about me must be.

“I am nothing more than a wandering swordsman who happened to pass by.”

“Even if the King of Wandering Swordsmen had come, he would not have handled them so easily.”

“You flatter me.”

When I still did not reveal my identity, Jin Ashu asked no further.

Jin Silian told Jin Ashu about the promise she had made with me earlier.

“I promised our benefactor. I said the reward would be money.”

Jin Ashu praised his daughter with a satisfied expression.

“Well done. You made the surest promise possible.”

Now that he had regained his composure, he finally revealed the bearing of the richest man in Fujian.

“If what our benefactor needs is money, then today, you have come to precisely the right place.”

Chapter 26: The More You Take the Deeper the Karmic Bond Becomes.
-----------------------------------------------------------------
Geum Asu led the way through the secret passage beneath the Golden Manor.

The path was as complicated as a maze. I could sense lethal traps and mechanisms hidden throughout the walls, ceiling, and floor. The tension of Geum Sayeon, who followed behind with us, reached me as well.

After winding through a complex route that only one who knew the way could traverse, we arrived at the secret vault where the treasures of the Golden Manor were stored.

“You saved our lives, so it would only be right to give you my entire fortune, but realistically, that would be difficult.”

“I’m not expecting that much.”

“It would be fine to set a sum and repay you that way, but how about this instead? Take as much as you can carry out of this place in one go. However, you may not use a cart, nor may you receive help from anyone else.”

“You mean you’ll acknowledge whatever I bring out myself?”

“That’s right.”

“May I use my internal energy?”

“Of course.”

“Then I’ll be carrying out quite a lot.”

“If not for you, benefactor, we would all be dead. This much compensation is only proper.”

“Thank you.”

“I will give you one shichen starting now. Will that be enough?”

“More than enough.”

I slowly walked into the treasure vault.

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk entered the treasure vault, Geum Sayeon asked Geum Asu,

“Will you be all right?”

“What do you mean?”

“With our benefactor’s martial arts, he may take more than you expect. Won’t you regret it?”

Geum Sayeon said it with a joking smile, but inwardly she was worried that her father might later fall ill from sheer vexation. Her father was a man who had devoted his life to amassing wealth. And now, a large portion of that life might vanish in someone else’s hands.

But her father’s reaction was unexpected.

“Is there anything in there more precious than what we protected today?”

Geum Asu patted his daughter’s shoulder.

“It is enough that you and Yang are alive. If I had lost even you two…”

Geum Asu could not bring himself to continue. All his life, people had said that even if pricked with a needle, not a single drop of blood would come from him. But at least when it came to his children, he was a man who bled and wept in torrents. He had simply never had the chance to show it.

Rather, through this incident, Geum Asu had come to a painful realization.

His own flesh and blood were worth offering up all his wealth without regret, yet he had neglected that flesh and blood in order to gather wealth. The regret was overwhelming.

And there was another reason it would not be a waste, even if he gave away all his fortune.

There were many kinds of death. If he died while striving toward some purpose of his own, perhaps he would have no lingering regrets. But this time, it had been the betrayal of a retainer he trusted. Even in death, he likely would not have been able to close his eyes.

“That young hero is no ordinary man.”

“Yes. Despite his young age, his skill was extraordinary.”

“It wasn’t only his skill that I saw.”

“Then what else did you see?”

“I’ve been thinking since earlier about how to express it, but I still can’t find the words. That young hero has a presence that cannot be described.”

He had met countless people over the course of his life. Yet this was the first time he had seen someone so young possess such composure and such a unique air.

“Money can be earned again, but a connection with someone like that may not come even once in a lifetime.”

Moreover, the other party was young. Geum Asu hoped that their bond with that young man would continue into the next era of the Golden Manor, led by his daughter and grandson.

“So it does not matter if he takes everything. The more he takes, the deeper his tie to us will become.”

* * *

I can say this with certainty.

Never again in my life would I enter a treasure vault like this.

On one side, gold was piled up like a mountain, so dazzling that merely looking at it made my eyes ache. Buddhist statues and warrior statues, tableware and ornaments, and even all sorts of animals—tigers, toads, turtles, pigs. Truly, every conceivable thing made of gold was piled there. At first, they must have been neatly displayed, but later, as their number grew too great, it seemed they had simply stacked them into a mountain.

In the display cabinets beside it, rare jewels were arranged. There were ornaments such as rings, necklaces, and bracelets, as well as women’s trinkets carved from jade.

On the display stands on the opposite side, porcelain pieces were arranged, and behind them hung paintings by famous artists. The place was filled with all manner of rare treasures.

Beside those, there were things that stirred my excitement.

Night-shining pearls that illuminated the darkness, and Poison-Averting Pearls (避毒珠) that prevented poisoning when held in the mouth. The Poison-Averting Pearls in particular were of the highest grade, exceptionally small in size.

There were five of each. Since they were not things one could easily obtain even with money, they could be said to be more valuable than any jewel here.

With a delighted heart, I first took the night-shining pearls and Poison-Averting Pearls. I gathered them all and tucked them into my clothes.

A tremendous value had entered my possession, but this was only the beginning.

Next, I put on rings that could be sold for a high price, and layered bracelets and necklaces over myself. I wore so many that they hung heavily from me.

I looked for something to carry the gold in. A large leather sack would have been nice, but unfortunately, there was nothing suitable.

Should I take off my clothes and wrap them in those?

Just as that thought crossed my mind, I discovered bolts of fabric standing in a corner. Upon examining them, I saw that they were bolts of heavenly silkworm silk brought in from beyond the frontier.

With my sword, I tore off as large a piece as possible and made it into a huge bundle cloth, then swept the gold into it.

Since I could move it with internal energy, I rearranged the contents this way and that, packing in as much as I possibly could. I left the paintings and porcelain behind. They were at risk of being crumpled or broken. I also did not know their exact value.

I did not take the Golden Manor Lord’s circumstances into consideration. At least when it came to wealth, he was not someone I needed to worry about.

Thus I filled not only the entire shichen, but the bundle cloth as well, taking the treasures with me.

When they saw me emerge from the secret vault, Geum Asu and Geum Sayeon’s mouths fell open.

I was carrying a bundle far larger than my own body on my back. It was so huge that one could not even imagine how much was inside.

The vault doors were so large that I could simply come out. Otherwise, I would have had to break through the wall to leave.

“I’ll humbly take only this much.”

Thud.

When I set the bundle down, the floor shook with a heavy thud.

Seeing that every one of my fingers was covered with rings and that necklaces and bracelets were draped all over my neck and arms, Geum Asu burst into loud laughter.

“Uahahahaha!”

“Shouldn’t you be crying?”

“It is true that I am saddened by the loss of my fortune, but at least I am pleased to know that you are not a hypocrite, benefactor. The hypocrites I know would have taken only a few items, clinging to useless and petty appearances. But how bitter would they feel inside? I utterly detest such hypocrites. Greed is one of the most precious and important emotions that governs human beings.”

He was a true admirer of greed. It must have been thanks to that greed that he had risen to become the richest man in Fujian.

“Using heavenly silkworm silk to make a bundle cloth was also a truly wise decision.”

“Thank you.”

“It is a shame to part like this. Have at least a cup of wine before you go.”

“I’m not the sort to drink with treasure sitting beside me. I’ll see you next time.”

“If I ever need help, may I seek you out again, benefactor?”

“Of course. Please earn a great deal by then.”

At those words, Geum Asu laughed heartily. Once again, I could feel that the more honestly I behaved regarding wealth, the more favorable his impression of me became.

“Where should I go to meet you, benefactor?”

“If you leave a message under your name at any West Lake Inn (西湖客棧) throughout the Central Plains, I will come to see you.”

West Lake Inn was a secret contact station operated by our cult. It received necessary messages in this way and relayed them to important figures of the cult.

“As expected, benefactor, you are no ordinary person.”

Geum Asu would know. Only extraordinary people operated contact stations in such a manner.

“Take care until we meet again.”

“I wish you fortune in battle, benefactor.”

After forming a deep connection with the Golden Manor Lord, I left that place. As a connection with the Golden Manor Lord was by no means a loss for me, I was able to depart in good spirits.

* * *

I obtained a carriage in town, loaded it full of treasure, and headed toward the cult.

On the way back, I sold the gold and treasures in portions. It was too much for any one place to handle, so whenever I stopped in a city, I visited several shops and sold the jewels.

Having wandered the Central Plains my whole life, I knew well where things were and where they should be sold. Aside from the night-shining pearls and the Poison-Averting Pearls, I sold off all the gold and jewels.

I then divided the money I acquired among the most trustworthy money houses: the Central Plains Money House (中原錢莊), the Great Continent Money House (大陸錢莊), and the Wind and Cloud Money House (風雲錢莊). The money I secured amounted to an astonishing three million eight hundred thousand taels. It was an even greater sum than I had expected.

‘Thank you, Golden Manor Lord. You do not know it, but this money will become the cornerstone of an organization that will one day become a legend of the murim.’

I left three million five hundred thousand taels at the money houses and returned with only three hundred thousand taels in promissory notes.

As soon as I returned to the cult, I went to the Heavenly Demon Hall and met my father first. As always, Father was guarding the Heavenly Demon Hall like Mount Tai.

As I walked along the Path of Blood, the red carpet, a question suddenly occurred to me.

Here, Father must receive Sama Myeong’s reports several times a day and make important decisions. By those decisions, some people would live, while others would die.

Was Father truly satisfied with this life?

Just as I had been, and just as Ian had been, perhaps Father too had been pushed into the position of Heavenly Demon. Perhaps what he truly desired was hidden deep within his heart.

“I heard you left the cult.”

“I went to earn money.”

“Why money?”

“I can’t live off your money forever, Father. And I think I’ll need quite a lot of it from now on.”

“What do you intend to use it for?”

“I want to have a private organization of my own.”

“A private organization?”

Father’s expression sharpened slightly.

“Don’t the Demon Venerables each have subordinates of their own? If I’m to deal with them, I need an organization as well.”

“That cannot be allowed. If you have one, your elder brother will want one too. The blood kin and disciples of the Demon Venerables will want the same. Absolutely not!”

I had not thought I would receive permission all at once. Nor was it urgent right now.

Once one or two good subordinates gathered, and the water someday overflowed, then no one would be able to stop the dam from breaking.

I did not pester him, and Father did not mention it any further.

“Did you earn a lot of money?”

“Yes, quite a lot.”

I took a thick envelope from my clothes and held it out.

“This is for you, Father.”

When Father extended his hand, the envelope in my hand flew through the air to him. It was Void Seizing (虛空攝物), containing the profound principle of a higher realm—supremely gentle, yet swift.

Father checked inside the envelope. What it contained was a promissory note worth one hundred thousand taels.

“What money is this?”

“It’s your allowance, Father.”

“What?”

Father was startled. The reason he was so surprised was not that the sum was too great to receive as an allowance, but because of the word allowance itself.

“I have plenty of money.”

“I know. You are the richest person in the world. The money I just gave you is my first allowance to you, given with gratitude for giving birth to me and raising me.”

After staring down at the promissory note for a moment, Father abruptly asked,

“Are you trying to flatter me?”

“I will flatter you so much from now on that it may make your skin crawl, but not with this allowance. This was something I wanted to give you at least once. Then please rest, Father.”

I bowed respectfully and walked back down the Path of Blood.

Father’s gaze heated the back of my head until I left the hall. He must be very flustered right now.

After all, he had likely become the first Heavenly Demon in history to receive an allowance from his son.

Chapter 27: I Heard.
--------------------
A few days later, I formally ascended to the position of Pavilion Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

Early in the morning, martial artists from the Yellow Springs Pavilion arrived at my residence.

The man at their head was Special Investigator Seo Daeryong, who had investigated the Demon Army with me.

“We have come to escort the Pavilion Master.”

“Investigator Seo. So you came.”

“I volunteered.”

In truth, this small, gloomy-looking man before me had played a major role in my becoming Pavilion Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

“Let us go.”

I decided to take Ian with me.

“Ian, you come along too. You need to learn how an organization is run.”

“Thank you!”

As if she had thought I would leave her behind again, she was overjoyed enough to fly.

“We’re not going there to play. Go and learn. How an organization functions, and how you ought to treat your subordinates. Learn everything, without missing a single thing.”

“Yes!”

When we arrived at the Yellow Springs Pavilion, all the investigators had come out and were lined up at the entrance. It seemed this was how they welcomed a newly appointed Pavilion Master.

As I passed, they all cupped their fists in unison and greeted me in loud voices.

“We greet the Pavilion Master!”

I had expected them not to welcome my appointment. But their gazes were more favorable than I had imagined.

So I quietly asked Seo Daeryong, who was standing beside me.

“Did you tell them you’d cut their monthly pay if they didn’t pretend to welcome me?”

“No.”

“Then why is the atmosphere so good?”

At that, Seo Daeryong lifted his foot and mimed stomping down on the ground.

Seeing that, I understood the reason for their welcome. It was because of Go Dang, the First Unit Leader of the Demon Army. Go Dang had killed the investigator who first went to investigate him, and this time he had injured an investigator.

In that situation, I had smashed Go Dang’s head in and killed him, so to them, I had given them a satisfying revenge. On top of that, whatever resistance they might have felt toward my being far too young to sit in the Pavilion Master’s seat would have been somewhat offset by the Heavenly Demon’s bloodline, and since I had even killed the Demon Army Lord, my martial prowess had also been proven.

“Would you say a few words?”

At Seo Daeryong’s words, before entering the building, I turned toward my subordinates.

I slowly looked over each of their faces one by one, then delivered a brief statement.

“I don’t know whether my becoming Pavilion Master will make you happier or more miserable. But one thing is certain. Your hearts will beat faster than before.”

After saying only that, I turned and entered the building.

A murmur rose behind me. There was no need to worry about how they would take my words. They would find out soon enough.

When Seo Daeryong, who had guided me to the office, was about to leave, I kept him behind.

“Investigator Seo, you didn’t think we’d meet again like this, did you?”

“To be honest, I was very surprised.”

“I came because I missed you, Investigator Seo.”

As if empty flattery did not work on him, Seo Daeryong’s expression did not change at all.

“Ian, you must absolutely avoid men this blunt. They’re no fun for life.”

“Instead, he might be the real deal.”

Ian took Seo Daeryong’s side.

“No, this fellow is thin soup without a single scrap of substance.”

This time, Seo Daeryong stepped forward himself.

“I may not be rich broth, but I am not bland either.”

“Oh, you’ve got a temper. You’ll need that temper often from now on.”

Quick-witted Seo Daeryong immediately understood what I meant by those words.

“No. I am bland soup. Please don’t look for me when there is dangerous work.”

“I’m going to pour rice into that thin soup and eat it. With plenty of spicy seasoning mixed in.”

Seo Daeryong stepped backward. He was only pretending to be frightened for no reason; Seo Daeryong was a brave man. I knew that great courage and spirit dwelled in that small body.

“Now, tell me about this pavilion. I know nothing.”

As if he had been waiting for that, Seo Daeryong began explaining.

“As you know, the Yellow Springs Pavilion is the institution that safeguards and enforces the cult’s rules and laws. We root out corruption and handle various incidents that occur within the cult. The total personnel number one hundred: thirty investigators and seventy enforcement martial artists.”

The enforcement martial artists assisted and guarded the investigators, and were in charge of arresting criminals.

“There aren’t as many as I thought.”

“It is not an organization that needs large numbers.”

The Yellow Springs Pavilion was an authoritative organization everyone feared. Of course, there were exceptions like the Demon Army, but most demonic practitioners were afraid of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

“Of the thirty investigators, five are special investigators. Special investigators mainly take on cases considered difficult and important, but in ordinary times, you may regard them as the same as regular investigators. Enforcement martial artists are not assigned separately to investigators; they are dispatched as support whenever necessary.”

After listening to his explanation, I asked him.

“Investigator Seo, what do you think is the greatest problem with the Yellow Springs Pavilion?”

After hesitating for a moment, Seo Daeryong answered.

“No organization in the cult can escape the influence of the Eight Demon Venerables.”

He had not singled out the Yellow Springs Pavilion, but his meaning was that the Eight Demon Venerables were the greatest problem after all.

“Are you under that influence as well?”

“I am not. As I told you, I am a loner.”

At the very least, that meant he had not entered the cult’s politics for money or promotion.

“That does not mean I am a man of great conviction. You can simply think of me as a grumbling malcontent.”

“That’s why I like you. I dislike people who lean too much toward ideals.”

I glanced back at Ian as I spoke.

“I like reasonable people. People who take care of themselves first.”

Knowing that I was speaking about her, Ian merely smiled faintly.

My gaze turned back to Seo Daeryong.

“You said it before, didn’t you? That the cult would not change. What do you think? From now on, how about changing the cult with me?”

“What if you pour out all that passion and then leave? Just as suddenly as you came.”

“When the time comes for me to go, I’ll have to go.”

“And I, who burned with passion alongside you, will be left alone amid the ashes?”

“That’s none of my concern. You said you were a loner to begin with, didn’t you? So, will you do it?”

At my shamelessness, Seo Daeryong let out an exaggeratedly deep sigh, but his answer differed from his action.

“I will.”

“Your reason?”

“Did you not already show me that reason while handling the Demon Army matter?”

“I’ll be counting on you from now on.”

“Yes. Please leave it to me.”

In good spirits, I held out my hand to him, and Seo Daeryong clasped it firmly.

‘What will you do when I leave, you ask? What else? You’ll sit in my seat.’

In any case, I had taken office to a warmer welcome than expected. What I needed now was to make them clearly recognize who I was.

To do that, I needed an example. Someone very venomous and wicked.

“Among recent cases related to the Eight Demon Venerables, is there one I need to look into again?”

The words “look into again” were a roundabout way of saying “handled unjustly.”

Seo Daeryong understood my meaning at once.

“Of course… there is.”

The words “of course” once again pointed back to the problem of the Yellow Springs Pavilion that Seo Daeryong had spoken of earlier.

“Bring it to me.”

A short while later, Seo Daeryong brought the documents.

After checking the contents of the case, I let out a long sigh.

“You’re doing this on purpose to get me killed, aren’t you?”

The case Seo Daeryong had brought happened to be related to the Blood Heaven Saber Demon.

“I do not wish to do this either, but… it is a recent case, and it fits what you said.”

“So why does it have to be the Blood Heaven Saber Demon of all people!”

Ian, who had been watching, quickly cut in.

“Young Master, you really must restrain yourself this time. Elder Saber Demon must be on the verge of exploding.”

I handed the documents to Ian.

“The story written there is too unjust to bury for that reason. Ian, read it and bury it if you can.”

After reading the contents, Ian could not bring herself to close the documents and sighed.

“I can’t let this one go.”

I sprang up from my chair.

“Let’s go. If fate calls me, I must answer.”

In truth, this was nothing like fate. It was only an inevitability caused by an old man who had left trash lying around him.

* * *

Gwak Su was drunk.

He had fallen into a deep despair that even alcohol could not help.

His son, who attended a martial academy, had been beaten by a group of friends. His son had suffered a head injury and had not regained consciousness even after ten days.

The owner of the Pungryu Tavern, Jo Chunbae, who knew his circumstances well, sat down before him.

“Is it true that those bastards were released?”

Gwak Su drank one cup after another.

“Damn it! Goddamn it!”

An investigator from the Yellow Springs Pavilion had investigated the matter. Naturally, he had thought the bastards who caused the incident would be locked up in prison. But the three of them had been found innocent and released. It had been handled as an injury suffered while sparring with a friend.

“It’s all over. They say the father of the boy who led this is Baekdogwi.”

Baekdogwi was the leader of the Hundred Saber Ghosts.

“Good heavens!”

Jo Chunbae sighed. If the other boy’s father was a subordinate of the Blood Heaven Saber Demon, and not merely one of the Ten Saber Ghosts but Baekdogwi, then this matter was finished as it stood.

“The Yellow Springs Pavilion investigators are all the same. Those sons of bitches are even worse.”

“Shh! Lower your voice. What if someone hears?”

Jo Chunbae looked around and tried to calm him.

“Let them hear. Tell those Yellow Springs Pavilion bastards to kill me first! Tell Baekdogwi to come himself and kill me!”

Bang!

Gwak Su slammed the table. As the liquor bottle shook and nearly fell, Jo Chunbae quickly caught it.

“Please! Hold yourself back.”

“If my son doesn’t wake up, my wife won’t live out her natural span.”

Jo Chunbae knew those words were no exaggeration. Gwak Su had only one son, and he had watched for a long time just how dearly the couple had raised him.

“He will wake up.”

“If he doesn’t, I’ll kill that bastard and then take my own life.”

It was right then. A voice came from behind them.

“And can you kill him?”

When the two men turned their heads, three young men were standing at the tavern entrance.

“You?”

The one who had just asked in a voice full of mockery was the young man in the middle, and this young man was Yang Ho, who had led the assault.

“What gives you the right to talk about killing someone else’s precious son?”

Gwak Su sprang up from his seat.

“You! You!”

When he encountered Yang Ho in a situation he had never expected, he was shocked and flustered. But soon he shouted in fury.

“You bastard. Because of you, right now… Save my son! Save him, I said!”

His emotions were so intense that he did not even know what he was saying, but Yang Ho looked indifferent.

“You bastard! We haven’t heard a single word of apology from you!”

At that, Yang Ho stepped forward and bowed politely.

“I’m sorry. I truly committed a crime worthy of death. This will never happen again. Satisfied? I clearly apologized. So you should stop calling me this bastard and that bastard too, sir.”

“What? What did you just say?”

“I’m not your child, am I?”

The two bastards beside him snickered, and Yang Ho was also barely holding back his laughter.

Gwak Su, drunk and furious, could not restrain himself and tried to draw his sword, while Jo Chunbae rushed in and desperately stopped him.

“Hold back. Hold back! If only for your family, hold back!”

If Gwak Su drew his sword here, it was obvious he would be killed by Yang Ho. Gwak Su was a low-ranking martial artist of the Outer Hall, and though Yang Ho was young, he had properly learned martial arts from childhood.

“Mister, are you crazy? You told me to apologize, so I did, and now you’re trying to kill me.”

He could have simply left, but Yang Ho continued to provoke Gwak Su as if he had come here with that very intention.

“You really have no conscience. Go on, kill me. If you can kill me, try it.”

How could a human being be so shameless? Gwak Su wanted to stab him on the spot.

But he could not. He could not win, and even if he did kill Yang Ho, that would be an even bigger problem. The moment he did, everything would be over. Not only he, but his unconscious son and his grief-stricken wife would all be killed by Yang Ho’s father.

“Young men, you should leave at once. Quickly.”

Jo Chunbae tried to usher Yang Ho and his friends out, but Yang Ho acted like someone who had given up on being human.

“I wondered who Dong resembled to be such a coward, and it turns out he took after his father.”

At that moment, Gwak Su lost his reason.

Gwak Su drew his sword, and as if he had been waiting, Yang Ho’s fist flew out.

Struck in the face, Gwak Su crashed into a table behind him and fell over.

“That bastard clearly drew his sword first!”

The two standing behind Yang Ho shouted that they had seen it, as if they were witnesses.

Yang Ho rushed in and began beating Gwak Su.

Thud! Thud! Thud!

“A bastard who only guards a warehouse dared to report me to the Yellow Springs Pavilion?”

This was the reason he had not left and had picked a fight. Because he had been reported to the Yellow Springs Pavilion, his father had severely scolded him, and unable to overcome his anger, he had come to vent it.

Only after beating him to his heart’s content did Yang Ho rise from Gwak Su’s body.

“This is all your fault, mister. I told Dong to go home and steal some money, but he said he couldn’t. Said it was money his father earned through blood and sweat. That idiot, acting righteous all by himself. Am I the only piece of trash here? So how could I not beat him? This is all your responsibility. Got it?”

“…You confessed with your own mouth!”

Gwak Su’s lip was split, and blood flowed from the wound beneath his torn eye, but in that moment, he found a certain hope.

“What confession?”

“Everyone here heard it! Why you beat my son. You confessed that it wasn’t a spar, but an assault.”

Yang Ho looked around fiercely.

“Well then, is there anyone here who heard what I said?”

There were several customers there, but no one could step forward. Even Jo Chunbae, the tavern owner, could not step forward. The moment he did, he would not only have to close down his business here, but his life would be in danger. It was the same for the customers.

“They say no one heard me? Heeheehee.”

At Yang Ho’s laughter, Gwak Su finally shed the tears he had been holding back.

“Ugh, hngh!”

He was so furious he could not endure it. The reality in which the bastard who had left his son comatose could mock him and laugh while he could do nothing was too wretched. As a father, he was ashamed, and he was so sorry. He did not want to shed tears, but they kept flowing.

“A grown man is crying like an idiot. Like father, like son indeed. Hahaha.”

The bastards who had come with Yang Ho also giggled and laughed.

It was right then. From the second floor of the tavern, a young man’s voice rang out clearly.

“I heard it.”

Chapter 28: Hold On Tight, We're Setting Off.
---------------------------------------------
At the words that came from the second floor, Yang Ho shouted irritably.

“Who the hell is that bastard?”

Then the same voice was heard again.

“You haven’t even checked who I am, and you start with curses?”

This time, a woman’s voice rang out.

“That is not courage. It is recklessness.”

The woman’s voice was so pleasant to hear that one could understand why people spoke of jade beads rolling on a tray. It seemed as though even being cursed by this woman would feel good.

“What on earth is inside that head of his to make him turn out like that?”

“His upbringing must have been poor.”

“Even when parents neglect them, some children still grow up well. So you can’t blame the parents for everything.”

“You’re right.”

Ordinarily, Yang Ho would have flown into a mad rage, but his mind was elsewhere. Right now, his head was filled with lustful thoughts.

‘Judging by her voice alone, she must be a peerless beauty under heaven, no? If things go well today, I might spend a heated night with her!’

He was thinking of tearing apart the man with her and taking the woman for himself.

There was a reason he could indulge in such fearless fantasies. In Maga Village, the inns, taverns, and pleasure houses one frequented were largely decided by one’s status or skill.

This Pungryu Tavern, run by Jo Chunbae, was a place where low-ranking martial artists came. Not only his father, but even his father’s subordinates, the Saber Ghosts, would never set foot here.

And Maga Village was not the sort of place passing martial artists of the orthodox or unorthodox factions would visit either, so he naturally concluded that the other party must be a low-ranking martial artist of the Cult.

Meanwhile, the conversation on the second floor continued.

“To think that the mere son of the Hundred Saber Ghost would commit such tyranny in broad daylight. The authority of a Saber Ghost is truly impressive.”

“It must be possible because he has the might of the Blood Heaven Saber Demon behind him.”

Yang Ho found it strange. It was the first time anyone had dared look down on his father, and now they were even mentioning the Blood Heaven Saber Demon.

“I can imagine how he must have grown up from childhood.”

“He grew up never learning to fear the world.”

Yang Ho, who had been listening, spoke politely, unlike before.

“Esteemed senior who has graced us with your presence, please reveal yourself and point out the faults of this foolish junior.”

No sooner had he finished speaking than he added,
