“You think I’d say that, you cripple? I don’t know what kind of bastards are pretending to be mysterious masters, but if you don’t stick your heads out right now, you’re dead.”

At that, a man poked his head out from the second floor.

Seeing how young he looked, Yang Ho’s tension eased.

“Was it you?”

“That’s right. It was me.”

“And the bitch who was chattering with you?”

This time, the woman poked her head out. At the sight of her, Yang Ho screamed.

“Eek! What the hell?”

“Why are you so surprised, young hero?”

“It was really you? You?”

The owner of the voice was not an unparalleled beauty, but a plump woman. His happy fantasy shattered, Yang Ho shouted furiously.

“You trash, get down here right now if you don’t want to die!”

* * *

“Let’s go down now.”

At Ian’s words, I emptied the rest of my drink.

The person drinking on the second floor had been me. I had come to meet Gwak Su, the victim’s father, and unexpectedly ended up hooking a big catch.

I lightly leapt down from the second floor to the first. Ian jumped down after me. Though her body was plump, she landed lightly with her movement technique.

Seeing our movements, Yang Ho flinched in surprise. He had thought we were third-rate martial artists, but our movements were far too nimble.

I helped the fallen Gwak Su to his feet and seated him.

“I’m sorry I didn’t intervene sooner. That bastard looked like his mouth was about to run wild, so I waited a bit. Thanks to that, I got his confession.”

“Second Young Master?”

“Oh, you recognize me.”

“To tell the truth, I recognized your bodyguard. She is very famous within the Cult.”

I looked at Ian and pouted.

“Does that even make sense? You’re more famous than I am!”

“How is that because I’m famous? They recognize me right away because I’m so huge. Hmph!”

At our exchange, Yang Ho’s face turned pale.

“You are… the Second Young Master?”

“Relax. Today, I’m here as the Pavilion Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion. No, wait. That means you should be even more nervous.”

Yang Ho, who had been strutting about and shouting as if the whole world belonged to him, lowered his voice and spoke respectfully.

“I failed to recognize an honored person of our Cult. I am still young and have committed discourtesy, so please forgive me.”

“Judging by what you’ve done, you don’t seem young at all.”

“No. I am a fool who knows nothing.”

Yang Ho bowed his head deeply. For such a young bastard, he was frighteningly cunning and knew exactly when to lower his head.

“May I take my leave now?”

“Wait. I came because I have business with you.”

“What business might that be?”

“I came to investigate the assault case you committed.”

“Hwangcheon Pavilion already ruled me innocent, though?”

“This case is being completely reinvestigated.”

Unlike the startled Yang Ho, Gwak Su’s face brightened. Beside him, Jo Chunbae flinched; he had been about to shout hurrah but stopped himself.

I listed Yang Ho’s crimes one by one.

“You took money every day, so that’s habitual extortion. You forced him to steal money, so that’s incitement to theft. You beat your friend and drove him to the brink of death, so that’s attempted murder. As if that weren’t enough, you even came to the victim’s father and assaulted him. Your crimes are vile—far too vile. You’ll have to spend at least twenty years in the prison dungeon.”

“Twenty years?”

Forget twenty years; he would not be able to endure even two days. Yang Ho turned deathly white.

“No, that’s not it! It happened while we were just sparring!”

“Too late. You confessed it yourself in front of the Pavilion Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion.”

Driven into a corner, Yang Ho brought up the person who had served as a shield for all his evils until now.

“Do you know who my father is?”

“I do. The Hundred Saber Ghost.”

“If you falsely accuse me, my father will not stand by.”

“False accusation, my ass. Do you want me to show you what a false accusation really looks like?”

At my pressure, the bastard flinched and shrank back.

“And your father will be too busy to look after you. I intend to arrest him as well on charges of pressuring an investigator of Hwangcheon Pavilion.”

The teenage Yang Ho could not endure the pressure of the situation.

“My father won’t let you get away with this. He’ll come out of the prison dungeon and take revenge on you. No, he can kill you even from inside the dungeon! So you’d better think carefully—”

Thud!

Before he could finish speaking, my fist drove mercilessly into his stomach.

As Yang Ho clutched his belly and sank to the floor, another thunderbolt fell upon him.

“If I add the crime of threatening the Pavilion Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion, that makes thirty years.”

Yang Ho raised his head in terror.

“…No, please spare me!”

“I’m feeling generous. I’ll let that slide.”

“Please forgive me.”

“Fine. Give me just one reason I should forgive you. And stop talking about your father.”

“That…”

“Have you ever saved a person? Ever helped anyone? No? Then tell me anything even resembling a good deed. Make something up if you have to.”

“…”

“You can’t even make one up, can you? Since you’ve never done anything like that, it’s hard to invent something on the spot. Why should I forgive someone like you?”

“F-from now on, I’ll live properly!”

“Good. Do that after you pay for your crimes in the prison dungeon and come back out.”

Whoosh, smack!

I struck his cheek mercilessly. His jaw snapped to the side, and he collapsed unconscious.

“Transfer him to the Pavilion.”

I could have half-killed him here, but I did not. For him, true hell would be spending twenty years locked in the prison dungeon. It had not been an empty threat. I truly intended to sentence him to twenty years and throw him in.

The Cult’s prison dungeon was a hellish place where human rights were thoroughly disregarded. I guarantee this bastard would not last even a month before taking his own life.

‘Turning over a new leaf? If he were that sort of person, he would never have done such vicious things to begin with.’

The two bastards at the entrance began backing away, trying to flee.

But there were martial artists blocking the entrance. They were Seo Daeryong and the enforcement martial artists of Hwangcheon Pavilion. Only someone on the level of a Demon Lord would dare defy them; they were known to be more frightening than any other martial artists in the Cult.

The two collapsed where they stood, wetting themselves.

“We only did as we were told!”

“Please spare us!”

Punishment was punishment, but first, there was a role they had to play.

“If you don’t tell the truth exactly as it is, you’ll take all the blame.”

“We’ll tell you everything!”

“I’ll tell you!”

I spoke as if testifying would get them forgiven, but absolutely not. Once I received their testimony, these bastards would also be sent to the prison dungeon. Their sentences would be lighter than Yang Ho’s, who had led the evil deeds, but I intended to give them at least five years and throw them in.

If I made examples of them, would anyone at the martial academy or the learning academy dare bully their fellow students again?

“Take them to the interrogation room and get their statements!”

“Yes!”

The enforcement martial artists sealed the three men’s acupoints and transported them to the Cult.

One of the guests who had been watching could not hold back and began to clap.

Clap, clap, clap!

Then the rest of them cheered all at once. They shouted the hurrahs that Jo Chunbae had not been able to.

If it had not been me, such a reaction probably would not have come out. But was I not the son of the Heavenly Demon? It was a situation where they could cheer without fear.

I spoke loudly to them.

“If you suffer any injustice in the future, report it to Hwangcheon Pavilion. As long as I am the Pavilion Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion, no outside pressure will work.”

The applause grew even louder. If I did not fear even a Demon Venerable, what outside pressure could they possibly think would work?

I finished with a joke.

“If anyone threatens to bring his father like that bastard did earlier, I’ll bring my father too!”

Laughter burst out from all sides.

Just then, one of my subordinates came and relayed something to me. I immediately conveyed that happy news to Gwak Su.

“Before coming here, the Demonic Physician treated your son.”

“Truly? Someone as esteemed as him treated my son?”

“To me, your son is more precious. And I was just told that he has awakened, so hurry and go see him.”

“Uaaaaah!”

So shocked, Gwak Su screamed.

“Ah! Dong-ah, Dong-ah! You’re alive! My son is alive!”

The tavern owner, Jo Chunbae, embraced him tightly.

“That’s wonderful. Wonderful.”

People who knew the two of them gathered around and congratulated him.

Gwak Su shed tears of overwhelming emotion.

“I will never forget this grace.”

I stopped him as he tried to give me a deep bow.

“Have you not devoted your whole life to the Cult? Naturally, the Cult should protect you.”

It was because people like him had devoted themselves silently somewhere within the Cult for their entire lives that the Cult had been able to endure. In the Cult as it was now, such natural gratitude had disappeared.

“The Cult is grateful to you.”

I bowed my head to him. Feeling that my heart was sincere, Gwak Su shed more tears.

“Thank you. Truly, thank you.”

“You should hurry and go see your son.”

“Yes. I will be going now.”

He hurriedly ran off to see his son. The tavern owner, Jo Chunbae, said to me,

“Whenever you come, I will serve you wine and food free of charge.”

“Thank you. Next time I come, I’ll take a cup of your wine.”

“If you do, it will be my honor.”

Jo Chunbae bowed at the waist. All the guests with him also bowed. For them, this must have been the most satisfying thing they had ever witnessed.

After they too paid their respects and withdrew, Ian and Seo Daeryong, who had been standing behind me, walked over.

Seo Daeryong asked cautiously with a worried expression.

“But… will this really be all right?”

Since we would be arresting not only Yang Ho but also his father, the Hundred Saber Ghost, we would once again properly offend the Blood Heaven Saber Demon.

“You think it’ll be all right? There’ll be another uproar.”

“I’m worried.”

“You’re the one who brought me this case, aren’t you? To bury me under the Saber Demon’s great blade.”

“That is true, but…”

Seo Daeryong let out a sigh. His regret—perhaps he should not have brought up this case—was written plainly on his face.

To Seo Daeryong, who earnestly dreamed of change, and to Ian, who still feared change, I spoke with force.

“We haven’t even properly begun yet. So don’t let your hearts waver, and keep a firm grip on yourselves!”

Chapter 29: What We Should Fear.
--------------------------------
Naturally, I had expected the Blood Heaven Blade Demon or the Blade Ghosts to come first.

But the first backlash against my arrest of Yang Ho came from within.

Over thirty investigators of Hwangcheon Pavilion had gathered in the main hall, calling for me.

The one leading this affair was the most senior special investigator, Qu Ming.

“I ought to have visited your office in person, but there were too many of us, so I have committed this rudeness.”

“It looks less like you have something to say and more like you’ve come to pick a fight.”

“That is not the case at all. Please do not misunderstand.”

They had not come with a united heart. I could tell roughly just by looking at their eyes—who had come out of dissatisfaction, and who had moved along only reluctantly.

Qu Ming stepped forward to represent them all.

“I heard that you reinvestigated a case our pavilion had already concluded with a not-guilty verdict.”

“I did.”

“That is an act that undermines the morale and authority of this pavilion’s investigators.”

I asked the investigators standing behind Qu Ming.

“Do all of you think so as well?”

“Yes, we do.”

They answered in booming voices.

“My thoughts differ somewhat. Rather, I believe this Yang Ho incident is what undermined our pavilion’s authority. And quite severely at that.”

“What do you mean?”

“Must I really explain a matter of such common sense? How disappointing.”

At that, Qu Ming hardened his expression and retorted.

“The unreasonable act has already been committed by you, Pavilion Master.”

“When I arrested Yang Ho this time, did you hear that the bastard confessed to everything in front of everyone?”

“I heard.”

“So what? Why have you all swarmed here?”

“What we wish to say concerns the reinvestigation of an already concluded case.”

“A matter of principle?”

“Precisely. It is also unprecedented.”

“Investigator Qu. An innocent child was nearly beaten to death, and the guilty bastard was caught and made to confess. Is it not only right to reinvestigate? In this situation, how do you think you look, brandishing your principles?”

“Even if you call me stubborn, it cannot be helped. Principles are that important to an organization.”

Whether he was being obstinate or trying to pressure me, not once did he attempt to step back.

“Very well, then let us see what each of you thinks. Those who believe the reinvestigation is right, over here!”

Since they had come to act as a group, I doubted anyone would move now, but…

one person walked over to this side.

It was Seo Daeryong, my absolute supporter. Since everyone knew he was close to me, the commotion it caused was negligible.

“My loyal subject and the investigator who can match a hundred men, Investigator Seo.”

“If you put it that way, it makes it sound as though I moved out of personal friendship.”

“Was it not?”

“It was not. Setting aside my loyalty to you, Pavilion Master, I believe this case must be reinvestigated.”

“Your reason?”

“The investigation was inadequate.”

Several investigators, including Qu Ming, openly hardened their expressions, but Seo Daeryong calmly stated his opinion.

“We also cannot rule out the possibility of external pressure.”

“Good. Who agrees with this opinion?”

Once again, I raised my hand high and looked at the investigators.

In a situation where I wondered if there could be anyone, one person stepped forward.

“I think so as well.”

He was the martial artist who had been injured by a high-ranking official during the Demon Lord investigation. I had not only avenged him but also asked a Demonic Physician to treat him with the best care.

“I am Yi Hu. I was unable to greet you properly then. I was truly grateful.”

“How is your injury?”

“I am fine.”

“That is a relief.”

No other investigators moved besides Yi Hu. They all seemed to be watching Qu Ming’s mood. Indeed, while they had known me for only a few days, they had worked alongside Qu Ming for anywhere from a few years to over a decade.

Then one more person moved.

“I am Liang Jun. I entered alongside Yi Hu. I sincerely thank you for avenging him.”

Emboldened by Liang Jun stepping forward, several others worked up the courage to move to my side. They had not moved because of personal ties. They were people who themselves believed this investigation had been mishandled.

But the number still standing on Qu Ming’s side remained overwhelming.

I called out one among them. He was the very man at the center of today’s matter.

“Investigator Cong Hua!”

“Yes.”

Cong Hua was precisely the investigator in charge of this case. The very one who had declared the son of the White Blade Ghost not guilty.

“What do you think?”

“…I conducted the investigation properly.”

I approached Cong Hua and stared hard into his face as I asked.

“Did you take a bribe from a Blade Ghost?”

Cong Hua’s expression stiffened instantly. The one who grew angry on his behalf was Qu Ming.

“What manner of reckless accusation is that?”

I revealed a cold look and asked Qu Ming,

“Then did you take one?”

“I did not.”

“Then why do you step forward? Going so far as to call your superior’s words reckless?”

“Because he is my subordinate.”

“He is my subordinate as well. Is he not?”

In that instant, Qu Ming realized he had misspoken and bowed his head.

“You are correct. I apologize.”

“Do not step forward without permission.”

“Yes.”

After silencing him, I spoke to Cong Hua again.

“Did you take money from the White Blade Ghost?”

“No.”

“Do you swear?”

“I swear on my life.”

He did not avert his gaze, as though he were innocent.

“If you cannot believe me, investigate me.”

“I already have. You truly did not take any money.”

Everyone was startled by my words. To say I had already investigated meant I had completed an internal investigation.

Though I had told him not to step forward, Qu Ming could not restrain himself.

“Our investigators cannot conduct internal investigations unless it is a special investigation.”

“I conducted it as a special investigation. I even received a direct order from the Sect Leader and carried it out as an external investigation.”

I coldly asked the shaken Qu Ming,

“When the Sect Leader appointed me as the Pavilion Master here, did you think he would not even back me to this extent?”

“I did not.”

I looked around at everyone and spoke calmly.

“I was curious. If you did not take money, why did you cover up the case like this?”

“I did not cover up the case. According to my investigation…”

I cut off Cong Hua’s words.

“Because he threatened to kill your younger sister?”

In that instant, Cong Hua froze. His expression seemed to ask, “How did you know that?”

“My investigation of you showed that you are a remarkably incorruptible person. I did not believe it. I ordered them to dig deeper. There is no one in this world who hates money. Yet, surprisingly, you truly were an upright man. Then why would such an upright person roughly hush up and gloss over a case? You had one great weakness. Your younger sister, your only family.”

Cong Hua’s pupils trembled.

The surprised Qu Ming asked him,

“Were you truly threatened by him?”

Cong Hua could not answer.

“You fool, speak! Were you threatened or not!”

Qu Ming pressed him, but Cong Hua kept his head lowered and said nothing. I also knew why Qu Ming had taken the lead in this matter. Cong Hua was the junior whom Qu Ming cherished most. He had stepped forward to ensure Cong Hua would not suffer harm from this incident.

I spoke to Cong Hua.

“If you are worried about your younger sister, don’t be. I have already sent enforcers to guard her at the An estate. Until this case is fully resolved, your sister will be safe.”

At those words, relief finally showed on Cong Hua’s face. Soon, as if acknowledging everything, he knelt before me.

“I have committed a capital crime.”

Qu Ming, witnessing the sight, let out a sigh.

“Ah…”

Having worked together for so long, he must have known better than anyone how dearly Cong Hua cherished his younger sister. But it seemed he had not known about this.

I spoke to Cong Hua, who had his head deeply bowed.

“You know that even under threat, you should not have done this?”

“Yes, I will accept my punishment willingly.”

The expressions of the watching investigators varied, but all were angry. This was not someone else’s problem. It was something that could befall them at any time.

“Rise.”

“Yes.”

Cong Hua rose from his place.

“If this had happened after I became Pavilion Master, I would not have forgiven you. But since this occurred before my arrival, I will forgive you as a special exception.”

“Do you truly forgive me?”

“It is not I who forgive you, but your past life of integrity that forgives you. Now, that past life of integrity has vanished because of this incident. Build it up again from the beginning.”

Cong Hua was deeply moved, for he had not expected to be forgiven.

“Thank you. Truly, thank you.”

But what truly moved him were the next words.

“Now that I am Pavilion Master, your families are as my own. Any bastard who touches your families, I will destroy them all. Why? Do you not believe me? I even beheaded a Demon Lord. Do you think I would fear some Blade Ghost?”

I could feel the gazes turning toward me growing hotter.

“From now on, this pavilion will not permit any external pressure. From this moment, taking even a single penny will be treated as a serious crime. Be prepared to be imprisoned in the same dungeon as the criminals you have incarcerated. If you cannot abide by this, leave the pavilion.”

Diverse reactions emerged. Joy and excitement, antipathy, disbelief… all flickering across their faces.

“In return, I will not leave untouched those who touch you, as happened this time. If you are threatened, tell me, and I will surely protect your families. Even if they have already been kidnapped, I will rescue them without fail. You may believe in me. If you do not find me trustworthy, trust the Sect Leader. Even if I must beg the Father to save them, I will rescue them without fail. Therefore, if external pressure comes, you must absolutely report it to me.”

I was speaking from the heart. Only when I and my family are safe can Hwangcheon Pavilion function properly.

“I am someone who believes that whoever commits an evil act must be punished. Even if someone from the Heavenly Demon Cult commits a wrong, I will not overlook it. If the Sect Leader commits a crime, I will arrest even the Sect Leader!”

In that instant, everyone held their breath. Words of arresting the Heavenly Demon were words for which one could be killed by the Heavenly Demon on the spot without it being the least bit strange.

“What I fear is not the Sect Leader, nor the Palmajon. What I fear is us becoming crippled. Failing to protect those we must protect, and being unable to stop those who deserve punishment from smirking and slipping away. When I caught that bastard yesterday, the people at the inn were so overjoyed they were all clapping. I am a man who loves the spotlight, so I will continue to receive applause in the future.”

As my sincerity was conveyed, the investigators’ expressions changed. Especially Cong Hua, who had been threatened—his eyes reddened, not knowing what to do.

Then an investigator who had been standing behind Qu Ming walked toward me and spoke. She was Jo Hyang, the woman Seo Daeryong liked.

“I, too, want to receive applause together with you, Pavilion Master.”

That was the beginning. One by one, the investigators walked to my side. Some were sincere, while others moved reluctantly, swept up by the mood. It did not matter. What was important now was that all of them were moving in the same direction.

The only ones left were Qu Ming.

“Do you dislike the new principles I have established?”

“I do not.”

“Then why do you stand there?”

“…”

“Is it because your pride is wounded?”

“No. I am uncertain whether your words are sincere, Pavilion Master.”

Not at all. His pride was wounded at this very moment. He had come with good intentions for his junior, but the justification and momentum he had arrived with had both vanished, and he had suffered humiliation by stepping forward. That was why those few steps were so difficult for him.

I had to soothe that wounded pride. That was a leader’s role. This was a time when unity was needed more than division, and though he was stubborn, he was not a bad man. In fact, when I investigated Cong Hua, I had investigated him thoroughly as well.

“Everything I said before is true. To make my sincerity into reality, your experience and help are absolutely necessary.”

I extended my hand to Qu Ming first.

“Help me.”

“Thank you, Pavilion Master.”

Qu Ming took my hand. His hand was trembling. This matter held value beyond simply comforting Qu Ming. It was because of the investigators watching this scene.

I immediately gave an order to Seo Daeryong.

“Investigator Seo.”

“Yes.”

“Go immediately with the enforcers and arrest Yang Ho’s father, the White Blade Ghost Liang Tai. The charge is special intimidation.”

Then Qu Ming spoke cautiously.

“He will not submit to arrest willingly.”

I said in a loud voice for all to hear.

“Tell him to go right ahead and resist, because if he does, I will wipe out all the Blade Ghosts.”

They must have been wondering what I was relying on to act this way, but one thing was certain.

The Hwangcheon Pavilion investigators standing before me had never once, since the pavilion’s founding, heard anyone say they would wipe out all the Blade Ghosts. They were excited. Their hearts must already have begun to race. I could feel that heat.

I could be certain because of this passion. Today was the day Hwangcheon Pavilion’s morale was at its highest since its founding.

Chapter 30: Why the Sword Weeps.
--------------------------------
Seo Daeryong, who had gone out with the enforcement martial artists, returned empty-handed.

“Yang Tae refused arrest. He claimed that within Namdojong territory, the Blade Ghosts have the right to protect themselves. We tried to drag him in by force, but his subordinates stepped forward, so we had no choice but to withdraw.”

As a Hundred Blade Ghost, he had a full hundred direct subordinates. If he refused, arresting him would not be easy.

The investigators, their morale soaring, grew agitated.

“He’s looking down on us.”

“We should take every enforcement martial artist we have and arrest him by force.”

Gok Myeong calmed the young investigators’ excitement and argued for caution.

“You must handle this carefully. If a clash breaks out with the Blade Ghosts, many of the enforcement martial artists will be injured.”

“Are we supposed to just let this pass, then?”

While the investigators went back and forth in heated debate, I held out a single document to them.

“Here. Send this to him.”

“Gasp!”

What I showed them was a summons, and the reason everyone was shocked was because of the charge I had written on it.

Treason.

“Does refusing arrest warrant a charge of treason?”

Even Gok Myeong, a veteran, did not know this.

“It does. We hold positions personally appointed by the Cult Leader, and a Hundred Blade Ghost is a position appointed by the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon. Our orders and the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon’s orders cannot conflict. In the end, he has defied the Cult Leader’s command. There’s no need to go in person. Send the summons by messenger, and he’ll come running on his own two feet.”

He would come. If he made one wrong move and the sparks of treason spread to all the Blade Ghosts, he would be killed by their own hands.

“Yes, understood.”

Seo Daeryong took the summons and immediately ran out.

Sure enough, less than half a shichen after the summons was delivered, Yang Tae appeared at Hwangcheon Pavilion in person.

The word “treason” was a magic word that made even the foulest-tempered opponent obedient.

But though I could make him come this far, I could not make him confess.

Even after a shichen had passed since the investigators began questioning him, he refused to admit that he had threatened to kill Jonghwa’s younger sister.

It was as expected, so I entered the interrogation room myself.

Yang Tae was sitting in a chair with his internal energy suppressed, and when I entered, he flinched in surprise. No matter how imposing a Hundred Blade Ghost might be, he could not compare to my current momentum after cutting down the Demon Army Lord.

“Second Young Master.”

“You should call me Pavilion Master of Hwangcheon Pavilion, not Second Young Master.”

“I was discourteous. Pavilion Master. The summons said treason. What on earth does that mean?”

“I thought this was the only way I’d get to see you, Martial Artist Yang.”

When I spoke gently with a smile, Yang Tae responded just as smoothly.

“I apologize for refusing arrest. As you know, when one belongs to an organization, there are many situations where one must be mindful of appearances. I have my dignity to maintain in front of my subordinates.”

“I understand.”

“This was never something that should have grown so serious in the first place. It was all because of some childish prank.”

“There is some truth to that.”

When I readily agreed, curious to see how he would proceed, Yang Tae seemed to think he had found an opening and began trying to win me over.

“I knew you would be someone I could talk to. We should be able to resolve this amicably.”

“How?”

“Pavilion Master, this is your first time taking on such an important post, is it not? As you manage Hwangcheon Pavilion from now on, you will need a great deal of outside help. It will cost a lot of money as well.”

He was speaking indirectly, but in the end, he meant he would give me a bribe.

“And that is not all. Later, if you are to become the official successor, help from our side will be absolutely necessary. After all, at present, everyone is lining up behind the First Young Master…”

“If you will help me, I would be grateful. Please help me greatly.”

At that, Yang Tae’s expression brightened at once.

“As expected, you are a broad-minded man. I will serve you well from now on. And soon, I will arrange a good gathering.”

Watching my reaction, he quietly began to rise from his seat.

“Where are you going?”

“Was our discussion not over?”

“Didn’t you mean that you would help me after you came out of the prison?”

At the word “prison,” Yang Tae’s expression stiffened.

“What a disheartening joke to make.”

I looked up at him and revealed a cold aura.

“Does this sound like a joke? We are discussing the crime of threatening to kill the family of an investigator of Hwangcheon Pavilion.”

“I clearly told you that I never made such a threat.”

“Your testimony is irrelevant. The testimony of the investigator who was threatened has already been accepted.”

“What do you mean by that?”

“Our cult has such a rule. The testimony of Hwangcheon Pavilion martial artists takes precedence over any contradictory testimony. It is a clause created to uphold the authority of Hwangcheon Pavilion investigators, who enforce the cult’s laws. You probably didn’t know. I only learned this time that such a law existed myself. This is all because of people like you. There are more than a few who think that if they simply insist, everything will go their way.”

Flustered, Yang Tae could not say anything. When he first came here, he surely had never imagined that things would turn out like this.

And then came another bolt from the blue.

“If you confess obediently, I will reduce your sentence to ten years. If you deny it, it will be twenty. There will be no further negotiation.”

“Wh-what? Ten years?”

Yang Tae was so shocked that he even stuttered.

“Choose.”

“Are you insane?”

Yang Tae’s true nature, which he had been holding back, came out, and his words turned rough.

“If I must be insane, then gladly. It does seem difficult to deal with people like you while in my right mind. Now, I’ll give you half a quarter-hour to think.”

In the end, Yang Tae’s composure shattered. He spoke with eyes blazing.

“Don’t make me laugh. Forget ten years. I have no intention of spending even a single day in that prison.”

There was no longer any value in talking to him, so I rose from my seat.

“The trial will be held within a few days.”

“On whose authority?”

“Mine. You live as you please too, don’t you?”

His urgent words caught me as I turned away.

“Wait! Second Young Master! Pavilion Master! Why on earth are you doing this?”

“Are you asking because you don’t know? Your son assaulted his friend and nearly killed him. It wasn’t simply an injury from a single fight; he tormented him every day for years. And yet, as his parent, you threatened to kill an investigator’s family to bury the incident. And you ask why?”

“Are we the same as some warehouse guard? Should we not at least be treated as superiors? Until now, things like this were always overlooked, weren’t they?”

It was a sorrowful thing to say, and at the same time a terrifying thing to say. It was also the kind of statement that became the driving force pushing me forward.

“Not anymore.”

“If you touch me, the Demon Venerable will not stand by.”

“He will stand by. He stood by even when I cut down the Demon Army Lord, so what are you?”

At last, Yang Tae exploded. He had reached the wrong conclusion that this was happening to him because he had been too polite.

Bang!

Yang Tae slammed the desk and spoke through clenched teeth.

“Listen here, Pavilion Master of Hwangcheon Pavilion! I am a man with many friends who would share life and death with me!”

“I guarantee you, no one will step forward for you. Who would sincerely like someone like you enough to risk their life?”

To him, trembling with rage, I threw out one last remark.

“Why? Why don’t you try saying you’ll kill my family too?”

Yang Tae was worked up, but he did not dare say anything.

Watching him grit his teeth and swallow his anger, a thought suddenly occurred to me.

Perhaps our collapse was not because of Hwa Mugi.

Discipline had fallen so low that a mere Hundred Blade Ghost dared threaten an investigator of Hwangcheon Pavilion, and even went so far as to subtly threaten me.

“Confess obediently.”

I spoke calmly and left the room. As the door closed, all I could hear was the sound of an innocent desk being struck.

* * *

That very day, the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon came to see me.

In the front yard of my residence, he had planted his great saber into the ground and was sitting before it, drinking.

“Welcome, Second Young Master. No, should I now call you Pavilion Master of Hwangcheon Pavilion?”

“Call me whatever is comfortable for you.”

“I am more comfortable with Second Young Master, so I will call you that.”

“As you wish.”

“Come here and have a drink.”

I obediently sat down across from him. Though he had invited me to drink with him, he did not pour me any liquor.

“I believe I warned you. Nothing good will come of seeing me often.”

“You are always the one who comes to find me, Elder.”

After staring at me for a moment, the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon abruptly brought up the past.

“There was a moment recently when I saw you in a new light. When you won the spar and made a wish to the Cult Leader, I shouted inwardly, Yes, that’s it. If he’s a smart one, that’s the kind of wish he should make.”

“Even in my own opinion, it was quite a good wish.”

“Yes, that was when it began.”

The Hyeolcheon Blade Demon emptied his cup and filled it again. He still did not hand me a cup. He poured for himself once more.

The Hyeolcheon Blade Demon sat with his back resting against the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber planted behind him like a wall. Compared to his gaunt frame, the overwhelmingly large saber was awe-inspiring.

“That truly is a magnificent saber.”

“Are you not afraid of that saber?”

“Why should I be afraid?”

“Because you make my saber weep.”

I did not avoid the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon’s piercing gaze.

“How could it be because of me that the saber weeps?”

“Then why does it weep?”

“Because it laments.”

“What does it lament?”

“Your disciple laced my tea with Energy-Scattering Poison at the martial tournament, and another disciple came to find me and insulted my subordinate. Your younger brother used the Demon Army for personal gain, and your subordinate threatened to kill an investigator of Hwangcheon Pavilion. The saber weeps because it laments that the people around its master fall short of its master’s character.”

The corners of the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon’s mouth curled upward.

“You have a silver tongue.”

“I merely recited facts, and suddenly I’ve become eloquent.”

“You are wrong.”

“Please enlighten me.”

“First, you said the people around me fall short of my character, but no. They suit my character exactly. I am only that much of a man.”

As expected, he was no easy old man. Those with great pride or hunger for honor were actually easier to deal with.

“And if we are being precise, it is not those people’s fault. It is yours.”

“Because of me?”

“When a dragon ascends to the heavens, it brings wind and rain.”

“Who is the dragon?”

“Who else? You. Are my people not being swept away by that wind and rain?”

“What ascension? I’m merely a loach stirring up muddy waters.”

“A loach becomes a snake, and a snake becomes a dragon.”

Again, the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon emptied his cup. He was still drinking alone.

“Damn it! Everything else is fine, but why must it be my people who get swept away?”

With that lament, the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon set down his cup and stated his purpose directly.

“Release the Hundred Blade Ghost you have locked up. Because of you, morale in our Namdojong has hit rock bottom lately.”

“If he is investigated and found innocent, he will be released.”

The Hyeolcheon Blade Demon began to emit demonic energy. Just as I had felt before, its intensity made me understand why Father had said I could not yet handle the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon. Indeed, the martial arts of a Demon Venerable were on a different level.

“Let me give you one piece of advice.”

“Do not.”

“What?”

The Hyeolcheon Blade Demon’s expression twisted for an instant, but I continued calmly.

“If you came to make a request, then only make the request. Do not make a request and give advice as well. Young people these days find such people unlikeable.”

It was a situation where he could have burst into rage, but old ginger was indeed spicy. Instead, he withdrew the demonic energy he had been pouring out.

“You’re right. These days, there are few who give me such frank words.”

“You should be wary. Stagnant water is bound to rot.”

“Who stagnates because he wishes to? When you have soaked in it long enough, attachment forms, and sometimes you miss the chance to let it flow away.”

“Even so, we do not soak ourselves in rotten water, do we? Take this opportunity to let the rotten water flow away. He is not someone worth protecting.”

The Hyeolcheon Blade Demon emptied the cup in his hand. After draining it cleanly, he held that cup out to me.

“Will you accept a drink from me?”

It was the first drink he had offered since we sat together. I obediently accepted the cup.

As he poured, the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon spoke.

“Very well. I will let the rotten water flow away.”

“You are wise indeed.”

Just as I was about to empty the cup, the Hyeolcheon Blade Demon threw out a meaningful remark.

“In return, from now on, Second Young Master, you become my water.”

Chapter 31: You're the Crazy One.
---------------------------------
My cup, heading toward my lips, stopped dead in mid-air.

Become his man? No way.

"What do you mean?"

"I'm asking you to become my man."

I hadn't expected Doma to come at me like this. He was publicly known as someone who supported my older brother. And yet he was abandoning my brother and choosing me? Truly, this was the most unexpected moment since meeting Hyeolcheon Doma.

"Young lord."

"Yes."

"Do you wish to become a Heavenly Demon?"

"I wish to become one."

I answered without hesitation.

"Then drink that wine. I shall become your backer."

"But weren't you supporting my older brother, sir?"

"That was a choice made when I didn't know you were such a madman."

"I've become a dragon, and I've become a madman—I've certainly been busy."

You're the madman.

I had killed his younger brother, crippled his disciple, and imprisoned his subordinate. And yet this old man was making an offer to become my backer. This old man was a human who would sell anything for his ambition.

"I killed your younger brother."

"Once one leaves the parents' bosom, one is a stranger. You think I feel pain like my entrails are being cut because my blood kin died? Don't make me laugh. It is the thorn stuck beneath one's own fingernail that hurts more."

This old man—every word he was spewing now was sincere.

"Anyway, it's not as if I've formally joined hands with the First Young Master... Even if I had, one's mind can always change."

"Gaining you, sir, would be like gaining a thousand armies and ten thousand horses. However..."

"What are you hesitating for?"

"I wonder if I can trust you, sir. Just as you are abandoning my brother now, you could abandon me too."

"If the situation calls for it, I must discard you. Do you think I'm doing this because I trust you? I'm a man who has never trusted anyone until now."

"Then why have you chosen me?"

"Because your madness, pushing forward so fearlessly, seemed worth staking my fate on."

"So in the end, you chose me because I'm a madman."

"Use that madness to devour them all."

If I were a young man of this age, I might have believed Hyeolcheon Doma's words wholeheartedly, swelling with pride at being chosen.

But I know well the despicable methods old men use when exploiting the young.

Do you want to become a Heavenly Demon?

The question he had asked earlier was like that. Words designed to create the illusion that if I took his hand, I could become a Heavenly Demon.

The question he was tossing out now was imbued with the same intention.

"If you become a Heavenly Demon, what kind of Heavenly Demon would you be?"

"At the very least, the Main Sect under my command would not see Demon Venerables stepping forth to pressure the Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord."

"Hahaha. As expected, my eyes are precise indeed."

That snakelike attitude that never reveals its true feelings even when disgusted.

All of it was the old man's high-level artifice to cloud my decision in this moment.

For now, I politely refused. In the push and pull of human relationships, if you're pulled and simply dragged along, you become unappealing prey.

"I am grateful that you think highly of me, but now seems to be the time I must attend to my duties. What would my father think? If he entrusted me with an important position and I did no work but instead engaged in politicking, would he not disapprove?"

"You're hiding behind the Cult Leader. Is that truly a wise choice?"

"What do you mean?"

"Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord? What if you do that job well? What if the Cult Leader keeps you seated in the position of Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord? Do you intend to rot away as the Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord for life? That task is merely a process now. The path you must choose is always one: a fork in the road of whether or not you become the successor. The moment you mistake that, you will lose your way and wander through the deceptively fine forest called Hwangcheon Pavilion."

He brought my father into it to finish his persuasion.

"Rather, the Cult Leader will take note of you joining hands with me. The mere fact that I chose you will increase your stature and draw everyone's eyes."

But there is one thing Hyeolcheon Doma is overlooking.

The will to enforce discipline in the Main Sect. It is not only my will that is invested here; my father's will is invested as well.

"Please give me time to think. I will drink this wine once I have made my decision."

I set the wine cup I was holding back onto the table.

Even though I had postponed my decision, Hyeolcheon Doma smiled with satisfaction.

"Exactly. Of course you should. You mustn't drink it like a fool just because someone tells you to."

Hyeolcheon Doma rose from his seat and drew the Myeolcheon Daedo that had been stuck behind him.

With the very momentum of drawing the blade from the ground, he swung it at me.

Without dodging, I drew the Black Demon Sword and blocked the incoming attack.

*Clang!*

An explosive sound mixed with metallic ringing burst forth. From beyond the crossed sword and blade, Hyeolcheon Doma spoke.

"I will hear your answer tomorrow."

"I shall tell you in five days."

For a moment, a taut battle of wills hung between us as we glared at each other.

"Three days then."

Without waiting for an answer, Hyeolcheon Doma shot up into the sky with a whoosh and disappeared beyond the wall. It was an extraordinary and flawlessly swift movement technique.

I sheathed the Black Demon Sword and rotated my wrist.

"Damn old man, it hurts like hell."

It hadn't been an all-out attack, but the internal energy carried by the flying blade was no ordinary matter, so my wrist throbbed.

'This old man. He's secretly enjoying inflicting pain on his opponent.'

He was making me aware that his martial arts were superior. One might think it was merely an old man's pride or bad taste, but I believe there is a clear intention behind Hyeolcheon Doma's actions like this.

Hyeolcheon Doma either jabbed you in the ribs repeatedly until bruises formed, or inflicted pain like today, where your arm would go numb and you'd suffer for several days.

This pain plants unconscious fear in the opponent. And this physical violence plays a considerably positive role in manipulating the opponent to one's will.

The more you experience it, the more you realize he is not a pushover old man. At the same time, I understand why my father is using me to perform this sword dance.

Because there are not one but eight existences like Hyeolcheon Doma. If they are tightly bound together under the name of the Eight Demon Venerables, even my father would not find them easy to deal with. Among themselves, they slander and backbite and fight and bicker, but when facing the Heavenly Demon Cult, they become of one mind.

Moreover, they are the main force of the Main Sect. If you eliminate them just because you don't like them, the Murim Alliance or the Demonic Path Alliance will immediately come charging in.

'You like me because I'm a madman? Those are words spoken only when you don't know how far I can go.'

Before returning to my quarters, I briefly stopped by Ian's personal training ground.

Ian was so focused on training that she didn't even know I had come; as I had instructed, she was in the middle of basic stamina training. Gasping as if her breath would give out, Ian endured and devoted herself to training.

'Why you?'

Because if it's my order, you become a stubborn fool of a bear. You weren't originally a stubborn fool of a bear, yet you willingly become such a person for me.

After watching her train for a moment, I returned to my room.

I brought some wine and sat by the window, taking it easy. When I think of Hwamugi, I cannot rest for even a moment, but that doesn't mean I'll just run forward looking only ahead. If I lose the leisure to look around, there will surely be things I miss.

And so, with the brilliantly bright moon as my drinking snack, I had a few drinks for the first time in a while.

I thought of my father, of Hyeolcheon Doma, and of the other Demon Venerables. I also thought about my life after killing Hwamugi.

* * *

Even the next day, Yang Tae's spirit was unbroken.

"Hmph! Hwangcheon Pavilion Lord, you've messed with the wrong person. Don't you know that touching me is the same as touching the entire Namdo Sect?"

Having threatened me like this, using Hyeolcheon Doma as his backing.

"If you just release me, I'll let bygones be bygones. I'll personally speak to the Demon Venerable to ensure his anger brings no harm to the Pavilion Lord."

He also tried to entice me like this.

He had never experienced such a desperate situation in his life. Looking at his records, he had risen steadily from Dogwi to Sipdogwi to Baekdogwi. He had never suffered hardship, and no one had ever picked a fight with him in his life.

So he didn't know how to conduct himself in this situation. Rather than sincerely apologizing and begging for forgiveness, he was simply resorting to enticement and threats as he had always lived.

Unlike yesterday, I faced Yang Tae calmly.

"Warrior Yang. Sit down. Today, the Namdo Sect officially announced its regret over this incident."

At that, Yang Tae was overjoyed.

"I told you so. The Demon Venerable would never abandon me. Now, release me."

Thinking he would soon be released, Yang Tae's expression softened.

"Let us forget the past."

At his attitude, as if he would forgive me, I calmly asked, "Do you know why I am treating you politely now?"

"Isn't it because we want to wrap this up nicely?"

"No. It is because this is the last day I will see you."

"Isn't that what I said? Once I get out, we'll have no reason to see each other again."

Yang Tae's face was full of joy. Now was the time to make that face show its true expression.

"I think you're misunderstanding something."

"Misunderstanding?"

"The Namdo Sect did not express regret to us; it expressed regret to you. To you, for tarnishing the Namdo Sect's honor."

"...What?"

"And they officially apologized for the fact that this unsavory incident was caused by a martial artist of the Namdo Sect. They apologized to the victim and promised compensation. They also said they would ensure such a thing never happens again."

"What... nonsense? The Namdo Sect has never officially apologized for something like this. No matter what incident occurred."

Yang Tae's voice was trembling.

"You should be honored. You've changed the Namdo Sect like this."

"Stop lying!"

"Well then, I hope you enjoy your stay in the underground prison."

I rose from my seat. Belatedly, Yang Tae screamed and caused a commotion. I told them to just leave him. That tantrum, too, would be his last today.

Instead, he would have to take responsibility for his actions, having not shown a single moment of sincere reflection until the very end.

I gave instructions to Seo Daeryong, who was waiting outside the door.

"Contact the underground prison and send him to the hardest section."

"Yes."

The Main Sect's underground prison is hell even in the comfortable sections. The saying that it's better to be beheaded than to go to the underground prison isn't spoken for no reason. With that personality, he wouldn't last in the underground prison. His son would be the same.

I did not sympathize with them. If I had not intervened, they would have lived their entire lives in arrogance, while the victim Gwak Su and his family would have lived miserable lives. There must have been other Gwak Sus I didn't know about before, and more Gwak Sus would continue to appear in the future. The investigator Jonghwa, who had been threatened, would have had his future life completely ruined as well.

Therefore, I felt not a shred of sympathy or regret.

"What in the world happened?"

"What?"

"Why did the Namdo Sect issue an apology statement? This is unprecedented."

"Go and ask them why."

"I would if I could. In the current atmosphere, if you went to the Namdo Sect, you probably wouldn't walk out."

"No. Right now is precisely when you could dance in the Namdo Sect's training ground and walk out safely."

Leaving behind Seo Daeryong, who couldn't understand what I meant, I strode down the corridor and added:

"For the next two days, that is."

Chapter 32: I Need Anju.
------------------------
The next day, I opened a branch of the Hwangcheon Pavilion in Maga Village.

It was located across from the Pungnyu Tavern. It was a truly small branch, with only one novice investigator and a single enforcement martial artist assigned to it.

As he watched the necessary furnishings being brought in and the signboard being hung, Jo Chunbae, the owner of the Pungnyu Tavern, cautiously glanced at me before asking,

“Pavilion Master, why did you put the branch here?”

“Suppose you were wronged by martial artists of the Main Cult. What would you have done until now?”

“……What do you mean, what would I have done?”

Everything was contained in Jo Chunbae’s question. What could he have done? He would simply have remained wronged.

“If the matter involved martial artists of the Main Cult, you could come to the Hwangcheon Pavilion and report it. Do you know anyone nearby who has done that?”

“No. Not that I know of.”

There was no way there could be anyone. To reach the Hwangcheon Pavilion, one had to report the purpose of one’s visit from the very front gate of the Main Cult, undergo an identity check and even a body search. One could only arrive at the Hwangcheon Pavilion after going through such procedures several times.

So who would go there to make a complaint? In the end, the Hwangcheon Pavilion had done little more than abandon the residents of Maga Village.

“Spread the word for me. Tell them that if the residents of Maga Village suffer harm at the hands of martial artists from the Main Cult, they can come to this branch and report it.”

“But…… everyone will hesitate, fearing retaliation.”

“Of course they will. That is why I will make an announcement soon. Anyone who retaliates against a complainant in connection with an investigation by the Hwangcheon Pavilion will be sentenced to decapitation.”

“Decapitation? Ah, so you truly are opening this branch for our sake.”

Jo Chunbae was deeply moved.

Under normal circumstances, he would not have believed it, thinking it nothing more than a formal procedure. He would have assumed that it would eventually become an empty shell.

But this time, he had personally witnessed and heard of me dealing with Baekdo Ghost and his son, so Jo Chunbae was elated.

“Everyone, come over here and listen.”

He began telling the people in the marketplace about the existence of the branch.

I had no way of knowing how many people would come forward with their grievances in the future.

But regardless of the number, this attempt would play a major role in establishing discipline within the Main Cult. Now that people could make complaints so easily, the abuse of the weak would decrease.

After the branch was set in order, Seo Daeryong looked pleased as we returned.

“We should have done this long ago…… You did very well.”

“It’s a matter of where you place the center of gravity.”

“What do you mean?”

“Until now, the Hwangcheon Pavilion’s center of gravity has been in its head. We solved various problems within the Cult while watching the expressions of the Eight Demon Venerables. We strayed from our original purpose and became servants of power. From now on, I intend to place the Hwangcheon Pavilion’s center of gravity in its legs. We will begin by protecting the residents of Maga Village, low-ranking martial artists, and those who suffer injustice because they are relatively weak. We only need to remember one principle throughout this process.”

“What principle is that?”

“That the weight of a life is the same. A low-ranking martial artist and a Demon Venerable have lives of equal weight. Therefore, we will handle matters according to the same standards.”

Passion flashed across Seo Daeryong’s face.

“If someone else had said that, I would have considered it nonsense. A pathetic idealistic theory that ignores reality. But if it is you, Pavilion Master, I believe you.”

“Was that an insult or a compliment? You just insulted me, didn’t you?”

“If you come at me any more frighteningly, I’ll be the first person to file a complaint.”

Watching him hurry away, I chuckled.

And there was one thing I needed to do if I was going to maintain the momentum of the Hwangcheon Pavilion that I had spoken of to him.

* * *

I summoned all the enforcement martial artists.

“We pay our respects to the Pavilion Master!”

They greeted me at the top of their voices. After locking even Baekdo Ghost in the dungeon this time, my popularity had risen higher than when I had first taken office.

It was not only my popularity that had increased. My authority had grown as well. Even those who had quietly looked down on me because they believed I had lost to my elder brother in the succession struggle had changed their attitudes. Whenever they encountered me while walking, they lowered their eyes first.

“I thank all of you for your devotion to the Pavilion until now.”

I stepped beside the platform and expressed my gratitude with a cupped-fist salute. The enforcement martial artists likewise saluted in unison and received my greeting.

“This is the first time we have gathered without the investigators, isn’t it?”

“Yes!”

“On a good day like today, I should be telling you something pleasant. But today, I intend to tell you something somewhat uncomfortable.”

The enforcement martial artists tensed at my words.

“As you all know, there are those within the Main Cult who refuse to submit to and challenge the authority of our Pavilion. The Demon Army did so, and the Ghosts did as well. The reason is simple. They believe themselves stronger than you.”

It was something that would wound their pride, but I did not mince my words. There were things to say to investigators and things to say to enforcement martial artists. For these men, speaking directly would be more effective than beating around the bush.

“Take any one of the Eight Demon Venerables. Could you take my warrant, arrest a leader of Baekdo Ghost’s caliber, and bring him here?”

No one could answer with confidence. That was reality.

“Why can’t you? Because they believe their fists are stronger than the laws of our Pavilion. How can we change that? It’s simple. Your fists must become stronger than theirs. If they are afraid of you, they will not even dare think of defying you.”

Everyone’s expressions grew flushed. Each of them must have experienced at least once what it was like for their authority to be ignored.

“I want you to become stronger than you are now.”

The enforcement martial artists were certainly strong as they were. But they were not strong enough to overwhelm the martial artists of the elite organizations. They were comparable, or perhaps even slightly weaker.

“That is why, starting today, we will cancel all schedules and begin special training.”

The words special training elicited various reactions. Some looked pleased, while others hardened their expressions.

“If you believe this change does not suit you, it would be best to quit now. I will send you to another department, so step forward.”

After hesitating for a moment, two men stepped forward. They honestly said that they wished to go to other departments.

I promised to assign each of them to the department of their choice and sent them away.

“Don’t worry about what others think. Not everyone needs to fight in the Hwangcheon Pavilion. Each person need only support the Main Cult from the place that suits them. Anyone else?”

No one else stepped forward.

“Good.”

I beckoned to someone waiting in the distance.

It was Jangho, the Third Commander of the Demon Army—the strongest man among its commanders.

Ever since I had killed the Demon Army Lord and avenged my friend the last time, he had felt great fondness for me.

“Starting today, this man will be giving you special training.”

The Demon Army was currently on standby, as a new Demon Army Lord had yet to be chosen. That was why I had been able to make this request.

Jangho stepped before them. With his massive build and the scars across his face, he overwhelmed the enforcement martial artists.

“I am Jangho, and I will be in charge of your training for the time being. I personally respect the Pavilion Master here, which is why I agreed to take on this task. The training will be grueling. In return, I promise you one thing. Those who endure will undoubtedly be stronger than before. Understood?”

“Yes!”

The enforcement martial artists answered in loud voices.

“Training will begin in half a shichen. Prepare yourselves and gather here again. Dismissed.”

After all the enforcement martial artists dispersed, only Jangho and I remained.

“How is the atmosphere in the Demon Army these days?”

“It’s unsettled. Things should stabilize once the next Demon Army Lord is chosen.”

I had also heard various rumors about the next Demon Army Lord. I had heard that, because it was such an important position, Father and Sama Myeong were agonizing over the appointment. There were also rumors that the Demon Venerables had begun moving to establish their own connections.

“I’m sorry to ask for your help at a time like this.”

“Not at all. Call on me anytime. If it is your command, Young Master—no, Pavilion Master—I will come running whenever you call.”

“Thank you.”

Even after Jangho left, I was about to turn away when I heard a voice coming from somewhere.

“Only two days left now.”

I turned my head and saw the Blood Heaven Saber Demon sitting atop the wall behind me.

“You certainly have a lot of free time.”

“Once you reach the level of a Demon Venerable, time is something you have in abundance.”

“If I become the Cult Lord, the Demon Venerables will have more than enough work to do.”

“To make that happen, take my hand.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon was pressuring me, trying to force his wishes through by any means.

“If my elder brother finds out you’re acting like this, he’ll be hurt.”

“He’ll understand. Isn’t it only human nature to side with whoever does things better? Do you know why I hate those orthodox sect bastards? They dress up feelings like this in far too many layers. They could simply say, ‘I like that fellow because he has more money,’ or ‘I like that fellow because he’s stronger.’ Instead, they prattle on about consultation and morality. They act as if being honest would cause them to suffer qi deviation.”

“Wouldn’t I be the same?”

“What would?”

“That human nature to side with whoever does things better. Another Demon Venerable might want to join hands with me. Among them, there may even be someone who does things better than you.”

His answer ought to have been awkward, but the Blood Heaven Saber Demon responded with ease.

“Listen, Young Master Yi. Do you believe in your own abilities and potential?”

“Yes.”

“And do you have the confidence to become the Heavenly Demon?”

“I do.”

“I was the first person to recognize that. Just consider the ability to see someone’s true worth. Doesn’t that alone tell you who would do the best?”

“Even a silver-tongued man like me can’t compete with you, Elder.”

“Hahahaha. See you in two days.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon burst into laughter, then leaped away and vanished.

‘The old man certainly knows how to make anything sound convincing.’

At a glance, everything he said sounded right. But when I thought about it calmly, it was little more than sophistry.

It was only recently that I had begun attracting attention within the Cult. Everyone must have been watching my transformation.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon had merely stepped forward first because he had become entangled with me. He was claiming me as his own just because he had spat a little.

Joining hands with the Blood Heaven Saber Demon was not unthinkable. If he truly became my ally, he would undoubtedly be a great source of strength.

But I could not join hands with him like this. He still held the initiative.

* * *

The next day, I watched the enforcement martial artists train from a hill overlooking the training grounds.

Jangho was merciless. It was truly training that ground down flesh and bone, yet the enforcement martial artists gritted their teeth and persevered.

Before they began, Jangho had provoked them with a single sentence.

―This is the training our Demon Army undergoes.

That one sentence was enough. If they could not endure the training undergone by the Demon Army, it would amount to admitting that they were allowing the Demon Army to make a mockery of the Hwangcheon Pavilion.

I thought Jangho was an exceptionally clever man. Nothing was more effective on martial artists than provoking their pride.

Jangho’s shout rang out from the distance.

“Endure! Your wives, your children, your friends—anyone can betray you. But this sweat you’re shedding now will never betray you!”

I chuckled as I listened to Jangho’s roar. It was amusing to hear him mention wives and children when he had never even married.

‘At any rate, they’ve found themselves a proper master.’

The thought briefly crossed my mind that having a martial artist like Jangho under my command would be extremely reassuring.

‘Ah!’

Then a good idea occurred to me regarding the Blood Heaven Saber Demon.

* * *

On the third day, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon was waiting for me at the place where he had first suggested joining hands.

As before, he had planted the Myeolcheon Great Saber into the ground and was drinking while leaning against its blade.

“Are you ready to drink my liquor now?”

I sat down across from him.

“I am ready.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon laughed heartily with satisfaction and poured me a drink.

We made an enthusiastic toast, but I did not drink. I set the cup down instead.

“Why aren’t you drinking?”

“I need a side dish.”

“A side dish? What kind of side dish does a true man need?”

Displeasure appeared on the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s face. He had realized that the side dish I referred to was not actual food, but a condition I was demanding.

“What sort of side dish do you need?”

Amid the taut tension, I answered,

“The Demon Army.”

At my wholly unexpected words, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s eyes widened.

“Put the Demon Army into my mouth.”

Chapter 33: Is That the New Protective Armor?
---------------------------------------------
“If I put it in your mouth, can you digest it?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked provocatively.

“I’ll chew it thoroughly and swallow.”

“Listen, Second Young Master. There is a proper order to worldly affairs.”

“Isn’t eating side dishes while drinking wine also part of the proper order?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon pressed his fingers hard against both sides of his brow, as if his head ached. Then he exploded in anger and shot to his feet.

“I can’t take this anymore. I waited three whole days, and now you’re putting forward this absurd excuse for a condition?”

He pulled the Heaven-Destroying Great Blade from where it was embedded in the ground.

“I’ll take that head of yours to the Heavenly Demon Hall!”

Shwaaaaaak!

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon swung the Heaven-Destroying Great Blade. It wasn’t truly meant to kill me, but it was a testy attack filled properly with rage.

Kaaang!

When I parried it with the Black Demon Sword, a sharp metallic ring burst out.

“Not bad! Not bad at all!”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon exclaimed in admiration. It seemed he hadn’t expected me to block so easily an attack into which he had poured a considerable amount of internal energy.

“Yes, I suppose it won’t be easy to sever that precious head of yours.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon swung the Heaven-Destroying Great Blade again. It was faster and stronger than the previous attack.

The blade came flying down vertically, tearing through the wind, and once again I struck it away with the Black Demon Sword.

Kaaaaang!

A louder sound than before rang out.

Though the Heaven-Destroying Great Blade bore the nickname Weapon Breaker, it could not damage the Black Demon Sword. I had room to spare.

—He keeps acting up. Should we just snap it in half? The Weapon Breaker, broken! Wouldn’t that make a splendid rumor to decorate tomorrow morning?

It was a thought I had sent to the sword like a joke, but perhaps because I had entertained such an idea, the desire to use the Four Steps of the Wind God surged within me.

Could the Blood Heaven Blade Demon truly block the Myeongwang Step if I unleashed it?

This was a temptation born from my fighting spirit. When faced with a powerful foe, a supreme martial art like the Four Steps of the Wind God reacted. Let us fight. Let us win.

‘No! Not yet.’

Time was on my side. There was no reason to wage a dangerous battle with my still-lacking internal energy.

Before the third attack could come, I pretended my wrist hurt and lightly retreated. Of course, that did not mean I withdrew my intent.

“Give me the Demon Army.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon lowered his blade and asked,

“Just who are you asking me to seat as the Demon Army Lord? Surely not yourself?”

“Of course not.”

“Then who?”

“Please seat Third Unit Captain Jang Ho.”

“Jang Ho?”

If Jang Ho became the Demon Army Lord, I could turn the Demon Army into an organization friendly to me. If that happened, my position within the cult would become incomparably stronger than before.

And not only would it help me, but if a martial artist like Jang Ho became the Demon Army Lord, it would be of great benefit to our cult as well.

“Seating Jang Ho as Demon Army Lord will not be easy even for me.”

His words were true.

If a commander were chosen from within the Demon Army, Jang Ho would be a strong candidate, but in the case of an elite organization like the Demon Army, its head was selected from outside. The Heavenly Demon Hall and the Eight Demon Venerables would engage in a battle of nerves, each striving to place their own people.

“Second Young Master! If greed goes too far, the belly bursts.”

“Isn’t the path we must walk one where we have to devour so much our bellies burst?”

“We?”

“You were the one who said we should devour everything with madness, Elder. You were also the one who asked to join hands with me. How could the path of mad bastards ever be an ordinary one?”

“Bastards?”

“Then is it not so? If it were anyone else, I might not know, but how could you possibly join hands with me unless you were mad?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon glared at me with blazing eyes.

I did not know where his ambition ultimately pointed. He had died before revealing it to the world.

Astonishingly, the first of the Eight Demon Venerables to die had been the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. This fastidious, cunning Blade Demon, of all people.

“Hmph! You speak as though you’ve already become the Heavenly Demon.”

“Were you not the one who put the wind in my sails, Elder? Just what do you intend to do with me? Are you simply trying to use a young man with nothing but fine-sounding words? Like your disciples?”

Perhaps my words struck home, for the Blood Heaven Blade Demon shouted furiously,

“Your greed has overturned the drinking table!”

Bang.

After actually kicking the drinking table over, he shot away just like that.

As I watched him disappear into the distance, I instead smiled faintly. It was the first moment the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s composure had cracked.

He would not be able to refuse my proposal. Because he knew better than anyone that, aside from his drinking table, I still had seven more.

* * *

I devoted myself to martial training in my private training ground.

It was something I always felt while training after my regression, but truly, tireless youth was a wonderful thing. This was not a matter of how much internal energy I had. Even without special effort, my body was light and full of vitality.

The eternal truth that one only realizes something’s value after losing it surely applied most powerfully to youth.

Yes, at this time, I had not known. How precious the time flowing past me was. How much I would come to miss this moment later.

Martial arts? Money? Power? The fact that what one offered up to obtain those things was youth… was both laughable and sad. Even after regressing, it was my reality that I had no choice but to run forward once more.

Even so, it was a mistake I made while knowing. Because I understood its preciousness better than anyone, I trained with care and all my strength, even in the act of taking a single step.

Using the Four Steps of the Wind God, I moved among imaginary enemies.

At first, there were three or four enemies, but as my training continued, their number increased. Five became seven, and seven became ten.

At first they had been enemies standing like scarecrows, but now the enemies in my imagination moved, conversed, and even cursed at me. The more I trained, the higher their martial level became. The level of my training was determined by the scope of my martial imagination.

If seventy percent of my training was devoted to the Four Steps of the Wind God, the remaining time was spent practicing the qi training my father had taught me and the Heavenly Demon Protective Art.

After a bout of training ended, I lay on the floor of the training hall and caught my breath. I had sweated enough for my whole body to be drenched, but there was no moment as pleasant as this one after finishing training.

Beyond the window, I could see clouds flushed red by the sunset drifting by.

How wonderful would it be to travel the Central Plains with this young body? The sunset I watched while gathering materials for the Great Art and the sunset I would see after killing Hwa Mugi and becoming the greatest under heaven would be completely different…

Before my regression, I had endured all that hardship by imagining only this moment after returning. Now I was enduring by looking forward to the future after killing Hwa Mugi.

I must not think only of today’s me. The me of tomorrow, who might die miserably to an opponent because my training was insufficient, was also me. Yes, endure it.

When I rose again, the imaginary enemies also rose once more.

* * *

Two days later, new news arrived.

It was news that the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had recommended Third Unit Captain Jang Ho as a candidate for Demon Army Lord.

Though the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had vanished in irritation, in the end he had accepted my will. From the start, it had been a drinking table he could not overturn.

The person most surprised by this news was Jang Ho himself.

“Just what happened?”

The mere fact that he came to ask me made him intelligent and perceptive.

If he had been an ordinary person, he would have considered this sudden good fortune. But Jang Ho was someone who had realized at least that there was no such thing as luck like that in the world.

“This was something you made happen, was it not, Second Young Master?”

“That’s right. I asked the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.”

“I am grateful that you think highly of me, but it is not a position I can handle.”

“There’s no need to be modest. The Demon Army Lord who died by my hand occupied that seat as well.”

“That is a different case.”

“Just lead them as you led the Third Unit. Then I am certain you will become the greatest Demon Army Lord of all time.”

In particular, he was the strongest in martial arts among the unit captains. He also had the active support of the Third Unit’s martial artists. If someone were chosen from within, he was fully qualified.

“You are overestimating me too much, Second Young Master.”

“Or perhaps you are underestimating yourself. In any case, other candidates are being mentioned as well, so let us watch a little longer.”

Even amid his confusion, Jang Ho was also happy. What else would be the dream of the Demon Army’s unit captains?

“Now, we’ve done all we can, so let us leave the result to heaven’s will.”

* * *

I had told Jang Ho to do all that was humanly possible and await heaven’s will, but as if that indifferent heaven would kindly answer. I had to move diligently and make things happen myself.

That night, I went to find my father.

My father had separate quarters in the Heavenly Demon Hall, and I suspect they were probably the largest and most splendid quarters in the world.

And at the unfamiliar sight of my father standing before me now, I blinked as though I had seen a hallucination.

“Is that a newly made protective armor?”

“No. These are my sleepwear.”

“……”

“……”

“They’re sleepwear with a flower pattern. Not demons or evil spirits…”

“I believe one should sleep comfortably.”

It was the first time I had seen my father in sleepwear. The bright flower-patterned sleepwear was shocking enough, but what surprised me more was that he had shown me this sight so openly. He could have told me to wait and changed into other clothes.

“What brings you here in the middle of the night?”

“The middle of the night? It’s early evening. I didn’t know you went to bed this early. Isn’t this top-secret information? If the Martial Alliance finds out, they’ll invade at once. They’ll say, ‘The Heavenly Demon goes to sleep early. Let’s launch a midnight raid!’”

“Get to the point!”

“Yes.”

I stopped joking there and revealed the reason I had come today.

“I intend to use the Blood Heaven Blade Demon as my blade.”

“You said you would kill him before.”

“He’s too precious to kill.”

This would not be new information to my father. When the Blood Heaven Blade Demon recommended Jang Ho, Father must already have noticed that the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and I had joined hands.

Not only Father, but anyone interested in the cult’s politics would be able to guess. Everyone was watching closely. How the relationship between the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and me would unfold. How my elder brother would react later, and how the other Demon Venerables would each move.

Father changed the subject.

“How is your new sword?”

“I like it.”

“Let me see.”

I handed the Black Demon Sword to Father, sheath and all.

Father slowly drew the Black Demon Sword, then soon nodded in satisfaction.

“You’ve tamed it well.”

“You can tell just by holding it?”

“You should be able to tell by holding it. Would you only know after being stabbed?”

Father returned the Black Demon Sword and said,

“The Blood Heaven Blade Demon will not be tamed this easily.”

“I don’t intend to tame him. I’ll use him until he breaks, then throw him away. For now, he isn’t worth keeping in my collection.”

“And if you get cut in the process?”

“Then I’ll have to be cut. But there will be a difference between the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and me. The old Blood Heaven Blade Demon will be finished once he breaks, but the more I am cut, the stronger I will become. I’ll apply medicine, wrap the wound in bandages, and keep moving forward.”

“Words are easy. But why are you reporting this to me?”

“I am now receiving a stipend from you, Father, so of course I must report it.”

My father’s gaze narrowed.

“You didn’t come to ask me to accept the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s recommendation?”

He was truly a perceptive man, but this time, he was wrong.

“No. It is the opposite. Please do not, under any circumstances, approve it according to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s wishes.”

“Do not approve it? Why?”

“I intend to take this chance to break the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s spirit. You have no idea how arrogantly he behaves in front of me. At times, he acts even more superior than you, Father. I plan to break that spirit.”

“Stop spouting nonsense. Tell me your true thoughts.”

“Why do you think that isn’t my true intent?”

“This is an opportunity to obtain the Demon Army, yet you would give it up merely to break the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s spirit? You are not that sort of child.”

As expected, Father was sharp.

“Yes, you saw through me.”

“What are you aiming for?”

“There is something I need to obtain from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. For that to happen, this matter must not go according to his wishes.”

“Is it more important than the Demon Army?”

“In some ways, yes. Ah, and I will also make the Demon Army mine. I will make all of it mine.”

Father, who had been staring at me, turned away just like that.

“Stop making me laugh and leave.”

That was what he said, but I could feel it. Father would grant my request.

Looking at the flowers blooming across my father’s back, I gave a small chuckle.

‘Father. At least tonight, nothing will be funnier than those sleepwear.’

Chapter 34: Rather Than Facing a Single Tiger
---------------------------------------------
The Blood Heaven Blade Demon entered the Heavenly Demon Hall.

He walked slowly along the path of blood and stopped at the end of the carpet.

“Have you been well?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon greeted Geom Woojin with proper courtesy.

“Well enough, thanks to you. And you?”

“I have been rather busy lately, thanks to your son.”

Geom Woojin smiled faintly.

“Once a son’s head grows big, he no longer moves as I wish.”

“Hahaha. So there are things even you cannot do, Cult Leader?”

“They say children are beings arranged by Heaven to show us that life does not go according to our will. You would know if you had children of your own.”

“My children may be growing up somewhere, for all I know.”

Geom Woojin laughed. He was softer and more familiar than when he dealt with Geom Mugeuk. If Geom Mugeuk had seen it, he surely would have said, “Please smile that broadly at your son too!”
