“It is far too dangerous and difficult a procedure. And I don’t know what variables may arise during it. So I will perform it when my martial ability has risen much higher than it is now.”

“Ah! There really was a cure!”

Joy filled her face.

“You will have to risk your life. I don’t know what might happen during the procedure. Can you still do it?”

“Yes!”

There was not the slightest hesitation in her answer.

“How disappointing.”

“Yes? Did I make some mistake?”

“I thought you would say this: If I were to die, I would no longer be able to protect you, Young Master, so I will not undergo the procedure.”

It had been a joke meant to block that possibility in advance.

“You told me to live my own life, didn’t you? Was I wrong?”

Then she smiled brightly, as if it had been a joke.

Now I knew. That expression just now was her true self.

Her original heart, which had been buried and put to sleep beneath loyalty and responsibility—that natural heart anyone should have—I wanted to help her find it again.

“Ian.”

“Yes, Young Master.”

“Is there anywhere in the Central Plains you want to visit?”

“No, there isn’t.”

Since she had spent her whole life looking only at me, she likely had never properly enjoyed any place she had gone.

“Later, let’s go traveling through the Central Plains together.”

“Really?”

“Yes. Let’s visit every famous site and see every scenic view. I’ll let you taste every famous dish. I happen to know quite a few places.”

“You promise?”

“You promise too.”

“What?”

“Don’t abandon me then.”

“How could I abandon you, Young Master? Even if heaven and earth were overturned, that would never happen.”

I wonder if you would still say that after becoming the greatest beauty under heaven, with every man in the world worshipping you.

“We shall see.”

Chapter 40: Get Stronger, Ian!
------------------------------
Early the next morning, I went to Cheonma Hall to find my father.

Father was not in the great hall, but in his personal training ground.

I was waiting outside for him to finish his training when:

“Enter.”

Having been informed of my visit, Father had me enter the training ground. As I stepped inside, my heart trembled inwardly. It was because I had never before set foot in Father’s personal training ground.

The moment I entered like that—

Father, who had been in the center of the training yard, charged at me.

The technique Father unleashed was the Myeongwangbo.

To top it off, the black Cheonma Sword flew in.

The Cheonma wielding the Cheonma Sword and unleashing the Myeongwangbo to attack someone?

No matter who the opponent was, it was an attack from which they could not survive.

In a flash, the situation was over.

The Cheonma Sword had stopped in midair.

I was standing right beside it.

It was a distance close enough for my breath to frost the blade.

I had dodged Father’s attack using the Jeommyeolbo.

Did the Jeommyeolbo win in a fight against the Myeongwangbo? Of course not.

It was an attack I had only barely been able to evade in the first place.

“You keep targeting this handsome face of mine.”

“Those who target you outside will not be as merciful as I.”

“Thank you for sparing my life.”

I looked around Father’s training ground as if nothing had happened.

“I thought there might be something special here, but there isn’t.”

There really was nothing.

In the empty space, only a few swords, blades, spears, and the like were propped against the walls.

“Not even a single training dummy, at that.”

“Why would I keep what is unnecessary?”

Well, I also trained by imagining a hypothetical enemy. How would Father be any different?

Then, a steel plate attached to the wall caught my eye.

One finger-span thick, it was densely pocked with fist-shaped dents, and surprisingly, its material was ten-thousand-year cold iron.

“Good heavens! Just how powerful are your fists? This is outright abuse of ten-thousand-year cold iron.”

“Stop your nonsense and come here.”

There was a reason Father had brought me into the training ground.

“Show me the Pungsinsabo.”

“Yes.”

Opportunities to receive Father’s teachings were rare, so I did my utmost to perform the Pungsinsabo.

The Pungsinsabo consisted of four major techniques, but within those techniques were over nine hundred variations. Depending on the interpretation, those variations could increase or decrease.

Depending on how one interpreted and utilized those variations, the movements changed little by little, and ultimately, the power of the Pungsinsabo differed. This was precisely why the same martial art could feel different and possess different power depending on who used it.

When I finished the techniques, Father was visibly animated. Likely because my achievement was higher than he had expected.

Together with Father, I discussed at length the techniques I had displayed.

At first, it was a debate about whether to put more power into the feet or retract it, and whether to twist the waist faster.

I conveyed my thoughts exactly as they were.

In some parts, I even contradicted Father’s opinion, but surprisingly, Father possessed incredibly flexible thinking.

He demonstrated the technique right there on the spot exactly as I said.

“As you say, putting more power into the right foot seems to be the correct interpretation.”

Only while discussing martial arts did Father feel like a different person. He was free, generous, and rational.

For a long while, I talked with Father about the Pungsinsabo like that.

So engrossed was I in the conversation that it was only when I felt hungry that the discussion on martial arts ended.

When the conversation ended, I felt that my understanding of the Pungsinsabo had grown a level.

“Thank you for granting me such great teaching. This grace is…”

*I shall repay it by killing Hwa Wuqi.*

“I will repay it all with money later.”

At my jest, Father abruptly said:

“Eat before you go.”

I was startled for a moment.

“Why are you so surprised?”

“It’s the first time I’ve ever heard such words from you, Father. Asking me to share a meal.”

“You are surprised by the strangest things.”

Father left the training ground first. As Father said, I thought it strange to be moved by such a trivial thing, but honestly, my heart welled up.

I ate a meal with Father.

I had expected a feast, but there were fewer dishes than I had thought. It was a truly neat and modest table.

“There is more rice, so eat your fill.”

“Yes.”

I ate without jesting. Since this was not an opportunity that came often, I tried my best to remember this moment.

Father gave me a glance as if wondering what was wrong with me for eating so quietly, then ate his meal in silence.

Come to think of it, when I was with Father, I used to deliberately joke around, jest, and banter more.

But in this moment, it was the me from just before my regression and Father who were having a meal. As old man to old man, martial artist to martial artist. And yet, still father and son.

*‘Father… I missed moments like this.’*

Never before had I missed them. But only now that this moment had come did I realize that deep in my heart, I had longed for this.

And so, I finished the historic first meal with Father. This first meal, where not a word was spoken, was something I would never forget for the rest of my life.

After the meal, Father asked me:

“Did the food suit your taste?”

Now it was time to return to my usual self.

“Would you like to switch chefs with me? Don’t feel slighted, Chef Im. It was just a joke.”

“Nonsense.”

“Haha.”

Father and I shared a drink.

“You drank with Doma at Magachon?”

“Yes.”

As expected, Father knew every single move I made within the sect.

“How much do you trust him?”

“I don’t trust him.”

“Why?”

“Because I don’t know him. No matter how transparent someone may seem, one must never be arrogant enough to claim to know them.”

When I answered according to Father’s teachings, Father wore a satisfied expression. He must be worried that I might trust Doma and get stabbed in the back.

“Why did you come to see me today?”

It was only after four double-hours or more had passed since I came to find Father that I received this question.

“I have a request.”

“Speak.”

“Please add one more member to my family.”

Father made an expression as if asking what kind of absurd nonsense this was.

“Do you mean you wish to marry?”

“No.”

“Then?”

“I wish to teach the Bicheon Swordsmanship to Ian, my bodyguard.”

I intended to teach Ian the Bicheon Swordsmanship, which I had mastered to grand completion. Only then could she keep her position as the Ghost Shadow Lord.

The problem was that the Bicheon Swordsmanship was a martial art passed down only to the direct bloodline of the Cheonma. It could not be taught without Father’s permission.

“You speak ill of your true blood behind their backs, yet you would treat a bodyguard as family?”

“Yes. She is someone dearer to me than blood. And someone who will accomplish great things in the future.”

“The Bicheon Swordsmanship is your exclusive martial art. Are you sure this is fine?”

“My exclusive martial art will be the Guhwamagong.”

The entire process of recruiting Hyeolcheon Doma and Ilhwa Sword Venerable, seating Jangho as the Demon Lord, and establishing a new demonic path would all be for naught if I failed to become the successor. Ultimately, all these things were to earn Father’s recognition. Because only by becoming the successor and receiving the Guhwamagong could I face Hwa Wuqi.

“You are confident.”

“She is someone sufficiently worthy to be taught.”

“In what way?”

“She is a better person than me.”

Perhaps because it was an unexpected answer, Father was silent for a moment.

After taking a sip of alcohol, Father asked:

“Do you intend to make that child the leader of your private organization?”

“Yes, that’s right. Going forward, there may be demonic followers who are eliminated by me. I will fill that void with my private organization. Therefore, please grant me permission.”

Then came Father’s cheerful single word.

“Very well.”

At first, I thought I had misheard. I hadn’t thought he would grant permission so easily.

“Do you truly mean that I may teach the Bicheon Swordsmanship to Ian?”

I asked again to confirm, but Father silently nodded his head. I had thought he would attach some condition and demand a mission be completed before granting permission… Why did he give it so easily?

“However, promise me one thing.”

“Please command me.”

“If you decide to kill a demonic follower next time, you must tell me first. No matter which one.”

It was not a difficult promise. No, I had intended to do so even without promising. I had no intention of harming demonic followers without Father’s permission.

“Yes, I shall certainly do so.”

* * *

“You called for me?”

Having received Father’s permission, I immediately called Ian to my personal training ground.

“Do you know why I called you?”

“Yes, I have a rough guess.”

“What is it?”

“Is it not to test what I have trained so far?”

It was a natural guess, since I had called her to the training ground.

“Well? Are you prepared?”

“I have trained to the best of my ability.”

“Not that.”

“Huh?”

“I asked if you are prepared to receive a new martial art.”

Ian was startled for a moment. Soon, her surprise turned to excitement. Was there any moment more pleasant and thrilling for a martial artist than learning a martial art that elevated them?

“Ghost Shadow Lord Ian.”

“Yes.”

“From this moment, I shall impart the Bicheon Swordsmanship to you.”

Ian, who had been blank for a moment, shrieked in astonishment.

“Eek!”

It was the most surprised I had ever seen her since we met.

“No! The Bicheon Swordsmanship is a martial art passed down only to the direct bloodline of the Cheonma, isn’t it? If you teach it to me, the Lord will strike me down dead in one blow. You will also be severely scolded, Young Master. If you intend to teach me in secret, please give up. There are no secrets in this world. Even if the secret is kept, I cannot learn it.”

After she poured out her words in one go, I asked with a smile:

“Are you done speaking?”

“There is more, but I will say just this. No! Absolutely not!”

I spoke to her with a smile:

“I received Father’s permission.”

“What?”

Ian’s eyes went wide.

Watching Ian’s surprised expression, I realized how varied her expressions were. There were many facets to her surprise, but this particular one was something I was seeing for the first time.

“Really?”

“Indeed.”

“That can’t be.”

“If you doubt it, come with me and check for yourself.”

Ian, who feared Father more than anyone else in the world, waved her hands frantically and stepped back.

“I’d rather you kill me.”

“I don’t hate you enough to lie about something like this.”

“Shall I tell you the decisive reason my words are true?”

“What is it?”

“You’re right. If I taught you without permission, you’d die. Would I put you in such danger?”

It seemed Ian had that much faith in me as well.

“So you really received permission?”

“Yes.”

Ian’s pupils trembled.

“But the Bicheon Swordsmanship is your exclusive martial art, Young Master. Why are you trying to teach it to me?”

“Because to become the Ghost Shadow Lord, you must master at least this level of martial art. And… you are no different from my blood. Therefore, you may learn it.”

Ian choked up instantly.

“Young Master.”

“It’s not for you, but for me. I’m going to work you hard in the future, so don’t be too happy.”

“But how could I dare learn your martial art?”

“My exclusive martial art will be the Guhwamagong. So learn it without burden.”

In the end, tears fell drop by drop from Ian’s eyes.

“Never has anyone… valued me so dearly…”

I gently patted the back of Ian, who could not continue speaking. Ian burst into loud sobs. Something that had been piled up deep inside her heart seemed to burst out. I left her to cry her fill.

Once she had cried her heart out and composed herself, I imparted the Bicheon Swordsmanship to her.

The martial art was transmitted through mental transmission.

—The Bicheon Swordsmanship consists of eight techniques that contain the changes and energy of the heavens. Think of your dantian as the heavens, and expand it as much as possible. Your true qi must flow like the wind—sometimes like a spring breeze, sometimes as powerful as a passing typhoon. The start of inner force is….

The quick-witted Ian had perfectly memorized the mnemonic formula of the Bicheon Swordsmanship by the time I repeated it five times.

“You must be able to answer immediately even if someone pokes your side while you sleep. Do you understand?”

“Yes, I understand.”

Ian would work herself to the bone, and if I taught her from time to time, her skill would improve by leaps and bounds.

“And this.”

I took out a small wooden case from my breast and held it out to her.

“What is this?”

“Open it.”

Inside the case she opened was a single Blood Poison Pill. It was one of the Blood Poison Pills taken from the secret vault of the Golden Castle Lord.

“What is it?”

“It’s a Blood Poison Pill.”

“What? The precious Blood Poison Pill?”

“Yes. If in the future you face the danger of poisoning or encounter someone who uses poison arts, hold this in your mouth and fight.”

“Why this to me?”

“It is a gift to celebrate your formal acceptance of the Bicheon Swordsmanship.”

“No! I’ve already received the martial art, I cannot accept such a precious thing on top of that!”

“You must accept it.”

“Why?”

“Because I intend to give it even to those less close to you. I cannot give it to others without giving it to you.”

“That’s strange logic.”

“Now then, I will come later to test what you have learned. Do not neglect your training.”

Before this place became a sea of tears again, I hurriedly left.

From behind me, I felt a fiery passion that exceeded mere gratitude and thanks.

I looked forward to the future that this heat would create.

At the very least, I would not let it become a passion that makes her throw her life away for me. Therefore…

“Grow stronger, Ian!”

Chapter 41: Refined Yet Terrifying.
-----------------------------------
After that day, for a while, I focused only on teaching Ian, the work of Hwangcheon Pavilion, and my own training.

Ian learned the Bicheon Sword Art in the blink of an eye, like cotton soaking up water. She had more talent for martial arts than I had imagined. And with her painstaking effort added to that, her progress was astonishingly fast.

There was one thing I realized anew while teaching Ian.

Teaching was, at the same time, learning.

As I explained what I already knew, I was able to view myself objectively.

‘Ah, so this is how I thought about this form.’

I gained the chance to reconsider parts I had never once thought about seriously.

Teaching is not simply scooping water out of a stagnant pool. If one teaches halfheartedly, without sincerity, the water will dry up and be depleted; but if one teaches with deep thought and devotion, it becomes a way to enlarge the well itself.

That was why, as I taught Ian, I was also learning. Since I had never taught anyone even once in my previous life, this process became an extraordinary experience for me.

During a short break in the middle of training, she asked me seriously.

“Young Master. You really are going to create the Ghost Shadow Unit, right?”

“Yes.”

“Then may I make the martial uniforms? I don’t want dull black uniforms. I want to make them stylish. We can change the colors and shapes, and make separate clothes for wearing inside the cult and for operations.”

For a moment, I almost burst out laughing. Since deciding to create the Ghost Shadow Unit, I had not once thought about the color or shape of its uniforms. Come to think of it, the same had been true of every martial uniform I had ever worn in my life.

“I also want to divide the masks that cover the face according to the difficulty of the operation. The ghost engraved on the mask should be stylish yet terrifying, so that just seeing it will make their knees go weak… Why are you laughing?”

“I was wondering what a stylish yet terrifying ghost would look like.”

“I’m fairly good at drawing too. I’ll show you later.”

“The ones who join the Ghost Shadow Unit from now on will get to wear fine clothes.”

“Don’t tease me.”

Ian was truly a delicate, attentive person. If the heart that had been focused on me were directed toward others, she would become an excellent unit leader who could soothe everyone’s hearts.

“The most important thing is your skill. The Ghost Shadow Unit will carry out the most dangerous missions in the murim. Succeeding in operations and minimizing the harm to your subordinates will all depend on your skill.”

“Yes, I’ll keep that in mind.”

“And one more thing. There is something more important than skill.”

“What is that?”

“The resolve to lose subordinates. A steadfast heart that will not be wounded even if you lose them.”

Ian stared at me.

“You see me as a child, don’t you, Young Master? Because I’m always by your side like this.”

“Am I wrong?”

“You are. I’m not a child, and I’m not as innocent or kind as you think. I won’t be so heartbroken I could die just because I lose a subordinate. I’m actually worried you’ll be disappointed in me later. You’ll think, ‘Was she really this coldhearted?’”

“I won’t be disappointed over something like that. Then that’s enough.”

Perhaps thinking that, since the subject had come up, she might as well speak honestly, Ian revealed what was in her heart.

“When you said you would pass the Bicheon Sword Art on to me, Young Master, I finally felt as if my feet had touched the ground.”

“What do you mean?”

“To be honest, until then, I felt like I was floating in midair. The Ghost Shadow Unit? Would an organization like that really be created? I’m the unit leader? Really? Young Master is just caught up in some fervor right now. Those were the thoughts I had. But as I memorized the formulas of the Bicheon Sword Art, I felt that this was real. Can I really bear all of this? Ah, I’m sorry. Ah, I was trying not to ask questions so lacking in confidence anymore.”

“You may ask. No, you must. How could you walk a path that will change the fates of countless people without asking such questions? Ask today, and ask tomorrow. Ask even ten years from now. It is a path you must walk while constantly questioning. I will keep nagging you as well. We must keep asking each other.”

The people who did not ask such questions were people like the Eight Demon Venerables.

They believed their decisions were right because they thought they were superior to others. They had no concern or empathy for other people. They did not ask.

“Yes, Young Master. Whenever I’m confused about whether this is right, I’ll ask. I’ll ask you, and I’ll ask myself as well.”

I gently stroked Ian’s head once.

“Well done.”

Ian’s face reddened slightly, and she smiled shyly.

“Now, begin training again!”

“Yes!”

Around the time training ended, I brought her books on organizational management, the art of employing men, and the self-discipline of a leader.

“Read them whenever you have time to rest.”

“Yes.”

These days, because it was one training session after another, she had been neglecting learning the work of Hwangcheon Pavilion. Even if it was through books like this, she needed to learn how to run an organization.

* * *

A few days later, I went to the Southern Blade Sect to meet the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

When I entered the Southern Blade Sect, the Blade Demons flocked out to look at me.

This, too, was a place where people lived, so some were trash and some were decent. All sorts of people gathered and lived here.

In general, I bore them no ill will. Thanks to them, I had been able to complete the Heavenly Meridian Reinforcement Art splendidly.

The Blade Demons also showed goodwill toward me, as I was on good terms with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Moreover, when I had displayed the Heavenly Meridian Reinforcement Art, many had been impressed by my courage and momentum, so some even sent me looks of respect.

A few Blade Demons came up and greeted me politely, while others applauded and cheered from afar. Did they take after their master? The Blade Demons seemed like adorably insane bastards.

I waved to them.

Guided by a Blade Demon, I arrived at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s office.

The one surprised by my visit was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Has something happened that requires you to drag me away?”

“Have you committed a crime in the meantime?”

“My life is full of sins, after all.”

“Fortunately, not today.”

“Then why have you come?”

“I came to give you this.”

I held out what I had brought to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“What is this? A box that sprays deadly poison when opened?”

“Where on earth can one buy such a box? I’d like to buy a few.”

“I asked what it is.”

“Open it.”

When he opened the box, inside was a Poison-Avoiding Pearl. Coincidentally, it was the complete opposite of his joke.

“It’s a top-grade Poison-Avoiding Pearl.”

“I can see that. But why this?”

“It’s a gift.”

“A gift? Why give me something this expensive all of a sudden?”

“It is a birthday gift.”

At that moment, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon froze in shock.

“What?”

“Happy birthday. Today is your birthday, isn’t it?”

“Is today my birthday?”

His voice trembled, as if he truly had not even thought of it.

“You didn’t know?”

“It has been a long time since I forgot about things like birthdays. But how did you know?”

“Who do you think I am? There is no information Hwangcheon Pavilion does not have. I even know how many pairs of undergarments you own, Elder.”

“How many?”

“That was an exaggeration. In any case, congratulations.”

It was clearly an intentional kindness, an effort to make him truly one of my people.

I bowed politely to him and turned around.

“Did you really come only to give me a gift?”

“Yes. I’m giving it purely as a birthday present, so please feel no burden whatsoever.”

“You’re just leaving like this?”

“I am a busy man. Let’s have a drink tonight at Pungryu Tavern. I’ll be waiting. Ah, and please don’t think of smashing the place up today.”

Leaving him still standing there in a daze, I stepped out of the office.

Before closing the door, I looked inside. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was looking down at the Poison-Avoiding Pearl with an expression I had never seen before. It was the first time I had realized that among his expressions, there was one called being moved.

* * *

“By the way, is it true?”

Seo Daeryong, walking beside me down the corridor, asked with a surprised face.

“What is?”

“That you gave the Blade Demon a birthday present.”

“How did you know?”

“Good heavens. So it was true.”

“I asked how you knew.”

“How else would I know? Rumors are going around. I naturally thought it was nonsense.”

I burst out laughing. If something given in a place where only the two of us had been had spread, then the Blade Demon must have bragged to the Blade Demons about receiving a gift. He really seemed like he would never do such a thing, yet he was surprisingly talkative.

“Why did you give it to him?”

“Because it was his birthday.”

“I truly cannot understand the relationship between you two.”

Of course he couldn’t. Incidents that could have made us mortal enemies several times over had occurred, yet the Blade Demon and I were growing closer and closer.

“You don’t become close just by exchanging pleasant words.”

Together with Seo Daeryong, I entered the great hall at the end of the corridor.

All the investigators and enforcement martial artists of Hwangcheon Pavilion had gathered there.

“It has been a while since we all gathered in one place like this, hasn’t it?”

“Yes!”

A resounding answer rang out. Since the training, the morale of the enforcement martial artists had been soaring to the heavens. The investigators, too, had handled the White Blade Demon incident well, so all of them were full of courage.

“I came today to pour some oil on your burning morale.”

Everyone looked curious.

I handed the envelopes I had prepared to Seo Daeryong. The number of envelopes matched the number of martial artists in Hwangcheon Pavilion.

After checking inside an envelope, Seo Daeryong was startled.

“What money is this?”

Inside was a promissory note worth one thousand nyang.

“Give one to everyone.”

“Yes.”

Seo Daeryong distributed what was inside to everyone.

“This money is a special gift from me to you.”

At the word gift, everyone cheered.

“Thank you, Pavilion Master!”

I spoke loudly to them all.

“I am giving you this money because our Hwangcheon Pavilion must become an organization that permits no outside pressure whatsoever. In other words, I am telling you not to accept bribes. If you suddenly need money, come to me. I’ll advance your monthly pay. If your monthly pay is not enough, I’ll lend it to you from my own pocket.”

“Yes!”

At the unexpected windfall, everyone’s expressions brightened.

Nagging when people are in a good mood only eats away at the points you struggled to earn.

“Dismissed.”

As I left the great hall, Seo Daeryong followed close behind me.

“Pavilion Master. How did you know?”

“Know what?”

“That Ikho needed money. His younger sibling is getting married, so he’s been going around trying to borrow money lately. While his parents were alive, he spent everything nursing them through illness. Now, with supporting his younger siblings, there’s never a day his pockets are full.”

“How would I know? I didn’t.”

“Please don’t lie.”

In truth, I did know. I had found out by chance and had intended to give only Ikho some money, but since it had come to this, I decided to give it to everyone.

“There’s no need to be so moved. It’s all an investment for my own sake.”

“An investment for your sake, Young Master?”

“If rumors spread that I spend money freely, won’t people start quietly expecting things? They’ll think that if I become the Heavenly Demon, I’ll spend even more. Then everyone will hope I become the Heavenly Demon. That is how shallow a person I am.”

“Did you really think that far?”

“No. Since you asked, the thought occurred to me. I suppose others could think that way too.”

“So you did know about Ikho after all.”

“I gave it to everyone while I was at it. They have all worked hard. Is that so important?”

“It is important. Do you think loyalty appears out of nowhere?”

“Don’t be fooled by things like this!”

Seo Daeryong stared at me and said,

“Because this is a world where people exploit others without even bothering with such tricks.”

Then he bowed deeply and walked off on his way.

Chapter 42: Having Often Crossed Paths with Misfortune.
-------------------------------------------------------
On my way back to my quarters, someone I truly had never expected was waiting for me.

Sitting in the very place where the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had waited was the One Flower Sword Venerable.

To my astonishment, she had even set out a table of wine.

“Second Young Master, you’ve worked hard today. Have a drink before you go.”

“Gladly, Senior.”

I sat down across from the One Flower Sword Venerable. As I had with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, I smiled at ease, but inwardly I was tense.

“I thought you said you did not drink.”

“This wine is not for me, but for you, Second Young Master.”

“Thank you.”

She poured me a cup.

I drank without hesitation.

“What would you have done if that wine had poison in it? You drank without even checking.”

“I did not check, but I did prepare.”

“How?”

I spat the Poison-Repelling Bead out from my mouth. It could block poisonous fumes, but if held in the mouth while drinking wine like this, it could also neutralize poison inside the mouth.

“When did you put that in?”

“I slipped it in when I greeted you earlier.”

“How did it not show, even with a Poison-Repelling Bead in your mouth?”

“It is a top-grade Poison-Repelling Bead, so it is very small, and I also trained so my pronunciation would not leak even with it in my mouth.”

A look of admiration crossed the One Flower Sword Venerable’s face.

“Forgive my bluntness, but I know you do not harbor good feelings toward me, Senior. Under such circumstances, I could hardly gulp down the wine you offered without thought, could I?”

Because I had honestly told her I had been holding the Poison-Repelling Bead, she did not grow angry. Instead, she confirmed only one thing.

“Did you drink the Blade Demon’s wine with a Poison-Repelling Bead in your mouth as well?”

“I did not.”

In an instant, the One Flower Sword Venerable’s expression hardened.

“You trust the Blade Demon, but not me?”

“Many things happened between Elder Blade Demon and me before I drank that first cup.”

“We had things happen between us too, did we not?”

“No, we did not.”

At my firm answer, the One Flower Sword Venerable protested.

“I was the one who recommended Jang Ho again.”

“You did recommend Jang Ho, but that was not for my sake. It was to force me not to form ties with Elder Blade Demon. Was that a choice made for me?”

I did not merely press the One Flower Sword Venerable, who had no satisfying answer.

“I want to make many things happen between us, Senior. So that I may drink the wine you offer without a Poison-Repelling Bead as well.”

“Do not say things you do not mean.”

“Why would I not mean them?”

“Because your heart already has the Blade Demon in it.”

“If you were me, Senior, whom would you wish to be in my heart?”

As if to say it was her, I drank the wine without the Poison-Repelling Bead. At my action, showing that I trusted her, the One Flower Sword Venerable’s expression softened.

She was someone who rejected even being compared to the Blade Demon. She was also someone with the pride to believe that, if it came down to one of the two, I would naturally choose her. As for poison—of course she was not the sort to put such a thing in.

“Second Young Master.”

“Yes.”

“Tell me what you want.”

“Help me become the successor. Help me without the condition that I sever ties with Elder Blade Demon. That is all I want.”

“Second Young Master, in this world, help without conditions is something one should only expect from one’s parents.”

Having made her refusal clear, the One Flower Sword Venerable rose from her seat. She was about to leave when she asked, as if the thought had just occurred to her.

“Is it true that you gave the Blade Demon a birthday gift?”

“Yes.”

Her eyes wavered. She had asked as though it had come to mind just then, but in truth, she must have wanted to confirm this herself.

“May I ask what you gave him?”

I picked up the Poison-Repelling Bead that I had placed on the wine table.

“I gave him this.”

“You gave him something precious.”

“Because I received something precious.”

The One Flower Sword Venerable seemed to think for a moment about what she should say, but in the end, she said nothing.

“I will be having a drink later at Pungryu Tavern. If you think of it, please come.”

The One Flower Sword Venerable left without answering.

* * *

When I returned to my quarters to change clothes, there was another guest.

To my surprise, Father was waiting in the courtyard of my quarters. Following the two Demon Venerables, even Father. If I were to give today a title, it would have to be Encounters with Great Figures.

“What brings you to this humble place?”

“I came to see how you live.”

It was the first time Father had come to my quarters. The hunt had been a first, eating together had been a first, and now visiting my quarters was a first. With Father, there were many such firsts.

“Shall we go inside?”

“Why would I enter a stinking room where a man lives alone?”

“Then I will at least bring out a chair.”

“No need. I sat plenty today.”

“True. The Demon Physician once told me that sitting for too long is the worst thing for one’s health. He said you shorten your life by as much time as you spend sitting. Please do not sit in your taishi chair for too long.”

I went to Father’s side and stood shoulder to shoulder with him.

“Do you want to live long?”

“Of course. There are still so many things I have yet to try. I want to try them all before I die.”

As I said that, I looked back at Father.

“What about you, Father? Is there nothing you wish to try besides being the Heavenly Demon?”

“No.”

His answer was so firm that, to me, it sounded instead like, “There are too many to answer.”

“Someday, I would like to go out into the Central Plains with you, Father.”

Father glanced at me.

“Well, I am not suggesting we go to strike the Murim Alliance.”

Perhaps that joke, at least, amused him, for Father let out a faint chuckle.

“I mean we should simply look around here and there, at places where people live. What do you think? Later, will you go see the world with me? They say the Snowy Mountains are wonderfully cool if you go there in summer.”

Father gave no answer.

For a while, we watched the sunset in the distance.

“On my way here, I met the One Flower Sword Venerable. She said that if I cut ties with the Blade Demon, she would become my backer.”

I told Father exactly what had happened.

“Whom do you want to join hands with?”

“The Sword Venerable seems like she would be easier to deal with. But the Blade Demon has his cute side.”

“That is how you let your guard down and die.”

“He certainly has a knack for making people lower their guard, that old man.”

Father would know the Blade Demon better than I did.

“Why did you give the Blade Demon the Beyond-Heaven Divine Pill?”

“I once entrusted him with a certain task.”

Father did not tell me what that task had been. It was surely something that had happened when both Father and the Blade Demon were young.

“What do you intend to do?”

“I want them both. A blade on my left and a sword on my right, masters on both sides.”

Father turned his head and looked at me.

“This is not needless greed. My instincts are telling me this: if I bring those two sworn enemies along together, the results will be far better. It will be difficult at first, but later, it will actually be easier. It keeps whispering that to me.”

“Where does that confidence come from?”

“Could it not be the confidence you passed down to me in my blood, Father?”

By then, the sunset had reached its peak, reddening both Father’s face and mine.

Father suddenly said, “The Sword Venerable is someone with many wounds.”

I looked back at Father, but after saying only that, he said nothing more. Was he telling me to use that point, or was he telling me to be considerate because of it?

“When I first met the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, he told me that he had lived his whole life with misfortune, so it was best not to see him often. Yet these days, I see him almost as often as I see you, Father.”

I made my will to bring the two of them along clear once more.

“I have already clashed well enough with misfortune, so I will try clashing with wounds too.”

* * *

That evening, I drank at Pungryu Tavern.

I had told the Blood Heaven Blade Demon to come, and I had told the One Flower Sword Venerable to come as well. I did not know who would come, or whether neither of them would.

“Please try this.”

The owner, Jo Chunbae, brought over a dish and offered it to me. It seemed he had made it specially for me.

“Thank you. Sit and have a drink with me.”

“Oh my, how could I dare sit with you?”

“It seems you do not wish to drink with me.”

“How could that be?”

Jo Chunbae sat down.

I poured him wine. Jo Chunbae accepted my cup, crying, “Goodness, goodness,” over and over.

“There is no need to be so formal.”

“No, sir. I ought to be. Thanks to you, Pavilion Master, our lives have become so much easier.”

“Have they become a little easier?”

“Of course. Ever since the Yellow Springs Pavilion branch was established in this alley, the number of martial artists who drink and cause trouble has dropped sharply. And that is not all. There are far fewer people leaving tabs they have no intention of paying too.”

“That is a relief.”

“This is all thanks to you, Second Young Master.”

“From now on as well, keep an eye on things, and if anything happens that you think I ought to know, be sure to tell me.”

“I understand.”

After drinking the wine I gave him in one refreshing gulp, Jo Chunbae rose from his seat.

Just then, another person came over, bowed deeply, and left.

“Thank you, Second Young Master.”

He did not say what he was grateful for. Perhaps he too had received help from the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

When he hurried back to his place after only greeting me, someone walked toward me and said,

“You are popular.”

The person walking over with a huge blade strapped to his back was none other than the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. At his appearance, the entire room froze. The last time I had drunk with the Blade Demon, it had been early and there had been no customers, but today there were many.

For them, how could drinking in the same place as a Demon Venerable be comfortable?

I stood and said to everyone,

“The Demon Venerable is a man of unsurpassed character. So do not worry and enjoy your drinks. As long as you do not come over here and make a drunken scene.”

When I even added a joke, the tension eased.

As I sat down, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon said with a sullen face,

“Why lie?”

“It was a kind lie. They came to drink after paying their money; they should not have to drink while watching our moods, should they?”

“If you do not like seeing that, then leave.”

“If you are going to say that, please hand out money to those people first. Tell them, Take this and go eat somewhere else. And give the owner money for his losses too.”

“Putting on an act again.”

“It is not an act. This is basic courtesy.”

“Is that what you call a demonic practitioner?”

“Are demonic practitioners not people?”

“See here, Second Young Master. At times like this, I cannot tell whether you are a man of our cult or a man of the Murim Alliance.”

I did not think the foundation of my own identity was the Demonic Cult either. It could not be helped. In my previous life, I left the cult in my youth and wandered the Central Plains for the rest of my life.

During that time, I met many people. Not only in my days as a wandering warrior, but also in the process of seeking materials for the Great Art, I experienced every sort of human being. I was wounded, I felt anger, and I also learned what true human compassion was.

The values by which I lived had all been formed then, so in a sense, I could be called someone who belonged neither to the demonic path, nor the righteous path, nor the unorthodox path.

My desire to eliminate absolute evil and establish a new demonic path had also been born from that experiential background.

I raised my wine cup high in a buoyant voice.

“Now then, happy birthday. Since midnight has not yet passed, the congratulations are still valid.”

“Lower your voice.”

Though he was a man who treated everything as insignificant, it seemed a birthday celebration was burdensome even for him. Truly, since meeting him, it was the first time I had seen him this embarrassed.

Then again, who would ever have celebrated him like this? Of course, he must have received countless formal birthday gifts, but their meaning would have been different from this gathering today.

“Do not do such useless things from now on. What is so good about getting older that it deserves congratulations?”

“It is just an excuse to drink and enjoy ourselves like this.”

“You like wine more than you seem to, Second Young Master.”

“That is true. I am raising a few wine worms inside my body as well. Some raise their heads when it rains, and some raise their heads when I am depressed or suffering. Do you like wine, Elder?”

“I used to like it… but these days, I have cut down a great deal.”

It was right then.

A sharp, ringing voice came from behind us.

“So you still have not quit.”

When I turned my head, the One Flower Sword Venerable was standing a short distance away.

Chapter 43: My Sword Is Faster Than the Eye.
--------------------------------------------
Blood Heaven Blade Demon was startled by her appearance. I had not told him that the Sword Sovereign would be coming here today.

“Welcome, senior. Please, sit here.”

Ilhwa Sword Sovereign, too, seemed not to have expected Blood Heaven Blade Demon to be here, for she stood there with a stiff expression. To her, looking as if she might turn around at any moment, I spoke calmly.

“Today is Elder Blade Demon’s birthday. I was taught that even if someone is hateful, one should at least congratulate them on their birthday.”

Perhaps she could not simply leave after I had gone that far; Ilhwa Sword Sovereign took a seat.

Once she joined us, the atmosphere turned chilly.

“Why did you ask me to come?”

It seemed she had taken what I said about drinking later at Pungryu Tavern to mean that she and I would be drinking alone.

“Since today is a good day, I called you so we could celebrate together.”

“Next time, call me when you’re alone.”

As she was about to rise from her seat, Blood Heaven Blade Demon abruptly threw out a single remark.

“What exactly are you so dissatisfied with?”

It was the moment when the grievance he had endured for a very long time burst forth.

Ilhwa Sword Sovereign also flared up and snapped back.

“What did you say? What am I dissatisfied with? Are you asking because you truly don’t know?”

The sword of emotion was drawn faster than her actual sword.

“That’s right, I don’t know, so I’m asking. What in the world are you so angry about?”

“You! You, that’s what!”

“What about me? What did I do wrong! Back then…”

“Shut up! I said shut up!”

Afraid that what had happened between them might come out, she grew tense. Her anger and tension came through in full.

“You are a selfish fool.”

“What?”

Blood Heaven Blade Demon also sprang to his feet.

“Are you going to swing that ignorant saber that resembles you? Fine, go ahead. Get drunk again and run your mouth however you please!”

The Heaven-Destroying Great Saber in Blade Demon’s hand trembled.

I did not stop them.

Because I knew they would not fight anyway.

There was an ironclad rule that Demon Sovereigns never fought one another. They might argue, gossip behind each other’s backs, and suffer all manner of internal strife, but they did not engage in duels to the death.

This was something like a principle of survival that had been upheld for a very long time, and it was also the most important reason the Eight Demon Sovereigns had existed until now.

“There is still some time before midnight, so please calm yourselves for a moment.”

At that, Ilhwa Sword Sovereign raised her voice in a sharp tone.

“Second Young Master! What exactly are you doing? How little must you think of me to try such a cheap trick?”

It was the moment when the composure she had always maintained with courtesy collapsed.

“Please calm yourself.”

“I cannot calm myself.”

“I will tell you why I invited you, Sword Sovereign. To be honest, I need you, senior. That is why I hoped the two of you would reconcile here today. Though I do not know what happened to make you hate each other so much.”

At that, Ilhwa Sword Sovereign let out a long sigh and forced herself to recover her usual demeanor.

“If we could reconcile over a single drink, why would our relationship have been bad until now?”

“Then we meet again and again until it is resolved. Curse each other, pull each other’s hair out. Keep meeting and fighting until it is resolved.”

“Second Young Master, your greed is far too excessive. I clearly told you on the first day. I cannot go together with Blade Demon.”

“Then, senior, are you confident you can make me the successor on your own?”

“I am.”

“I cannot trust that. If the reason the two of you are on bad terms were public, I would have known of it as well. Yet no one in our cult knows. That must mean it is an extremely private reason. How can I stake my life on a senior who cannot cast aside private feelings for the sake of a great undertaking?”

Ilhwa Sword Sovereign’s expression twisted. She seemed about to pour out a torrent of words, but instead she glared fiercely at me and Blade Demon, then turned sharply and left.

Blood Heaven Blade Demon emptied his cup, then rose from his seat.

“I received your congratulations well, you damned brat!”

Bang!

In the end, after smashing the table again today, Blood Heaven Blade Demon also walked out just like that.

I placed the cost of the table on the neighboring table again and smiled at Jo Chunbae, who was standing in front of the kitchen.

“I should fix that habit of smashing things first, shouldn’t I?”

Jo Chunbae smiled awkwardly and tossed out a joke.

“At this rate, I think I’d make more money becoming a carpenter.”

I also laughed and left the inn.

I had expected it to turn out like this if the two of them met.

I had to keep dragging them out. The emotions they had buried had to be pulled forth so they could collide, curse, and fight. In doing so, I had to make them realize that the stale resentment that had devoured them was, when they looked back on it, nothing much after all.

I thought it was possible. The two of them had already lived far longer than most.

Their reconciliation was something that needed time, but Ilhwa Sword Sovereign did not wait even a single day.

* * *

That night, I opened my eyes while sleeping.

What woke me was the Heavenly Demon Protective Art. Sensing an energy from outside, I rose from my bed, strapped on the Black Demon Sword, and went out.

The person who had awakened me was standing in the middle of the courtyard.

It was Ilhwa Sword Sovereign.

“As expected. Your skills are no ordinary thing.”

Although she had not revealed any particular presence, it seemed she was surprised that I had woken.

“I don’t sleep very deeply.”

“Second Young Master.”

“Yes, senior.”

With her back turned, she spoke.

“Because of you, ripples are spreading across a tranquil lake.”

Did that lake refer to our cult? Or did it refer to her own heart?

“I am still young, so I long for a stormy sea more than a tranquil lake.”

“You once said this to me, did you not? That you despise those who try to win the hearts of their subordinates for free with dreams or ideals.”

“Yes.”

“There is someone I despise as well.”

“What sort of person?”

“Someone whose words are smooth, but whose skill does not follow.”

“Am I such a person?”

“Shall we check whether you are or not?”

With her back still turned, Ilhwa Sword Sovereign slowly drew her sword. It was a perfect draw. Though she stood with her back to me, I could not sense the slightest opening in her.

Even so, I spoke coldly to Ilhwa Sword Sovereign.

“There is another kind of person I dislike.”

“Who would that be?”

She asked without looking back.

“Someone who is strong but has no consideration. I despise those who use force to press others down and steer relationships to their advantage. I hate seeing people hurt others with such impoverished imagination.”

Demonic energy exploded from the Sword Sovereign’s body.

“Shut your mouth! I praised you and praised you, and now your arrogance has gone too far.”

She turned toward me. From her eyes poured demonic energy as sharp as frost.

“If I win, what will you do?”

The moment conflict arose, she came to me immediately. Seeing that, I realized that the only way to make her my person was to subdue her through sheer skill.

It was a situation in which one display of ability would be more effective than a hundred political maneuvers. Her relationship with Blade Demon was a matter for later.

For an instant, the Sword Sovereign looked dumbfounded.

“Did you just say you would win?”

“Yes.”

“If you defeat me, I will do as you wish. If you tell me to die, I will die.”

She had wagered her own life. It was the confidence of someone certain she would never lose.

“Very well.”

“If you lose, Second Young Master, what will you do?”

“I will likewise do as you wish, senior.”

“What I want is simple. Cut Blade Demon out of your life.”

Her confidence was, in a sense, only natural. After all, she took pride in being the strongest in martial arts after my father.

“Aren’t you curious about what I want?”

“Not in the slightest. It is a story I will never have to hear.”

I slowly drew the Black Demon Sword.

“Then today, you will come to know what I want.”

With our swords drawn, we silently stared at one another.

I know very well how fast a Demon Sovereign-level master is. You cannot block after seeing the attack. If the instincts honed through training fail to function properly, you die.

It is the same reason weaker foes cannot block my attacks and die. My sword is invisible to them.

Whoosh.

At a strand of wind that blew from somewhere, the Sword Sovereign’s hair stirred.

She slightly twisted the tip of the sword she had let hang down. That alone changed her aura.

This kind of tension—it had been a long time.

“Second Young Master, though I will not kill you, you should be careful. You may suffer internal injuries.”

If this duel had been a life-or-death battle against an enemy who had to be killed, I would have cut off my opponent’s breathing and struck the moment she uttered the words “be careful.” They were words born from an arrogance that looked down on the opponent, and the opportunity to strike was then.

Of course, this fight was not a life-or-death battle, so we had the leisure to converse calmly.

“Yes, I will be careful. However, since my sword is the Black Demon Sword, your sword may be damaged, senior.”

She ignored my concern, and instead went one step further.

“I will yield three moves to you, Second Young Master.”

In the past, seniors yielded three moves when facing juniors. That was something people of old did; nowadays, one could hardly find a senior who yielded to a junior. In that respect, the Sword Sovereign was a person with a sense of romance.

“I will gratefully accept your concession. Then, this junior will come.”

I was by no means overconfident, but I did hide one skill.

I would face her without using the Wind God’s Four Steps. A hidden card must always remain hidden. That was surely the same for Ilhwa Sword Sovereign as well.

My plan was this.

‘I end it within the three moves she yielded.’

I omitted the probing exchange and immediately unleashed the Flying Heaven Sword Art, which I had cultivated to Great Completion.

The First Form, Equal Heaven Form, unfolded.

Shiiiiing!

A streak of sword light seemed about to bisect her horizontally.

Chaaaaang!

In front of her chest, sword clashed against sword. Her surprise traveled through the blade. The internal energy contained in my attack must have been greater than she had imagined, and the sense of crisis she would feel upon meeting a true archenemy was surely making every hair on her body stand on end.

Immediately, the second attack unfolded.

The Second Form, Transforming Heaven Form, scattered splendid sword light. Before her eyes, the sword changed twelve times, and the Sword Sovereign retreated while deflecting each attack one by one.

But on the ninth transformation, she broke her promise.

Shiiing!

Clang.

Her sword flew toward my chest, and I twisted my body, striking away the attack with the Black Demon Sword.

Ilhwa Sword Sovereign broke her promise to yield three moves and launched an attack. From her standpoint, she had no choice. She could not block the continuing transformations of the Transforming Heaven Form with defense alone, and in the end, she had used offense in place of defense.

“Oh.”

Just as she, flustered, was about to offer some excuse.

I was already launching my third attack. This was the opportunity. Her composure was greatly shaken, and she was even about to speak.

The Third Form, Profound Heaven Form, flew in.

Swaaaaaaak!

Kaaang!

Together with the sound of clashing swords came a single cry—words that absolutely should never have come from the Sword Sovereign’s mouth burst out.

“Not the face!”

And then, silence followed.

My sword had stopped in front of Ilhwa Sword Sovereign’s face. In truth, the sword had stopped before her cry. My sword was faster than her eyes.

Meanwhile, her Ilhwa Sword, having left her hand, was floating in the air.

Both our gazes turned toward the Ilhwa Sword. The sword was falling to the ground in an arc. Perhaps Ilhwa Sword Sovereign wished it would never fall to the ground.

Thud.

The Ilhwa Sword embedded itself in the ground. The trembling blade, because of its pure white hilt, felt as though a white flag were waving.

Ilhwa Sword Sovereign stared blankly at the sight, then slowly turned her gaze toward me. Her eyes said she could not believe this situation at all.

I sincerely hoped.

That her first words would be a string of curses.

That a single stone would come loose from the dike of loftiness she had built so firmly. That through that hole, I might glimpse her wound…

Chapter 44: With a More Relaxed Face Than the First Day.
--------------------------------------------------------
Unfortunately, my wish did not come true.

“Second Young Master, what in the world just happened?”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s voice trembled, but she maintained her composure.

Her original plan must have been this: yield the first three moves lightly, toy with me for twenty or thirty exchanges, then defeat me. Instead, she herself had lost in a mere three moves.

“Did I not tell you? It’s the Black Demon Sword, so be careful.”

I spoke as though I had won thanks to the Black Demon Sword, but she was not so weak as to cover her defeat with an excuse.

“Even if it had not been the Black Demon Sword but the Heavenly Demon Sword, this was not a fight I should have lost.”

She knew better than anyone that she had lost not because of the sword, but because of skill.

“I’ll tell you a secret. I have already achieved great mastery of the Flying Heaven Sword Art.”

For an instant, she was startled.

“You’ve already achieved great mastery? I can’t believe it.”

The level of the Flying Heaven Sword Art was comparable to the martial arts cultivated by the Demon Venerables.

Since I was saying I had already achieved great mastery of such a superior martial art, her disbelief was only natural.

“Do you not know my father? Would he place me in the position of Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion simply because I’m his son?”

“Even if the Second Young Master has achieved great mastery of the Flying Heaven Sword Art…”

“You thought my internal energy would be lacking? Did I not consume the Heavenly Beyond Divine Pill?”

“Ah!”

I did not bother mentioning that I had also obtained the Majeong Pill in Socheondong.

“I won this duel. Do you acknowledge it?”

“…”

She could not acknowledge it.

“If I had known the Second Young Master’s skill was at this level, I would not have been so careless.”

She had not known my true ability, and the fight had taken place after she yielded the first three attacks. On top of that, just as she was about to speak, she had been struck by an attack and her sword had flown away. Several ill turns had overlapped for her.

“So you refuse to acknowledge it? If it becomes known that the Sword Venerable broke her promise, the label of coward will hound you for the rest of your life, Senior.”

“Shut up! There is no need to provoke me with such unnecessary words. I keep my promises. Second Young Master, what do you want?”

“You said that if you lost, you would even give me your life.”

“This duel was—!”

She closed her mouth, as if about to make some excuse. Whether she had lost because she had been careless or because her martial arts were inferior, a loss was still a loss. She was the one who had been arrogant enough to say she would yield three moves.

“Is what you want my life?”

“Of course not. I have been called a madman by Elder Blade Demon several times, but I am not that mad.”

“Then what is it you want?”

“Please accept my apology.”

For an instant, the Sword Venerable was startled.

“I apologize for the discourtesy I have shown you until now, Sword Venerable. This is a sincere apology, so please forgive me.”

She stared at me with an expression that said she could not understand.

“Are you mocking me?”

“No. I was rude to you today, Senior. I should not have summoned you to that place.”

“You won, and yet you are apologizing to me. Why?”

“Shall I be honest?”

“Of course!”

“I want to apologize like this and win your favor, Senior. I want you to support me sincerely.”

“Sincerely? Are you speaking of sincerity in a succession struggle? If you thought such a thing would work in this arena, I am disappointed. I did not know the Second Young Master was so naive.”

“It depends on the opponent. I am the sort of person who, if someone throws dirt at me, will shove them into a mud pit, and if someone splashes water on me, will answer with filthy water. But with you, Senior, I want to compete through naivety.”

“Why? Because I look naive?”

“No. Because you possess a lofty character. Because among the Eight Demon Venerables, you are more worthy of respect than anyone.”

Considering the life she would lead afterward, everything I had just said was a lie.

But those things had not yet happened. If her fate changed with her meeting me as the turning point, would the future where she stirred up a bloody storm disappear, and a new life unfold? Could flowers bloom in the desolate wasteland I had seen in her eyes?

“Do you truly think that of me?”

“Yes. You are noble and dignified.”

She bit her lip lightly. She seemed pleased by the compliment, yet at the same time, her feelings appeared complicated.

I walked over, pulled the Ilhwa Sword from where it was embedded in the ground, and handed it to her.

“Then I will see you next time.”

After bowing politely, I went into my quarters.

Once inside, I slept as I was, so I did not know how long she stood there before leaving.

* * *

The next night, Ilhwa Sword Venerable came to see me again.

Perhaps she had not slept all night, for her eyes and face were swollen.

“I will accept the Second Young Master’s apology.”

“Thank you.”

“In return, I have one request.”

“What is it?”

“I lost yesterday’s duel. I acknowledge that. But in exchange, fight me again today.”

I refused at once.

“I don’t want to.”

“Why not?”

“Because I will never be able to win again. If the Sword Venerable had not been careless, if you had not yielded the first attack, I would never have won.”

“Second Young Master, you have achieved great mastery of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, have you not? You have sufficient power as well. You can win even if we fight again.”

“I cannot.”

“Why are you so certain?”

“It was a stroke of luck that comes only once or twice in a lifetime. I am not someone who mistakes that for skill and becomes arrogant.”

“I will not set any conditions for when I win. But if I lose, I will grant whatever the Second Young Master wants.”

She was, in every sense, trying to recover her pride.

I felt as though I understood why she had become the Cult Leader, stirred up nothing but bloodshed, and then fallen.

What was that paltry pride worth? How could she know what I would demand? Truly foolish, and naive.

“Senior, I will not tell anyone about yesterday’s match. So you need not insist on defeating me. In any case, no one will ever know about what happened yesterday.”

“One never knows with people. You might blurt it out while drunk.”

“That will not happen.”

I did not rush. Since I held the initiative, I simply had to draw her over to my side slowly.

“Please sleep well tonight. You look worn.”

I turned to go back in, but she suddenly spoke.

“It was humiliating.”

Those words stopped my steps.

I turned back to her.

“Did I not tell you? My victory was entirely luck.”

“It was not only because I lost the duel.”

The source of that humiliation lay somewhere I had not expected at all.

“The words ‘not the face’—the more I thought about them, the more humiliating they were.”

They were the words she had shouted when the sword had flown toward her.

Not the face!

“That can happen. It was your face. Even as a man, I would not have been any different.”

“An ordinary person could be that way. But the Sword Venerable cannot. I should have let my face be pierced instead.”

It seemed she had found it terribly shameful and humiliating that she had spoken those words.

Of course, I understood her for saying them. Just as much as her honor and her pride as the Sword Venerable, she had strong pride in her appearance. She was the sort of person who wanted to hear that she was beautiful and looked young more than that she fought well.

“Do you understand now why I came back?”

“Yes.”

“Then will you fight me?”

She was filled with desperation, but my answer did not meet it.

“I don’t want to.”

As I turned away, her cry rang out behind me.

“Second Young Master!”

“Can you not simply let me savor this one stroke of luck?”

Then the Sword Venerable said something unexpected.

“I made a promise. That I would never lose.”

“With whom?”

“That I would lose to no one except that person.”

That person? If the Sword Venerable was referring to someone as that person…

“Surely not?”

The person who flashed through my mind at that instant flowed from her lips.

“I made that promise with the Cult Leader.”

The moment I heard those words, what Father had said to me came to mind.

—The Sword Venerable is a person with many wounds.

I could tell that there was some deep bond between Father and the Sword Venerable.

“When did you make that promise?”

Instead of answering, the Sword Venerable revealed her respect for my father.

“The only person I respect is the Cult Leader.”

I could not believe her words.

A person who respected Father this much had stirred up such a bloody storm to seize the position of Cult Leader? Why on earth?

“I do not want to break my promise with the Cult Leader.”

As if worried I might misunderstand her in some insolent way, she quickly added,

“If you are harboring any disrespectful thought right now, I will make this clear. Do not insult my pure respect for the Cult Leader.”

“No, I was not thinking that.”

“Then that is fortunate.”

“Why do you respect my father so much?”

The Sword Venerable merely smiled faintly and did not tell me the details.

If she had brought up her matter with Father, then she had used her final move. I could no longer refuse.

“Very well. A promise made with Father must be kept.”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable was delighted. It was a smile of genuine happiness, making every smile or laugh she had shown until now seem false.

“Are you that happy?”

“I am.”

“I will do my best this time as well.”

“That is what I want.”

In the second match as well, I fought with all my strength.

If anything, I was more excited than during the first duel. Ilhwa Sword Venerable was not careless, and her eyes were filled with an uncontrollable passion.

Of course, this fight was not a battle to kill, but a duel solely to determine victory.

A dozen or so moves of probing passed, then a hundred or so exchanges to warm the body, and after pouring out brilliant techniques, more than three hundred moves went by.

The longer the duel continued, the further we drifted from the matter of victory.

We had become immersed in the pure pleasure of matching martial arts. Who would win was a matter for later.

I was astonished when techniques that seemed impossible connected into one another, and even more astonished by the sense with which she swiftly grasped my openings.

Ah! She is truly remarkable. This person truly deserves to have “Venerable” attached to her sword.

Normally, I would have had no time to think such thoughts, but as though by prior agreement, we stepped back and rested at the moments when rest was needed.

Because we were wholly absorbed in the fact that we were matching martial arts, we chewed over the many techniques we had exchanged.

This moment of purely competing in martial arts was giving me greater joy than any thrill that the crossroads of life and death had given me in the past.

Just as I had felt with Father and Ian, I had to admit that I had misunderstood parts of her as well.

Her desire for honor and her pride were not struggles to fill her barren spirit. Their source lay in the pride she had in her martial arts.

At least when it came to swordsmanship, she was more serious, more sincere, and more genuine than anyone in the world. Her attitude toward swordsmanship was enough to remind me of the way Father regarded martial arts.

The two of us, who had spent our entire lives learning only swordsmanship, thus fell deeply into a conversation carried out with swords.

The match was a draw. In a fight without the will to kill, our skills were neck and neck.

“You must not have used your killing move, so this match is my loss.”

“Is that not the same for you?”

“No. This is the full extent of my ability.”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who had been quietly gazing at me, suddenly spoke.

“Second Young Master, thank you.”

Leaving that single sentence behind, she turned away.

What was certain, however, was that she left with a more relaxed face than on the first day.

I still did not know how she would act from now on. Since she had promised Father she would not lose, she might come to see me again tomorrow. Or perhaps today’s duel would be the end forever.

The duel with her had moved my emotions. What meaning had it held for her? I did not know whether it had been enough to moisten the desolate wasteland of her heart.

In any case, my investment in her began now. Making even one person mine was this difficult.

Chapter 45: Habits Are Scary.
-----------------------------
The Ilhwa Sword Venerable came to see me again the following night.

“They say things should be done three times, don’t they? Let’s fight one last time today.”

I could not tell whether her visit was because she valued her promise with Father, or because she had enjoyed yesterday’s sparring match.

“Very well.”

I readily accepted the third bout.

“After returning from yesterday’s match with a draw, I slept all day. When I lost on the first day, I couldn’t sleep a wink, but on the day I came back after drawing with you, I slept like the dead. So well that I wondered if I had ever slept that deeply before.”

“You must not have slept well usually.”

“That’s right. I had never once fallen into a deep sleep. Do you know what I even thought?”

“What did you think?”

“If I want to sleep soundly, should I go and spar every day?”

I laughed, and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable smiled as well as she drew her sword.

After one exchange of combat ended, we stepped apart for a brief rest.

And whenever we had questions, we asked them then and there.

“During your twenty-third attack earlier, you dodged to the left. Why left? Wouldn’t the right have been faster?”

“I moved to the left in order to reduce the Second Young Master’s options. If I had dodged to the right, you would have had three choices. But by dodging left, you had only two.”

“Because of my center of gravity.”

“That’s right.”

When one reaches a level such as the Sword Venerable and I had, even after exchanging hundreds of moves, one can replay the battle just as one reviews a game of Go.

When I asked, the Sword Venerable told me exactly what she had been thinking, without hiding anything.

You can tell when you hear it. Whether a person is truly speaking sincerely to you or not. Father would scold me, asking how I could know what lay inside another person’s heart, but at the very least, the Sword Venerable answered me sincerely.

She, too, asked me questions whenever something puzzled her. I answered with my own thoughts as they were. Just as I sensed sincerity from her, she would also feel that there was no falseness in my answers. Teaching someone was no loss. It had been the same when I taught Ian; one often learns more while teaching.

“I don’t know when it was. The last time I was immersed so purely in martial arts like this. Perhaps this is the first time.”

Considering her age and position, “the first time” was not something that could be said lightly.

“How much more so for me?”

“Shall we begin again?”

“Gladly!”

This time, as if by mutual agreement, we leaped into the air and fought. This bout was, quite literally, an aerial battle.

Whenever it seemed the other would fall to the ground, we would launch an attack that allowed them to step off it and leap again, keeping them aloft. In that process, neither of us ever made a surprise attack. We helped each other remain airborne as we crossed swords.

Brilliant sword-light embroidered the night sky, and we flew like that until we had exhausted all our inner force, then descended to the training ground.

Only the sound of our breathing could be heard in the training ground. If this had been the first day of our sparring, we would have judged the result by who steadied their breath first, but neither of us cared at all.

“Do you have any wine?”

She, who did not drink, was asking for wine.

“Yes, I do.”

I went into the house and brought out some wine.

“This is good wine.”

“You know quite a bit about wine?”

“Not drinking it doesn’t mean I know nothing about it. And I did drink in the past.”

The two of us drank together. Wine shared after sparring with the Sword Venerable had a special charm to it. Though she had supposedly given up drinking long ago, she drank quite well. Well enough that I wondered if perhaps she secretly drank alone.

“Second Young Master, may I ask you something?”

“Yes, Senior.”

“Why did you suddenly change like this? You weren’t originally this kind of person, were you? It’s not as if I observed you closely, but I did have a rough idea of what sort of person you were.”

“It wasn’t sudden. I had been preparing for a long time.”

“You lived hiding your true self?”

“Something like that. If I had revealed my true colors when I was too young, my brother would have killed me. I kept myself crouched low until I could protect my own life.”

“You truly are a frightening person, Second Young Master.”

When the bottle we had passed back and forth was empty, she said,

“Let’s do it one last time.”

I was startled. The Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s tone had changed.

She, who had always used respectful speech, had naturally begun speaking down to me.

“This may sound strange, but when I am with you, Second Young Master, I feel as though I am with a friend.”

Perhaps she had sensed a fragment of my past life. In front of Father or Ian, I worked tirelessly to act more frivolous and playful so as not to drag with me the darkness, hardship, and years of the past, but I did not do that in front of her. Especially while sparring, I was more serious than anyone.

We learn from masters, and we learn from the weak as well. The realization that comes from that gap is so strong that we often neglect the learning that can be gained from equals. We belittle its value by saying it is only more of the same.

But today, I realized that even more of the same could become the finest banquet.

We talked, then fought. We fought while learning, and fought while attaining understanding. Now we set down our cups and fought. I can say it without hesitation.

Our battle was elegant. What we held was not killing intent meant to harm the opponent, but only the refinement of enjoying a spar.

And throughout all of this, I did not say a single word about the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. I even forgot the thought of making her my ally.

Thus we clashed fiercely and exchanged our final techniques, bursting apart in a brilliant explosion.

“It may be irreverent of me to say, Senior, but I feel as though we shared a friendship through the sword.”

She agreed with my sentiment.

“I do not know what kind of relationship we will have in the future, but today’s spar with you, Second Young Master, will remain in my memory forever.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable, too, regarded the value of this spar highly.

“Yes. I will never forget it for the rest of my life. Thank you for granting me such great instruction.”

“I wonder if I truly was the one who gave instruction.”

Perhaps she thought she had received more herself.

But I was certain that I had learned more. She may have learned a few lessons, but I had absorbed everything.

Through the sparring of the past three days, I had thought deeply about the Flying Heaven Swordsmanship, and the firm system of Great Completion I had achieved was shaken.

I could tell. The wall of Tenth-Star Great Completion had collapsed, and now my realm had begun moving toward Twelfth-Star Great Completion. At this new change that had come after so long, my heart began to race.

We did not exchange words of farewell.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable drew the Ilhwa Sword and extended it forward, and I answered with the Black Demon Sword.

Clang!

With the clear farewell of sword striking sword, our sparring came to an end.

* * *

Having returned to her residence, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable was drinking alone.

After her sparring with Geom Mugeuk ended, many emotions stirred within her.

Three days had passed as though she had been bewitched by something.

She remembered the first day, when she had lost the spar.

That day, every sort of thought had crossed her mind. She had even thought of putting on a mask, returning, and killing Geom Mugeuk.

The self she had been that day and the self she was today were completely different people. They were the same person, and yet at least in terms of her relationship with Geom Mugeuk, she had become someone entirely different.

Then, from outside the thatched hut, Sa Ujong’s voice was heard.

“It is Ujong.”

In his hand as he came inside was a dish that could serve as drinking food. As he set it down on the table, Sa Ujong asked,

“Is something troubling you?”

Ever since he had served her closely, he had never once seen her drink.

Yet today, she was suddenly drinking.

‘A bad sign.’

Sa Ujong judged the situation as such.

Because the Ilhwa Sword Venerable had moved in extreme secrecy, Sa Ujong knew nothing of the sparring matches over the past three days.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable gave no answer to his question.

At times like this, Sa Ujong felt a deep sense of self-reproach. She had never once shown him her true feelings without reserve. He had thought it was because she was a person with dried-up emotions, but why was she suddenly drinking?

“Does this perhaps have something to do with the Second Young Master?”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable glanced at Sa Ujong. That gaze, telling him not to speak nonsense, was cold.

“Nothing happened.”

Sa Ujong quickly lowered his head.

“I spoke out of turn. Forgive me.”

For times when she was in a bad mood, Sa Ujong had a tailored prescription.

“Ah, I brought a new one. He is waiting in the secret chamber, so…”

“Enough.”

“Pardon?”

“Do not bring any more men… from now on.”

“!”

In that instant, Sa Ujong was startled out of his wits.

It was not because she had told him to stop bringing men. It was because the Sword Venerable had spoken respectfully to him. He felt as though he had been struck on the head.

‘What is this? Why is she suddenly acting like this?’

He had taken pride in the specialness of the fact that she spoke down only to him.

He wondered if he had misheard, but he had not.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable let out a light sigh.

“It was something I should have stopped long ago. No, it was something I should never have begun in the first place.”

When Sa Ujong first brought a young man to her, the root of the trouble had been that she lost to the lust of that moment.

Just once! Yes, just once. I cannot even remember when I last slept with a man. Let me enjoy it just once!

But for lust that had opened the box, there was no such thing as just once.

Once became twice, and twice became three times.

When Sa Ujong said that the men wanted it, and that since she even paid them a great deal of money, it was in fact helping them, she had tightly shut her eyes. To be honest, later on, she did not even feel guilt.

Lust became habit.

Habit was more frightening than lust. Because even when it was not all that enjoyable, she could not stop.

But now, the time had come to stop. Once her sparring with Geom Mugeuk ended, she felt that it was time to begin something anew. Whether with Geom Mugeuk or not.

“Then I will send him back today. Whenever you simply say the word…”

“There will never be such a thing again. Do you understand?”

At her cold gaze, Sa Ujong lowered his head.

“I will keep it in mind.”

“Leave. And if you touch the young men under the pretext of keeping the secret, you will die by my hand.”

Until now, she had made certain the young men were properly taken care of. Whether they were paid properly, and whether they were allowed to leave alive. She had done so to prevent Sa Ujong from killing them in order to preserve the secret.

“That will not happen.”

After hurriedly bowing in farewell, Sa Ujong turned around.

His expression was stiff. He had won her favor by using men, and believed that this secret, hidden affair had solidified his position as the perfect second-in-command.

But what was that unfamiliar coldness? What on earth was that confidence?

He had been certain that he would naturally become the next Sword Venerable.

The Sword Venerable’s heart was wavering. No, it was turning away from him.

He knew the reason better than anyone. The one person who had shaken her recently.

‘Geom Mugeuk!’

* * *

That night, someone was waiting in Sa Ujong’s residence.

The woman’s name was Cheongseon.

One of the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s five disciples, she was a practitioner of ghost arts.
