Cheongseon ran into Sa Ujong’s arms as he entered the room and embraced him. It had now been a year since the two of them began seeing each other, and their passion was still at its height.

Held in Sa Ujong’s arms, Cheongseon spoke with a slightly complaining expression.

“Can’t you take that painting down?”

Her gaze was fixed on the painting hanging on the wall. The woman in the painting was the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“You are jealous even of a painting?”

“It is not jealousy. I know your loyalty well enough, but is there any need to hang that woman’s painting in your room?”

“One does not become the second-in-command of the Northern Heaven Sword Family for nothing.”

“I know. I know, but every time I see it, it puts me in a bad mood.”

Cheongseon led Sa Ujong to the bed.

“Do you know? Today is the first time you were the one to call for me. I was always the one who came looking for you.”

“Was it?”

“When it comes to being blunt, you are the best in our cult.”

“I have been busy.”

“Because of that old hag, the Sword Venerable.”

“Watch your tongue.”

“Sorry.”

Cheongseon burrowed into him, and Sa Ujong held her fiercely, as he always did.

But Sa Ujong’s gaze was directed at the painting on the wall. The fervent heat contained in those eyes was not loyalty.

Cheongseon did not know. At this very moment, her man was thinking of another woman. A woman far older than herself, at that.

When the moans of pleasure died down, words that would change their fate flowed from Sa Ujong’s mouth. Those very words that, in every relationship, mark the beginning of the end.

“…I have a favor to ask.”

Chapter 46: The One Who Bewitches People.
-----------------------------------------
I stood in the middle of the training ground with my eyes closed.

Through my spar with the Sword Venerable, the Flying Heaven Sword Art had crossed the wall of Tenth Stage Great Completion and entered a new realm.

Until now, whenever I thought of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, it had been tightly woven from beginning to end without the slightest deviation. Now, however, it felt as though something once taut had loosened. As doubts arose about the sword forms I had believed perfect, my certainty vanished.

I slowly drew the Black Demon Sword and began to execute the forms of the Flying Heaven Sword Art. That did not mean my existing skill had disappeared or diminished.

Brilliant sword light filled the training field. If this dazzling martial art were to reach Twelfth Stage Great Completion, what form would it take?

Most crucially, my goal was to achieve Twelfth Stage Great Completion in the Guhwa Demonic Art.

That was why I expected that the experience of attaining Twelfth Stage Great Completion in the Flying Heaven Sword Art might one day be of great help in reaching the same realm with the Guhwa Demonic Art.

When I finished a round of training, Seo Daeryong hurried over and reported.

“An incident has occurred.”

“What kind of incident?”

“I think the Pavilion Lord should see it for himself.”

The place Seo Daeryong led me to was a nearby mountain.

Enforcement martial artists had already arrived and were controlling the area.

Gok Myeong, the special investigator who had arrived at the scene first, reported with a grave expression.

“More than ten corpses that seem to have been buried not long ago have been discovered.”

The problem was that they were not ordinary.

“The hearts of all the bodies are missing.”

The moment I heard that their hearts were gone, one great art came to mind.

The Heart-Soul Great Art.

It was a great art that absorbed life force from a person’s heart, and its process was said to be truly brutal. It was, in other words, an art that ripped the heart out of a living person.

And in our cult, there was only one person who could use such a great art.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

When I had returned to save Bimahon, there had briefly been a rumor that the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable was using the Heart-Soul Great Art to gather ghostly qi.

But it had only been a rumor, and the truth had never been revealed. At the time, I had been running toward Bimahon and nothing else, so I had had no room to dig into it either.

‘Could that rumor have been true?’

The image of a living person’s heart being pulled out made my brow furrow on its own. But the reason I was truly angry lay elsewhere.

Seo Daeryong reported cautiously.

“But after examining the corpses, they appear to have been people who had not learned martial arts.”

“Civilians?”

“Yes. They’re mixed together without regard to gender…”

Seo Daeryong let out a sigh and added,

“Among the bodies… there is also a child.”

In that instant, my heart went cold. This was the thing I hated most.

A martial artist laying hands on people who had not learned martial arts—even children. This was an utterly unreasonable, one-sided violence.

When it came to martial artists fighting one another, whether by ambush or by swarming in groups, they were people who had thrown themselves into the chaos of the ruthless jianghu knowing what they were in for.

In truth, if you looked closely, I was not such a righteous person. Yet at points like this, strangely, I could not endure it.

What if these corpses truly were victims of the Heart-Soul Great Art?

When I returned to the cult to find Bimahon, I had heard rumors that the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable practiced the Heart-Soul Great Art. That meant he had been carrying out this great art for decades. There must have been thousands of victims, perhaps more.

If that was the case… The face of the old man who sent me back suddenly came to mind. Revenge was important, and so was my life, but—

‘I understand. I don’t know about anything else, but I will stop this first.’

Just then, another investigator came and reported.

“Look at this.”

What he brought was a fan. When I opened it, it was covered in an indecipherable language and grotesque drawings.

“It is a Ghost Fan used by ghost sorcerers.”

It was a fan used by the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s disciples for evil arts.

“Where was it found?”

“It was with the corpses.”

It was a moment that made it even more certain that the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable was behind this.

But at the same time, suspicion arose.

The person in charge of disposing of the bodies dropped a fan and left it behind?

“Where is the herb gatherer who discovered the bodies now?”

“We have him waiting over there.”

“Let’s go.”

But the herb gatherer had vanished somewhere.

“It seems he ran away out of fear. I’ll search for him at once.”

I had a feeling they would not find him.

Something about this incident was unnatural.

No matter that this was our cult, no one could openly carry out a great art that gouged hearts from the bodies of living people. Therefore, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable must have handled matters related to the great art with the utmost thoroughness.

And yet the corpses were discovered by an herb gatherer, with even a fan dropped at the site? Impossible. Someone had found out where the bodies were buried and arranged for them to be discovered.

“Now that evidence has been found, we will have to go to Seohwanjin and investigate the owner of this fan, won’t we?”

Seo Daeryong was thoroughly frightened.

“Are you afraid?”

“Yes. In our cult, I fear the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable the most.”

“Why?”

“If I’m caught by ghost sorcerers, my soul will be taken. Rather than be controlled like a jiangshi and die miserably, I’d rather be cleanly stabbed to death.”

“What to do? You’ll have to go to Seohwanjin with me tomorrow.”

“As of today, I resign from Hwangcheon Pavilion. Ah, I really don’t want to go.”

“Don’t worry. We’ll take enforcement martial artists with us too. Pick out a few with strong martial arts.”

Seo Daeryong shouted,

“Those people will go mad and kill me, I’m telling you!”

Someone had gone so far as to deliberately manipulate the scene to inform me that the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable was practicing the Heart-Soul Great Art. I would have to confirm whether it was true, but one person came to mind as the one behind this manipulation.

‘Saujong.’

Saujong, the right hand of the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

There was a decisive reason I guessed it was him.

It was because of his memorable end, which I had recalled even when I first saw Saujong.

Amazingly, he had been under a soul-capturing spell and tried to kill the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, only to be killed in return. How could I forget an incident in which the most loyal subordinate fell under a soul-capturing spell and tried to kill his master?

After his death, it was revealed that he had loved the Sword Venerable as a woman. And who had placed the soul-capturing spell on him was never revealed to the end. People gossiped about it as a tragedy entangled in romantic obsession, and then forgot it.

Another reason I thought it was Saujong.

By coincidence, I had recently spent three days sparring with the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. That must have provoked Saujong somehow.

‘This is a trick to make me die fighting the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, or to keep me from paying attention to the Sword Venerable while I deal with him.’

I did not know how he had discovered the body disposal site or stolen the fan, but he had properly shoved me toward the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. This incident was not something I, as the Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion, could simply overlook.

But Saujong would not know.

That by pushing the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable forward, he himself had stepped onto the stage as well.

* * *

The next day, I took Seo Daeryong and the enforcement martial artists with me and headed for Seohwanjin.

Since we had sent word in advance, a ghost sorcerer was waiting in Seohwanjin to guide us.

“My name is Lu Can. Please follow me.”

We walked after Lu Can.

After walking for quite some time, the moment we entered a flower garden, I stopped in my tracks.

“Wait. I sense ghostly qi.”

I stopped my subordinates and looked around. The ghostly qi was because of the Ghost Flowers planted in the garden.

“It’s because of the fragrance those flowers are giving off.”

Lu Can was startled. It was likely because only an expert with truly sharp senses could detect the ghostly qi of the Ghost Flowers.

“If someone who is not a ghost sorcerer is exposed to the ghostly qi of Ghost Flowers, their internal energy circulation will be hindered for a while, and they will become more susceptible to soul-capturing arts. They may suffer side effects such as seeing illusions, and if unlucky, they may fall into heart demons. I do not want my subordinates to suffer such a fate.”

When I raised the Black Demon Sword as if to release sword qi at once, Lu Can cried out,

“Please wait a moment. I can suppress the scent of the Ghost Flowers for a very short time.”

He took a small cylinder from his robe and placed it in the center of the garden. Smoke rose from the cylinder, and the ghostly qi nearby gathered into that smoke.

“Please pass through quickly now!”

I swiftly passed through with my subordinates.

“If I had not mentioned it, we would have entered while being exposed to the ghostly qi.”

“I apologize.”

At my rebuke, Lu Can lowered his head. Since I knew he too was in a position where he had to follow orders, I did not press him further.

At that moment, Seo Daeryong spoke softly from behind me.

“Thank you for looking out for us, Pavilion Lord.”

When I looked back, not only Seo Daeryong but the enforcement martial artists were also grateful. If not for me, all of them would have been affected by the ghostly qi.

“None of you let your guard down.”

“Yes!”

Ghostly qi emanated from stone statues and towers throughout the place.

As soon as I noticed it, Lu Can suppressed the ghostly qi on his own.

I could understand why Seo Daeryong feared the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. At this rate, we might die on the way to meet him.

In that manner, we were able to enter the center of Seohwanjin.

The houses built there had peculiar shapes. Most of them resembled snail shells, structures made of overlapping circles—buildings unlike anything I had ever seen anywhere in the Central Plains.

It was Seo Daeryong’s first time here, and at first he admired the exotic sight. But before long, he complained that he felt unwell. The buildings here made one dizzy just by looking at them.

I told Seo Daeryong and the enforcement martial artists to walk with their eyes closed. With their eyes shut, they walked in a line behind me, each resting a hand on the shoulder of the person in front.

Among the strange buildings was one shaped like a cone. This was where the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable resided.

His room was the very extreme of dizziness. The walls, the floor, even the ceiling—everything was covered in whirling patterns. Truly, simply being in that room was painful.

At the center of that room sat the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, Ye Su.

I had felt this in the past as well, but he truly looked ordinary. He had the sort of face that made one think that if you went to Magachon right now, you could bring back ten people who looked similar. Such ordinariness amid this dizziness?

He seemed like a person who showed, as if by symbol, that all humans who bewitch others are this ordinary.

“I greet the Demon Venerable.”

I greeted him respectfully.

Before my regression, I had had no particular connection with him. Even when I returned to the cult in search of materials for the great art, there had been no meaningful point of contact between us. Only one thing was certain. Among the Eight Demon Venerables, he had been the one who survived the longest.

“Welcome, Second Young Master.”

His voice scattered from every direction. It seemed to come from the left, and then from the right.

“What brings you all the way to the edge of the world?”

This time, it sounded as if he were whispering from behind my back.

Since he was confusing the mind like this, in a sense, one could say the soul-capturing art had already begun.

“I have come to investigate an incident.”

At that, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable stared piercingly at me.

Suddenly, the black of his pupils expanded. As I looked into his eyes, now entirely dark, I felt my mind grow hazy. The subordinates who had come with me were probably not in their right minds by now.

“Not bad.”

As though it were a thought in my own mind, his voice rang like an echo inside a cave.

“I am often told that I have a firm will.”

Of course that was part of it, but the decisive reason was the Heavenly Demon Protection Art. The Heavenly Demon Protection Art activated on its own and blocked the ghostly qi from invading my mind and heart. Of course, since my attainment in it was not yet deep, the situation would change if the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable exerted his full power.

Before it could turn into an unnecessary contest of wills, I quickly revealed the purpose of my visit today.

“More than ten unusual corpses were discovered, and an item belonging to a ghost sorcerer was found there.”

“What kind of item?”

I showed him the fan.

It seemed each Ghost Fan had its own owner. As soon as he saw the Ghost Fan, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable summoned someone.

“Call Ling Xie.”

A question that would normally have been asked was omitted. Even though I had deliberately emphasized that the corpses were unusual, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable did not ask what was unusual about them. Which meant—

‘He already knows what kind of corpses they are.’

Chapter 47: You Fill the Gap.
-----------------------------
A short while later, Neunghyu entered.

“This subordinate has come at the Demon Venerable’s summons.”

Our gazes turned to his waist. Sure enough, the Ghost Fan carried by practitioners of ghost arts was not there.

“Where is your Ghost Fan?”

“I lost it yesterday.”

At that, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable threw him the Ghost Fan he had been holding.

“It’s mine. Where did you get this?”

He did not dare ask the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, and instead directed the question to me.

“Several corpses were discovered yesterday, and this was found with them.”

When Neunghyu heard my answer, he flinched in surprise. It was a different sort of shock from the ordinary surprise one would feel upon learning that one’s belongings had been found at the scene of an incident.

And Neunghyu, too, did not ask where the bodies had been found, nor what the cause of death was.

‘Ah, so Neunghyu is the one in charge of disposing of the bodies!’

It was the moment I confirmed that the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable had been carrying out the Heart-Soul Great Art.

“Given the circumstances, you will have to come with me and be investigated.”

Neunghyu was not afraid of me. He looked at the only person he feared and awaited his decision.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable merely stared at him in silence, but I could tell he was sending a sound transmission in some peculiar manner.

Soon after, Neunghyu said to me,

“Understood. Let us go together.”

He was arrested obediently, without protesting his innocence or resisting.

I gave the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable a courteous bow.

“Thank you for your cooperation. I will see you again.”

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable nodded without a word. His expression was utterly calm, so there was no telling what he was thinking inside.

* * *

As soon as I returned, I ordered an investigation into Neunghyu.

The special investigators took turns questioning him, but he only said that he had lost his fan and refused to say anything else.

“It won’t be easy to make him open his mouth.”

At Seo Daeryong’s words, I nodded. I could tell just by looking into his eyes. His loyalty to and fear of the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable were so strong that neither persuasion nor interrogation would work on him.

“We have no proof that he killed them, so we won’t be able to hold him for long.”

“In any case, he’s nothing more than a lackey. If we’re going to catch someone, it has to be the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.”

Perhaps reading the force in my eyes, Seo Daeryong said worriedly,

“Please be careful. Your opponent is the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.”

“Do you remember what I told you when I first took office? That even if my father did wrong, I would go to arrest him.”

“Yes.”

“Then he is the one who should be careful. Go and tell him. Tell him to be careful.”

“I only wish I had that kind of courage.”

That day, an unexpected person came to see me in my office.

Seo Daeryong entered with a startled face, even stammering as he spoke.

“The C-Cult Leader has come to visit.”

I was shocked as well. I truly had never imagined Father would come here.

“Show him in at once.”

Seo Daeryong left, and soon Father entered the office.

“Father? What brings you all the way here?”

“I was passing by, so I stopped in.”

As if. Father had come because he had something to say to me.

“There is tea, and there is wine.”

“What wine in broad daylight? Let us have some tea.”

I brewed the tea myself and brought it out. In the meantime, Father stood by the window behind my seat, looking outside.

“Please have some tea.”

Still gazing out the window, Father said,

“You must not touch the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable yet.”

I knew then. He had come to say this.

He knew that I had gone to Seohwanjin and arrested Neunghyu. And he knew that, now that the bodies had been found, I had no intention of leaving the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable alone.

“Why not?”

“Because with your martial arts, you cannot kill him.”

“I can cut him down when his guard is down. If that doesn’t work, I’ll stab him in the back and kill him.”

“Standing behind his back will be harder than killing him.”

“Is the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable really that strong?”

“He is both the weakest and the strongest.”

I understood what those words meant.

If one could overcome his soul-capturing art—that is, if one mastered martial arts impervious to his sorcery, or reached a realm of martial learning where such sorcery had no effect—then he would become the easiest opponent. Without his sorcery, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s martial arts would be nothing special.

Seen from that perspective, the weakest Demon Venerable to Father would likely be the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. The Soul-Capturing Demon’s sorcery would have no effect on Father at all.

“Will you help me overcome it?”

“It is impossible in a short time.”

“As you have seen so far, am I not a quicker learner than you expected?”

“That is nowhere near enough. There are two ways to block the Soul-Capturing Demon’s soul-capturing art. The first is to master the Nine Flames Demonic Art. The degree to which one can withstand it differs according to the aptitude of the one who has mastered it, but in your case, even reaching the fifth level would make the soul-capturing sorcery ineffective.”

“I do not wish to hear the second. Please let me block it with the first.”

Pretending not to hear my request to make me his successor, Father spoke of the second method.

“Or you must achieve great completion in the Heavenly Demon Protection Art. Both are impossible right now, which is why I am telling you to step back.”

At that moment, one solution arose in my mind. There was one method I knew because I had regressed.

‘Father, in truth, there is a third method.’

A method a hundred times easier than the first two.

The problem, in fact, lay elsewhere.

“Is the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable truly indispensable to our cult?”

Father must have sensed it—the killing intent contained in my calm question.

“Among the Eight Demon Venerables, the one the orthodox faction finds most troublesome is the Soul-Capturing Demon.”

He spoke indirectly, but he was saying that he was indispensable.

“Even though he is ripping out living people’s hearts for his Great Art? Did you perhaps know?”

Father shook his head.

“I learned of it this time as well.”

How would Father handle this?

Would Father, of all people, like a man who pulled out hearts from living bodies?

However, from the position of one who led the cult, he could not recklessly eliminate someone who would be among our strongest forces when facing the Martial Alliance. This was not a matter to leave to Father. It was a problem I had to solve.

“You told me to inform you in advance before killing a Demon Venerable, did you not?”

It was the promise Father had demanded when he permitted me to teach Ian the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

“I am telling you now. I will kill the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. Among the Eight Demon Venerables, he will be the first to die.”

In the world before my regression, the Demon Venerable who lived the longest was the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. Now he would live the exact opposite fate.

“This is different from killing someone in a fit of rage because I dislike him. It is worse than a madman roaming around killing people. He is killing people systematically and regularly while perfectly sane. If his goal were grand, I might at least understand. But is he not doing this madness merely to become a little stronger? This is not human, Father. You have your position with the Eight Demon Venerables, so please leave this to me. You placed the hilt in my hand, so allow me to swing the blade to the end.”

I did not know how much Father would sympathize with what I had said. I wanted to walk the same path as Father, but just for this matter, I decided to think of it this way.

On a long journey, there are times when, from this road to that road, one must part from one’s companions for a while and meet again later.

Father stared at me in silence for a long time, then left the room after saying something I had not expected.

“If you become skilled enough for that… then you will be the one to fill his absence, so there should be no great problem.”

“!”

It was permission to kill him. In one stroke, he had given me the advice to step forward if I had the ability, and the pressure that I must fill the vacancy afterward.

A smile formed on my lips. Since he had already left, I thanked him in my heart.

‘Thank you, Father.’

Father was Father after all. Yes, this was not a journey that would split apart over something like this. Father and I were still walking the same path.

Though it would never happen, if I were to become a father in this life… for a very brief moment, I thought I would like to give my son that same thrill Father had just given me.

Of course, I would live alone in this life as well.

* * *

“Please, please spare me. I have children. If I die, my children will…”

The man quietly looked into the eyes of the woman pleading before him.

The woman stared at the man in terror.

The gaze of this ordinary-looking man was horrifying.

But thinking of her children, she mustered her courage. She was raising them alone. If she did not care for them, her young children would not survive the winter and would die.

“Please let me go. I’ll do anything you tell me. Just please let me go. Please! Spare me.”

Tears streamed down the woman’s face.

Then the woman saw the man’s pupils turn black. Within that black shadow, she saw her children.

For an instant, she smiled brightly in joy, but soon her face began to fill with agony.

The children suffered from hunger, were beaten by other beggars, and were dragged away by a madman and tormented.

“No! Please! No!”

The man’s pupils were cruel. As if determined to draw out every last bit of her sorrow, he staged even the final moments of her children.

The children were collapsing on the frozen winter road, breathing their last. From their mouths came the words, “Mama, I’m hungry.”

“Aaah! No!”

When her grief and despair reached their peak, even her heart was stolen.

Her heart was beating in the man’s hand.

More than the sorrow of losing her own life, the sorrow that she would never see her children again became bloody tears that splattered in every direction.

Her breath ceased, and the ordinary-looking man’s internal energy increased by the slightest amount.

* * *

The next day, I took Neunghyu from the interrogation room and went to Seohwanjin.

I had decided to use the third method to kill the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

But to do so, I needed roughly two months. I had to arrive somewhere on a fixed date. Until then, I needed to buy time by any means necessary. Just two months.

This time, I brought only Seo Daeryong. He said he was too scared to go, but I dragged him along by force. I had to take him with me. The next Lord of the Yellow Springs Pavilion needed a variety of experiences.

The black pupils of the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, who greeted us, had grown grotesquely large.

“We judged Neunghyu innocent. We found no evidence that he killed them.”

“Then you need only release Neunghyu. Why have you come?”

“I have something to tell you.”

“Speak.”

“Dismiss everyone nearby.”

I had Seo Daeryong wait outside. What I was about to say was important enough that the other party might respond by killing me to silence me.

When only the two of us remained, I said to him,

“I know that you are performing the Heart-Soul Great Art, Demon Venerable.”

“What is this Heart-Soul Great Art? I have never heard of it.”

He lied without even a flicker in his gaze. Since he was a man who had risen to the position of Demon Venerable through the manipulation of people’s minds, there was not the slightest emotional disturbance.

“Stop the Great Art.”

“Second Young Master, what in the world are you talking about?”

His words rang noisily from every direction.

“If you refuse my proposal, you will die.”

As the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable laughed as if it were absurd, the words I had spoken echoed back as though mocking me.

—If you refuse, you will die. If you refuse, you will die…

The words “you will die” were heard in the voices of many people I knew.

I calmed my mind with the Heavenly Demon Protection Art and said firmly,

“It will not be I who kills you, Demon Venerable.”

—It will not be I. It will not be I…

Once again, my words were repeated in the voices of many people, mocking me.

“I will persuade Father.”

At the mention of Father, the echoes abruptly stopped.

A silence came over the room.

The one and only person he feared.

The only one who could restrain him, even for a while, was Father.

“Every morning, I will go to pay my respects and make my request. Please kill the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. The next day, I will go and say it again. One who removes the hearts of living people is only someone who lowers the dignity of our cult. The day after that, I will go and say it again. I will give up my claim as successor, so please kill him. The next day, and the day after that, I will persuade him with every word I can think of. Then one day, when you make a grave mistake before Father, perhaps he will agree.”

The moment my words ended, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s black pupils shrank until they were like dots. It was a grotesque sight, as though his pupils had been sucked away somewhere and only dots remained. At the same time, powerful ghostly energy burst from his body.

Kihihihihi.

The sound of ghosts rang out as the surroundings darkened, then brightened again.

Before I knew it, everything around me had completely changed.

Chapter 48: What's So Lonely About Living Alone.
------------------------------------------------
The objects around me began to ripple as if they were alive. The writing desk, the display cabinet, even the sword and fan set against the wall all melted and ran. It was as though they had softened and sagged under a scorching heat.

The floor, too, heaved like waves. In a place where even walking was difficult, it seemed impossible to perform any footwork technique.

The walls and ceiling were also undulating, and lines like those on a go board appeared, dizzying my vision.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s voice rang out, deep as something echoing from within a cave.

“The world you lived in was nothing but an illusion. This place you see now is the real world.”

The Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art activated naturally. If I had not learned it, he would already have begun stealing my mind.

That did not mean I was in a situation where I could escape the crisis by attacking blindly.

Because the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable standing over there was surely an illusion.

He had not yet unleashed his most powerful sorcery, so I could still keep the situation under control. Regulating the ghostly energy, I spoke calmly.

“It’s a world where one can save on drinking money. I feel this dizzy, as if drunk, without even drinking.”

Seeing me calmly make a joke when I should have already collapsed, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable looked surprised.

The next moment, his whisper reached my ears. It was an overlapping voice, like several people murmuring and spreading rumors.

“So the rumors were true. They said the Second Young Master had become a hidden dragon and begun to ascend.”

Before I knew it, he was beside me.

I could not tell whether the one standing next to me was an illusion or the real thing.

I would have to cut him to know, but first came the doubt of whether I could truly cut him at all. If such thoughts arose before I had even drawn my sword, then if a fight broke out with him, it would be a truly difficult battle.

I understood clearly why Father had come to warn me.

Even with the Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art activated, my mind was this unsettled; if he attacked on top of that, how in the world was I supposed to defeat the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable?

I turned my head toward the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable standing beside me. His face was so ordinary that it seemed extraordinary instead.

“I have no intention of ascending. I want to live thin and long, enjoying this world for a very long time. That is why I seek to grow stronger.”

More precisely, it was the life the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable pursued. He had survived, thin and long, until the very end. Before he died, he left many words behind, and the following was something he had often said as well.

—The reason I strove my whole life to become stronger was so that no one could kill me.

He was a man who truly cherished his own life. The Mind-Soul Great Art was also an expression of that self-love.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable grinned at me.

“A splendid thought.”

At the same time, the rippling around us stopped. The surroundings darkened, then brightened again, and before I knew it, everything had changed.

The space that had seemed enough to drive one mad disappeared, and this time a vast plain spread out before me. It was a sight that lifted the heart just by looking at it.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable stood far away in the middle of the field.

I shouted toward him.

“It truly is a splendid martial art.”

Ordinarily, if I wanted to twist his mood, I would have called it sorcery or trickery instead of martial art, but I deliberately did not do so now. In his world, yielding him a move was the wise choice.

“It is a martial art that only I in this world can perform.”

He spoke proudly. But that was not true.

The leader of the Wind Heaven Cult could also perform the exact same martial art. I had learned that fact in the past while rescuing the Yin Thunder Sect. The roots of the martial arts the two had learned lay in the demonic arts of the Blood Cult.

“What do you think? Do you not wish to live in a world like this?”

He was standing far away, yet his voice sounded right beside me.

“If you teach me, I will gratefully learn.”

Then the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable appeared in front of me in an instant.

“You are more insidious than I expected. Trying to obtain a divine art of the world for free.”

“In return, would you not be able to obtain me?”

He looked into my eyes. His pitch-black pupils shrank until they were as small as dots.

“I can see clearly what you are thinking. You may deceive an idiot like the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, but you cannot deceive me.”

“What am I thinking?”

“You want to grab me by the hair and slap me across the face. You are saying this as you strike my cheek. You bastard, who the hell do you think you are to disobey me?”

“Close, but wrong. You bastard, who the hell do you think you are to kill people? Let’s start by pulling out that shameless heart of yours! How is it? A little different, right?”

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable did not get angry. Because he was confident he could kill me, he displayed the composure of the strong.

“You are the Cult Leader’s son, so it must be hard for you to die, is it not? That is why you want to die by my hand.”

“Of course not. Isn’t it better to be a living third-rate nobody than the dead greatest under heaven?”

This, too, was something the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable had often said.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s expression seemed to change subtly.

Then the pupils that had shrunk to dots returned to normal, and he withdrew all of his ghostly energy as well.

The surroundings returned to normal. Once again, we were facing each other in the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s room.

“What I showed you just now was merely a taste. If I wished, I could open a place like hell and trap you there for the rest of your life. Even so, do you not fear me?”

“I do fear you. I am afraid. That is why I said I would persuade Father, did I not?”

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable stared at me blankly for a moment, then burst into loud laughter.

“Hahahahaha.”

Even his laughter was not ordinary. It was a bizarre laugh that rummaged through the listener’s heart. My insides churned, and nausea rose within me.

“At this point, I must acknowledge it. No wonder the Blade Demon was making such a fuss trying to attach himself to you.”

As expected, he had been watching my relationship with the Blade Demon. Well, it would not only be this Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. The other Demon Venerables were surely the same.

“Listen, Second Young Master. You should return now.”

“Even if only out of regard for Father, please restrain yourself for the time being! Then I shall take my leave.”

Since it was a threat made using Father, it would work for two or three months.

“If you see me again, you will never return to this world.”

I walked out without replying. Our conversation in words was now over.

* * *

Seo Daeryong, who had been waiting outside, asked me,

“Was the matter you came for resolved well?”

“If you say it went well, it went well; if you say it did not, then it did not.”

“I think the fact that you came to find that terrifying Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable like this and tried to seek a solution is already something well done, Pavilion Master. Whatever it may be… no one else had done it. I think there is meaning in that.”

To think I would be comforted by this dark and crooked fellow.

“You were very angry, weren’t you?”

“Do I look that way?”

“Yes.”

“When I first saw the corpses, I was very angry, but I am fine now. I have already made up my mind.”

Seo Daeryong looked at me for a moment, then started walking again.

“You are not going to ask what decision I made?”

“I have already heard it. It is the look in your eyes that you have always shown until now. The look you had before accomplishing the impossible.”

I smiled awkwardly.

“This is why one must have a right arm.”

“You keep calling me your right arm, but isn’t Martial Artist Ian your right arm, Pavilion Master?”

“Ian? Ian is not my right arm.”

“Then am I your right arm, ahead of Martial Artist Ian?”

There was a slight trace of expectation in him.

“You are my right arm, but you cannot surpass Ian.”

“What do you mean?”

“Ian is my heart. My heart.”

“Now I feel disappointed even though I am the right arm.”

I laughed loudly, and Seo Daeryong laughed along with me.

“I will buy you a drink today.”

“Really? Are we finally going to a top-class pleasure house?”

“The Pungryu Tavern.”

I stuck out my lower lip, and Seo Daeryong cried out about his meager salary.

“Fine, then. Since our miserly Inspector Seo is buying drinks for once, let us call my left arm, my heart, and even my left wing to drink together.”

“The left arm must be the Demon Army Lord, and the heart is Martial Artist Ian. Then who is the left wing?”

“There is someone. Come on, let’s go.”

I strode ahead. After walking some distance ahead, I looked back at Seo Daeryong.

“Aren’t you coming?”

Standing where he was, Seo Daeryong spoke with an apologetic face.

“I am sorry. This is all I can do for you. If my heart had its way…”

“If your heart had its way?”

He looked toward the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable’s residence. His eyes looked as if he wanted to go in, grab the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable by the hair, and drag him out. I could feel the heat in him, as though he would have done so if only he were strong.

Our gazes met in the air.

These days, I had come to rely quite a bit on this small, gloomy man.

“I will accept the thought.”

“You should!”

Leaving Seohwanjin, we headed together for the Pungryu Tavern.

“It looks like it is going to rain.”

Indeed, dark clouds had gathered thickly, and it seemed as though rain would pour down at any moment.

“You picked a fine day. With rain coming too, the drinking bill is going to be no joke today, isn’t it?”

“If it is at the Pungryu Tavern, it is fine. There are no women, so there is nowhere to spend money.”

“Ah… that is too sad a thing to say. This won’t do. I should buy the drinks.”

“Second Young Master, you do not have a woman either, do you?”

“I have Ian, don’t I?”

As if he had not expected Ian to come up, Seo Daeryong blinked.

“She is your guard, is she not? If you put it that way, I also…”

“You have that junior from back then?”

Seo Daeryong’s face reddened slightly.

“I told you, it is not that junior!”

We took the largest table on the second floor of the Pungryu Tavern.

“Welcome, Pavilion Master.”

These days, I was the best customer for the owner, Jo Chunbae. He said his sales had doubled thanks to the Hwangcheon Pavilion branch. Not only that, but the tyranny of martial artists had greatly lessened, making life worth living, he said.

“Do not worry today. No tables will be broken.”

“Haha, what if another one breaks? As long as no one gets hurt, that is enough.”

“Look forward to it. Today is the day this friend here opens his hidden purse.”

“I will prepare something delicious and send it up.”

Before Jo Chunbae brought out the proper dishes, he brought the alcohol first.

For a while, we said nothing and quietly drank. Seo Daeryong was lost in his thoughts, and I in mine.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable came to mind.

Whenever I thought of him, along with the desire to kill him, fighting spirit surged fiercely within me.

If it had been the old me, on a day like today, I would not have been sitting here drinking. I would have run to the training grounds and swung my sword without rest.

In my youth, when I suffered setbacks, I exhausted all my heart and mind in the effort to overcome that hardship. Back then, I did not know that when a person is desperate, they can also flee into effort.

But I no longer do that.

The more anger wells up, the more I strive to become calm. Because now I know that drinking with my people strengthens me just as much as training does.

Then Seo Daeryong said,

“But today, for some reason, I feel a little strange. My heart keeps pounding.”

“Did you see a pretty woman? Where?”

When I glanced around playfully, Seo Daeryong laughed.

“You do not even like women, do you?”

“Who says I dislike women?”

“Is that not so? I thought you had no interest in women, Pavilion Master.”

Women, huh. In my past life, I lived alone all my life, so I was lonely, and because of that, I was also not lonely.

“Are you lonely these days?”

“What is there to be lonely about when I live alone? I am just bored. Isn’t loneliness an emotion you feel when you are with someone?”

“Oh! I should use that line later.”

“Use it on the junior you like.”

“I told you I do not like her. And I have no reason to meet her.”

“Shall I put you in the same squad and have you work together?”

“No, thank you. A bond should come about naturally.”

“See? You do like her.”

“That is not it… Forget it. Whew, I should stop talking.”

He drank.

“Is your heart still pounding?”

“Yes.”

“Something fated must be about to happen today.”

“Surely not.”

In the meantime, Jo Chunbae brought the dishes.

At that moment, Seo Daeryong did not yet know. He did not know what kind of change today’s decision to buy drinks would bring to his life.

“Let’s drink ourselves senseless today!”

Even though he could hardly hold his liquor.

Chapter 49: If You Won’t Allow It, I’ll Cling to Your Pant Leg.
---------------------------------------------------------------
The first person to arrive at Pungryu Tavern was Demon Army Lord Jang Ho.

His huge build and the scar on his face overwhelmed everyone inside the tavern. Not a single breath could be heard from the guests as Jang Ho crossed the first floor and came up to the second, and only after he joined our table did the time that had stopped begin to flow again.

“Have you been well, Second Young Master?”

“I have. I hope I didn’t call over a busy man for nothing.”

“If you hadn’t invited me to this gathering, I would have been very disappointed.”

“Of course I had to invite you. It’s my first drinking party with all my people. Now, take a cup from me first.”

I filled Jang Ho’s cup to the brim.

“Thank you.”

Seo Daeryong and Jang Ho exchanged greetings as well. They had met before during the anonymous letter incident. With Jang Ho sitting beside Seo Daeryong, who was already on the small side, he looked three times larger.

After we had a drink, I asked Jang Ho.

“You’ve been busy, haven’t you?”

“I was busy at the beginning of my appointment, but it’s gotten somewhat better now.”

“If you ever have any problems or anything troubling you, come find me anytime.”

“Of course. I’ll come to you first.”

Jang Ho was dependable every time I saw him. Contrary to his appearance, he was emotional and delicate, and he valued relationships with people.

Once Jang Ho arrived, the pace of drinking quickened. Jang Ho drank as well as befitted his large build.

As we drank, Seo Daeryong kept sneaking glances at Jang Ho. He wasn’t watching his mood; there was envy in his eyes.

“Are you jealous?”

When I hit the mark, Seo Daeryong flinched.

“Of what?”

“Are you jealous of Lord Jang?”

Borrowing courage from the alcohol, Seo Daeryong answered honestly.

“Yes, to be honest, I am. There was a time when I wanted to become like him too. Though I failed.”

“It’s not too late yet.”

“You said that before. When I lamented that I was already too late, you said you were disappointed. But what can I do if it really is too late? I’m already thirty-two.”

“It’s still not too late.”

Seo Daeryong, who had been about to flare up, drank the cup of liquor before him. If it had been anyone else, he would have shouted at them not to speak so easily about someone else’s life.

Though he swallowed his anger with the alcohol, Seo Daeryong was flushed.

“Is it really not too late?”

I knew he was asking sincerely, so I answered sincerely as well.

“It is late.”

“Phew, I knew it.”

Seo Daeryong was disappointed. It seemed some part of him had hoped that perhaps it really wasn’t too late.

“But that’s why there’s something good about it too.”

“What is that?”

“You’ll strive more sincerely. Even if you thrust your sword just once, you’ll do it with more of your full strength than others. Even if you take a single step, it will contain deeper thought than others.”

“Even so, isn’t the reality that I won’t be able to catch up in the end?”

“You can catch up. Ah, speak of the devil, a fine example is coming over there. The incarnation of reality denial!”

Following my gaze, Seo Daeryong and Jang Ho looked down at the first floor. The large-framed Ian was drawing everyone’s eyes as she hurried up to our seats on the second floor.

“Someone who walked that path one step ahead of you, someone who practically lives in the training hall these days, and yet someone who’s still that cheerful—there she comes.”

When Ian reached the table, she apologized first.

“Ah, I’m truly sorry. I was late because I had to wash and change out of my training clothes.”

“It’s just a drinking party. What’s there to hurry for?”

“Of course I have to hurry. It’s a gathering with honored people.”

Ian greeted the two of them.

“Hello, Lord Jang. Investigator Seo.”

The two stared at her as she greeted them brightly. Thinking perhaps it was because she was late, Ian apologized again.

“I’m sorry. I’m late, aren’t I?”

Seo Daeryong had stared at her because of the word “example,” and Jang Ho for a different reason. Jang Ho spoke to her politely.

“Something about you has changed, Lady Ian.”

“Me?”

“Yes. Your feeling is different somehow.”

The eyes of a lord who led the Demon Army were certainly not ordinary.

At that, Ian glanced at me, then smiled and said,

“Falling into training hell even makes you lose weight. I do look a little different, don’t I?”

Of course, both Ian and I knew that wasn’t the reason. It was because her aura had changed after receiving the Bicheon Sword Art. For now, the Bicheon Sword Art was a secret that absolutely could not be spoken of outside.

Jang Ho must have sensed that it was not merely because of that either, but he asked no further about the change.

“The weather is gloomy too, so I was really craving a drink. Please give me some liquor!”

Ian held out her cup, and I filled it to the brim.

“You’ve been working hard training, Ian.”

“Working hard? It’s something I ought to do. And I know you train far more than I do, Young Master. You even cut down on sleep to do it.”

At that, Seo Daeryong was startled.

“Really?”

“You didn’t know?”

At Ian’s question, Seo Daeryong looked at me.

“You’re someone who looks like he’d slack off no matter who looked at you.”

“That’s true. But inside that lazy-looking shell is one incredibly hardworking martial artist.”

Seo Daeryong looked at me, then at Jang Ho, then back at Ian. Then he drank alone.

“It seems I’m the only loser here.”

“But you work hard at your job, don’t you?”

At Ian’s consolation, Seo Daeryong lowered his head deeply.

“I put off today’s work until tomorrow and came to drink.”

“Ah, dear. Our Investigator Seo’s shell looks terribly diligent, though.”

Ian teased him lightly. At times like this, Ian was unmistakably a young girl who suited bright laughter.

“Now, let’s drink for today’s loser!”

Ian raised her cup, and we toasted. The loser, too, joined in the final toast, saying he was the very person in question, without taking offense.

“What do you think? Everyone’s working this hard. Do you want to properly learn martial arts too?”

“What? From you, Pavilion Lord?”

“No.”

“Then from whom?”

Without realizing it, Seo Daeryong looked at Jang Ho.

“Why are you looking there? He’s the busiest person among us.”

“Ah, it’s just that he trained the enforcement warriors before, so I wondered.”

“If there’s someone to teach you, will you learn? You won’t be able to become a formal disciple, but they should be able to teach you proper martial arts.”

“Who is it?”

Instead of telling him who it was, I said to him,

“Your resolve comes first. If you make up your mind to be reborn, tell me. Then I’ll tell you who it is.”

I was thinking of him as the next Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion. But he was too young to become the next pavilion lord. Nor did he have a fine sect behind him or political connections. To become the pavilion lord, he needed something.

Then suddenly, one person came to mind.

Someone who did not suit him at all, and perhaps because of that, might suit him even better.

“I’ll give you fifteen minutes. Think it over.”

“What? I have to decide right here?”

“With matters like this, if you think too long, you can’t decide. Once today’s drinking party ends, it’s back to square one. For you and for me. You know that, don’t you?”

At that, Seo Daeryong fell into serious contemplation, and we drank as we looked outside, where it seemed rain would pour down at any moment. We drank quietly for Seo Daeryong’s sake. I, Ian, and Jang Ho all had many thoughts these days too.

By the time we emptied one bottle of liquor, Seo Daeryong had made his decision.

“I’ll do it.”

“Really?”

“Yes.”

“This is something that will change your life.”

“I’m ready. What is life, anyway? If you think an opportunity has come, you grab it! If things go wrong and I die, please bury me somewhere with good sunlight!”

“I’ll bury you on a road where lots of pretty women pass by.”

“Excellent.”

Seo Daeryong drained the liquor before him in one refreshing gulp.

“Oh! Congratulations!”

Ian clapped energetically, and Jang Ho offered his congratulations.

“If anyone else had suggested it, it would have been unthinkable. But because you’re the one suggesting it, Pavilion Lord, strangely, my ears became thin and I thought… Let’s grab this. This is an opportunity.”

Ian smiled and agreed.

“Our Young Master’s persuasiveness is the best in all ages.”

Jang Ho also nodded as if acknowledging it.

Perhaps because Seo Daeryong felt good, he emptied another cup. He, who usually could not drink much, was overdrinking.

“But as you know, I’ve worked as an investigator for ten years. I stayed up countless nights trying to become a special investigator too. To throw all that away, my heart feels……”

“Why would you quit being an investigator?”

“What?”

“I said there’s no need to quit. You’re the person Hwangcheon Pavilion needs most, so what are you quitting for? I mean you should learn martial arts after working hours.”

Seo Daeryong took my words as a joke.

“You want me to die of exhaustion?”

“In return, you’ll fulfill the dream you couldn’t achieve.”

Seo Daeryong quietly stared at me as I answered seriously, then was shocked.

“Surely not? Are you serious?”

“Of course I’m serious. You’re learning late, so what’s the rush? Learn slowly after work.”

“Because I’m late, I have to rush. Can I become an expert by learning like that? Shouldn’t I wager everything in my life and challenge it?”

Seo Daeryong looked at Jang Ho, hoping for support as if asking whether that wasn’t right. But Jang Ho gave no response and only looked at me, as if telling him to listen to me.

Unable to secure reinforcements, Seo Daeryong returned to the battlefield.

I spoke to him calmly.

“That’s usually why everyone fails. In your mind, you feel like you could stake your entire life on it, but in reality, that doesn’t work out so well. Rather, if you have time left over from morning to night, it becomes even harder. With so much time, why am I not improving? What exactly am I doing? You drown in shame and defeat. You achieve far greater results when you continue doing your work while carving out precious bits of time to strive little by little. Because then it can become not work, but joy.”

Aside from Seo Daeryong, the person directly involved, Ian and Jang Ho nodded. Seo Daeryong looked conflicted.

“And the important thing is who you learn from. If you learn from a true expert who’s compatible with you, training time doesn’t mean all that much. From someone like that, even learning for the time it takes to drink a cup of tea will be better than a half-baked fool babbling for a lifetime.”

“A true expert? Who?”

Even Ian and Jang Ho were curious as to who I intended to make his master.

“They’ll be here soon.”

“They’re coming here?”

Not only Seo Daeryong, but Ian and Jang Ho were startled too.

“Then did you know Investigator Seo would make this decision?”

“What am I, a fortune-teller? How would I know that? I just thought of it on impulse.”

“Then that person doesn’t know about this situation?”

“Of course not.”

“Good heavens!”

Seo Daeryong drank another cup. Having already far exceeded his usual capacity, he was beginning to enter the stage where liquor called for more liquor.

“The greatest contest is whether we can get permission or not. If it doesn’t work, we’ll have to cling to his trouser legs.”

We all drank together, saying that we must secure permission no matter what.

Ian told Seo Daeryong to only touch his lips to the cup, but he said he couldn’t do that with an important matter ahead and drained it clean.

“I don’t know who it is, but I’ll definitely get permission. If he won’t permit it, his right trouser leg is mine. Pavilion Lord, please take charge of the left.”

Perhaps because he thought something he had long kept in his heart was about to come true, Seo Daeryong was greatly flushed.

“If he’s a master found by you, Young Master, then he must truly be a remarkable and good person.”

No sooner had Ian said that—

Flash! Rumble! Boom!

Lightning struck, and thunder roared.

“It looks like it’s finally going to start pouring in earnest!”

It was at that very moment.

“Ah, there he comes.”

At my words, everyone’s eyes turned toward the first floor.

A person was stepping inside.

Upon confirming who it was, Ian covered her mouth and held back a scream.

Jang Ho, too, flinched in surprise and widened his eyes.

Seo Daeryong, who had his back turned, was the last to turn his head.

I was able to see Seo Daeryong’s shock unfold in real time. His eyes widened, he let out a short scream, and as he tried to spring to his feet, he spilled his liquor.

Thud.

The person who arrived at our table set a huge blade down on the floor.

From his small, slitted eyes, a ferocious demonic energy poured forth.

“How dare you summon me here and there?”

The person who had arrived was none other than the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Swaaaaaaa!

As if it had been waiting, the rain began to pour down.

Chapter 50: When the Rain Stops, the Birds Will Fly.
----------------------------------------------------
The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon swept his gaze over the people seated there. The meaning contained in that displeased look was this:

‘You’re telling me to sit among this rabble?’

Before those words could leave his mouth, I greeted him warmly.

“Welcome, Elder. Come, please sit here.”

When everyone rose and bowed respectfully, he sat down as if he had no choice.

“Why did you call me?”

Just as the Sword Venerable had misunderstood before, it seemed he had thought I would be alone.

In truth, to the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon, who happened to be at the drinking party was a secondary issue; he was simply unfamiliar with and burdened by drinking gatherings like this. Just as when I had given him a birthday gift, he was not accustomed to such private, personal atmospheres.

“Why else would I call you to a tavern? I called you to drink together.”

I poured him a cup. Though he looked reluctant, the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon accepted the drink.

“You have to mingle with young people to become young yourself.”

“What would I do with becoming young?”

“If you can become even a day younger, that’s a good thing. What does it matter if you’re an expert, or if you hold power? Before the years, everyone must kneel.”

“You’re still green yourself. Why are you going on about the years?”

Everyone was listening to our conversation with startled expressions. They probably had not imagined that the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon and I would speak so comfortably.

“I’m saying we should live while enjoying this moment. What do people regret when they die? That they didn’t learn more martial arts? That they didn’t build up more internal energy? That they couldn’t defeat some Sword Ghost living somewhere over there? I don’t think it’s any of those.”

“That is why everyone makes this fuss before dying. So they won’t die while regretting idiotic things like that.”

“You, Elder, will be different.”

“What am I supposed to be?”

“You’re young for your age, and open-minded, aren’t you?”

After all, when it came to praise, wasn’t praise given in front of others the best kind?

“Hmph!”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon snorted and drank, but his expression did not seem entirely displeased.

Seo Daeryong’s face had been truly complicated ever since he entered. His drunken eyes seemed to be saying this.

—No, I don’t want this! This can’t happen! Right? You’re joking, aren’t you?

I felt sorry to say this to him, who was engulfed in shock and terror, but yes. I intended to make the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon Seo Daeryong’s master.

Jang Ho offered the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon a drink.

“Thank you very much for recommending me the other day. I am only now able to properly greet you.”

“It was not my will, but because of the Second Young Master here, so offer your thanks to him.”

“I’ve received plenty of thanks, so please accept his gratitude as well, Elder. In truth, it would have been impossible without you.”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon looked at Jang Ho and said,

“I recommended you because I judged it a position you could sufficiently handle, so do not concern yourself with it.”

“Thank you.”

At the first praise he had ever heard from a Demon Venerable, Jang Ho’s expression brightened.

A person’s nature may not change easily, but their attitude can change depending on the situation. The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon I first met and the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon seated here now were truly very different. Different enough that I could make this sort of proposal.

“There is something I would like to ask of you, Elder.”

“What is it?”

I looked at Seo Daeryong. No longer able to withstand the alcohol, he had his eyes closed and his head lowered.

“Please teach this friend some martial arts.”

As if the words were completely unexpected, the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon was startled.

“Who is this little rat?”

Just as I was about to introduce him, Seo Daeryong suddenly lifted his head and shouted in a slurred voice.

“I am Seo Daeryong, the right arm of the Pavilion Lord, a hard-boiled man who, though power-oriented and cold-blooded by nature, loves only one woman, a rebel who raises his hand alone when everyone else remains silent, and a pacifist! And I am not a little rat!”

After pouring all that out in one breath, he dropped his head again. We could tell. Seo Daeryong was completely drunk.

“Hahaha.”

I laughed aloud, and Ian lowered her head, holding back her laughter. Jang Ho gave a faint snort of laughter and drank.

Seeing how he remembered exactly what I had said even while dead drunk, he was a genius when it came to memory.

“What is that strangely insane bastard?”

“He’s very drunk, so please be understanding.”

“Martial arts, my foot! He should consider himself lucky I haven’t killed him for your sake.”

“He is someone we absolutely need.”

“No.”

“Elder!”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon looked at me as if glaring.

“But why me?”

“Because you are the most skilled, Elder. I want this friend to learn from the most skilled person.”

“He is not my disciple, so how could I pass on martial arts to him?”

“Then take him as your disciple.”

“What?”

“I don’t think there is anyone among your current disciples who could inherit your position. Your youngest disciple is kind, at least, but with that personality, it would be difficult for him to become your successor. What do you think of this friend here?”

“No!”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon refused flatly.

“You must have spent your whole life doing only what you wanted to do. Now live while doing some things you dislike.”

“I said no.”

“Why do you dislike it? There must be a reason.”

“I just dislike it. If I dislike it, what need is there for a reason?”

It was right then.

“I dislike it too!”

Everyone’s gaze turned toward the owner of the voice. It was Seo Daeryong, the protagonist of a drunkenness that risked his life.

“You think I would like it?”

A startled Ian tried to stop him, but he was already challenging the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon with half-lidded eyes.

“Elder Blade Demon!”

Fortunately, he did not call him “hey, Blade Demon.”

“I clearly said I dislike it. So don’t even dream of teaching me! You! Can! Not!”

He enunciated each syllable of the words “cannot.” With every word, everyone grew tense, fearing the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon might make a move.

Seo Daeryong, who had been raising his voice, now looked at me. His eyes had already gone completely unfocused. Well, if he were in his right mind, he would never act like that toward the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon. Ah, this was the final flare before death.

“Our respected Pavilion Lord! I’m going to ask you one thing!”

“It seems you are about to be murdered, so I will answer you with utmost sincerity.”

“Why are you trying to throw me to that cold and heartless man? Uhuuhuk, if you do this, I, Seo Daeryong, will feel wronged.”

A vein bulged on the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon’s forehead. I smiled at the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon with an expression asking him to be understanding.

“Why else? So you can learn martial arts.”

“So why?”

“To raise your self-esteem.”

I did not say it in front of everyone, but it was to make him the next Pavilion Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion. If he learned martial arts from the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon, he would gain the strongest backing there was.

“Ahh, it’s true that my self-esteem is a bit low.”

He dropped his head again. Iyeon, beside him, held him up.

“You’re very drunk. I’ll escort you back first.”

When she tried to help him stand, Seo Daeryong rejected her hand.

“I’m not drunk, our Heart.”

“Heart?”

“Yes, I am the arm. Lady Ian is the heart. Our Heart, more precious than an arm. An arm can be cut off, but the heart must not be hurt.”

Ian would probably think Seo Daeryong was spouting nonsense because he was drunk.

You jealous bastard. You’re smart enough to remember everything even in the middle of this.

I once again asked the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon politely.

“Please teach him for just half an hour after you finish your work. A few days is fine, and a few years is fine too. If you don’t like this friend, you can teach him for just one day. Please give him a chance first.”

“You really do believe in that drunkard.”

“I believe in you, Elder.”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon and Seo Daeryong somehow had something in common. They were slightly removed from the mainstream, yet possessed a greater passion than anyone in the mainstream.

Just as the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon was about to say no—

“I said I dislike it!”

Seo Daeryong beat him to it.

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon frowned. At that moment, I saw it. The emotion contained in the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon’s eyes.

‘Shall I show you what truly disliking something is? Shall I put you through hell once?’

Seo Daeryong, unaware of that, stood before the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon with his eyes almost closed, jutting out his lips and shaking his head. If he had stuck out his tongue as well, it would surely have been cut off.

Fortunately, before the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon’s blade could fly, Seo Daeryong slammed his head onto the table with a thud and fell asleep. It was a magnificent death in battle. It would be the bravest drunken fit of his life, both up to now and for the rest of his days.

And it was also the drunken fit that changed his life.

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon asked me,

“If I teach that damned bastard martial arts, what will you do for me?”

The prepared answer came from my mouth.

“I will resolve the old resentment you have carried for a long time.”

“!”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon would know what that meant.

A brief silence flowed between us, and then we drank again. We no longer talked about asking him to teach martial arts. We simply drank quietly while talking about this and that.

After two more bottles of liquor had been emptied, Ian passed out, and Jang Ho remained to the end, keeping the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon and me company as a drinking partner.

In the middle of drinking, I stepped outside the tavern for a moment.

As I stood there, getting some air, the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon came and stood beside me.

“What you said earlier was not a joke, was it?”

“No. I can’t let him learn your martial arts for free, Elder.”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon was silent for a while.

Did he truly wish to reconcile with the Ilhwa Sword Venerable?

As he looked across the street, the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon abruptly asked,

“Does that branch over there have any effect?”

“It’s running well.”

“No, I’m asking whether it has an effect. I mean, does it help raise your reputation?”

It seemed he probably thought I had established it to increase my popularity.

“Yes. It helps a great deal.”

I did not bother persuading him that I had established it for the residents of Maga Village. He would not understand that helping them was as important as killing the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

“Please take good care of Investigator Seo.”

“I truly am doing all sorts of absurd things. Leaving a young whelp to me—what are you planning to do?”

“I’m busy.”

“What keeps you so busy?”

“I have to spend every day thinking about how to kill things that don’t deserve to be called human, run here and there trying to obtain my right wing, and curry favor with my father.”

He realized that the right wing was the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“Then perhaps… am I the left wing?”

“Yes. If you would like to be the right, I can switch you.”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon shook his head with an expression of disbelief.

“You really are insane. To think you would bind the Sword Venerable and me as a pair of wings.”

“You said it to me at first, didn’t you? That with my madness, you felt you could stake your fate on me. That we should devour everything with madness. Have you already forgotten?”

“I did not know you were this insane.”

“Let’s go mad together. How much time do you have left to live that you would hesitate?”

“Is that something you should say to an elder?”

“I’m insane, after all.”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon smiled faintly.

“I will soon leave the cult and be away for two or three months. Please look after my people well in the meantime.”

“Understood.”

That single word, understood, asking nothing at all—those few short syllables sounded tremendously reassuring.

Through today’s drinking gathering as well, I felt that his door had opened a little more. But I still could not be certain what lay inside that door.

To open the door wide, I needed the other wing. Only when the two wings faced each other, flapping and clashing, would I finally learn why that wing moved the way it did.

“The rain will stop soon. The birds will fly too.”

The drinking gathering had ended, and I was on my way back with Ian.

“I made another mistake today.”

“What mistake? If you drank that much, of course you would fall asleep.”

“Falling asleep is also a drunken habit.”

“You wouldn’t drink like that unless I were there anyway, would you?”

“That’s true, but still.”

“Then it’s not a drunken habit. It’s rest.”

At my words, Ian smiled.

“How many times am I allowed to ask the same question?”

“It depends on the question.”

“For example… why are you so good to me?”

This question, I was willing to receive for the rest of my life.

“About five times?”

“Starting now?”

“Yes, starting now.”

“Why are you so good to me?”

“So I can work you to the bone for the rest of your life. Why are you so curious about the same answer you always hear?”

Ian smiled shyly.

“I’m going to ask again.”

“You have four times left.”

Like that, we walked together beneath the gentle moonlight. As if something had suddenly occurred to her, Ian asked,

“But wait, what about Investigator Seo? He looked very drunk earlier. Did he go ahead?”

“Before you woke up, his master took him away.”

“His master?”

Ian’s eyes widened in surprise.

“Don’t tell me! They went together?”
